- **Chat history in the prompt**: at most 10 prior tasks (the first 2 and the newest 8 when the chat has more), under `## Previous tasks and results from the chat session for reference`, followed by `# Task (work on it now)`. With `KISS_CHAT_HISTORY_DIGEST` on (the default) the newest two tasks and results are quoted in full while they fit, and the older tasks become 600/300-character task/result digests, the oldest dropped first until the prefix fits 6,000 characters; when the newest two alone exceed that, they are digested too and the prefix is then cut at 6,000 characters. A task resumed from an earlier one is seeded from that task's parent chain instead of the whole chat.
- **Bare-path prompts** (`bare_path_task.py`, 112 lines): a prompt that is only an existing path, optionally quoted or starting with `~`, becomes a directive to open it with `open` (macOS), `rundll32.exe url.dll,FileProtocolHandler` (Windows) or `xdg-open` (elsewhere). The rewrite is for a task typed into a chat, the prompt of an `in_chat` cron job included (section 20). The daemon skips it for a run that names a SEA, such as a `/xxx` command (a model-picker SEA alone does not count), and for every sub-agent task, since there the path is the task itself; a run with its own base system prompt is exempt too. The history row and tab title keep the raw path.
- **Unattended runs**: with `is_unattended` set, `ask_user_question` refuses instead of blocking.
- **Run settings** **NEW** (`run_config.py`, 201 lines, 10-05): the `task_settings` event carries the model, work dir and budget together with the SEA, channel, tool profile, timeout and which settings were inherited or pinned; the same data renders the `ran:` line that opens every `run_agent` and `run_parallel` result (section 10).
- **Transcript digests** (`task_digest.py`, 752 lines): the persisted event stream of a task is turned into numbered, clipped entries and rendered as a paged transcript (150 entries a page by default, 400 at most), an overview, one entry in full, or a 60,000-character context; the task-update agent, `/ask` and `rsi7d` read them (sections 9 and 16).

### Finish gates and guards

Every tool call passes the SEA's `tool_call_hook` and then the agent's guard. The guard lets the coding gate observe the call, then asks four checks in order and returns the first refusal: a pending user message, live `run_agent` jobs, the worktree guard, the coding gate.

- **Verdicts** **NEW** (`tool_verdict.py`, 219 lines, 10-07): a hook answers `ALLOW`, `allow_with_note(text)` (the call runs and the text is appended to its result; 11-02), `refuse(text)` or `end_run(result, success=True)`. The `"OK"` allow-spelling was dropped on 10-07.
- **Run-ending verdicts** **NEW** (10-31): `end_run` from `tool_call_hook` or `llm_response_hook` ends the run at once with the given result, shaped like a `finish` result.
- **Refused calls** **NEW** (10-24): the result of a refused call starts `REFUSED: the <tool> call did NOT run and had no effect.` The agent remembers the refused call and appends a re-issue reminder to the first allowed result of another tool; a refused `finish` owes no re-issue.
- **Redirected calls** **NEW** (11-01): a tool can raise `ToolRedirect(name, arguments, note, followup)` to have the agent make another call in its place; the redirected call and its follow-up pass the same hook and guard.
- **Coding gate** **NEW** (`coding_gate.py`, 2,349 lines; evidence gate 10-26, own module 11-01, one review plus the reading check since 11-05):
  - **When it applies**: to a run whose tool set has `Edit`, once it changes a code file: an `Edit` or `Write` of a file with a code suffix or name (`.py`, `.ts`, `.go`, `.sh`, `.toml`, `.json`, `Makefile`, `Dockerfile` and the like), a shell command that writes one (`cp a.py b.py`, `sed -i`, a redirection, read by `evidence_gate.py`, 447 lines), a sub-task or `rollback` after which the diff holds one, or the presence of a non-empty verifier script. The gate object records the calls of every run, but it is consulted at `finish`, at `commit_and_merge` and for the reading check only when the run has `Edit`, so a run without it (the `review`, `shell` and `bash` profiles, for instance) is not gated whatever it changes. Files under a `tmp` directory are scratch. Prose-only runs are not gated, nor is a run whose `model_config` sets `coding_gate: False`, as `/sync` does.
  - **Verifier script**: `verify.sh` in the run's scratch dir is optional. When it exists and is not empty it runs under `bash` with a 900 s timeout at `finish(success=True)`, and the finish is refused while it fails; without one the stage is recorded as `missing` and refuses nothing. The agent's own passing run is reused when nothing changed since. After 3 verifier refusals the script is no longer run and the result carries an "unverified" caveat.
  - **Cross-family review**: one review per run by a model of another family than the run's. The `coding_gate_reviewer` key of `config.json` names it: empty picks the strongest configured model of another family (no review when every configured model is of the run's family), `off` disables it, a model name is used as given. The review is one non-agentic call with a 240 s deadline, retried once at low effort when it comes back empty, cut by the output cap, refused or late. It sees the task, the summary, the verifier and its last output, the commands named in `finish`'s `evidence` argument with the exit code of their last run (`not run` otherwise) and the diff (a file's diff is cut at 30,000 characters, and a file shown by its content, such as an untracked one, at its first 30,000 bytes; once 120,000 characters are collected the files left are only counted), and answers `VERDICT: OK` or `VERDICT: NOT DONE` with numbered gaps. Verifier and review run concurrently. A sub-agent gets no reviewer, since the calling run's gate reads the whole diff. A read-only review the agent dispatched itself counts in the gate's place once one of its sub-tasks has finished with success and no file has changed since the dispatch; a later change cancels the credit. A failed review refuses nothing, and after 2 failures none is asked for again.
  - **Rounds**: the first refusal buys one round whatever refused it; later rounds are refused only while the verifier fails; after 3 refused rounds the work is delivered with the open findings appended as caveats. Each open finding must be answered at the next finish by a change or by quoted evidence, judged by the decisions model (without it, findings count as answered).
  - **Give-up and implicit finish**: `finish(success=False)` runs the verifier as well when there is one, and when it passes the agent is asked once to name the unmet requirement or to finish with success. A text-only reply does not end a run that changed code (vetoed once). A pause, `finish(success=False, is_continue=True)`, is not gated; `is_continue` does not exempt a `finish(success=True)`.
  - **Merge**: `commit_and_merge` of a coding run passes the same verifier and review while the worktree still exists; a merge that is not refused carries its open findings into its result and the finish summary. A merge that went through resets the round count and the open findings, so the `finish` after it is gated on rounds of its own; the verifier's last run in the worktree is carried into the checkout only when the checkout then holds exactly the worktree's files (same content fingerprint), so a finish there that finds the same script reuses it, and runs it again when another merge changed the files; the limits of one review and 3 verifier refusals per run are not reset.
  - **Reading check** (11-05): the first `Write` of `ledger.md` in the scratch dir is read once, with the task, by the reviewer model, and the sentences it reads differently are appended to the Write's result.
  - **Records**: each stage is a `gate_stage` record (`verifier`, `review`, `reading`, `round`, `delivered`) with its `stage`, `seconds` and `outcome`; the two stages that call a model, `review` and `reading`, add `model`, `cost_usd` and `tokens`, and their spend is charged to the run. The earlier refusals on summary wording and on the `evidence` field were removed after a measurement on 11-04.
- **Other finish guards**: a `finish` issued while a user message is pending is rejected; the first `finish` and the first `commit_and_merge` with `run_agent` jobs still running are rejected once each, listing the jobs, and finishing stops whatever still runs **NEW** (10-05); the first `finish` with an unmerged worktree is refused once, asking for `commit_and_merge`.
- **Worktree guard**: `Write` and `Edit` of a checkout path that git does not ignore, and `rollback` in a checkout, are refused until `create_worktree`. A run without that tool (a worker SEA), a run in a Docker container and a repository whose worktree setup failed edit in place, and a checkout path of the work dir's own worktree passes because the tools remap it into the worktree (section 12).
- **Fan-out guard** **CHANGED** (`fanout_guard.py`, 441 lines): `tasks` must be a JSON array of non-empty strings (a lenient decode repairs raw newlines and lone backslashes). A task that reads as a review and not as an implementation runs on the read-only `review` profile, and a review dispatched without a budget has the share it inherits from the caller capped at $5 plus $5 per 1,000 changed lines (10-25; an `inherit: false` review inherits no share and has no such cap). A sub-agent of the caller's own model family is refused (11-01) unless the fan-out has two or more tasks over disjoint named pieces, the caller's own task asks for sub-agents, the child's model is of another family, or the child is a review (section 10).
- **Shell guards** **CHANGED** (`shell_guards.py`, 231 lines): one module shared by host and container `Bash` and `run_commands_parallel` since 09-30; before that the guards belonged to the coding SEA's container harness. A command that would end the task itself is refused before it runs: `kill -1`, `pkill -f .`, or an `rm` of `/` or the work dir, also through `sudo`, `env`, `nohup`, `timeout` or `sh -c`. The patterns are plain regular expressions on the command text, so a quoted mention is refused too. Package installs and builds (`apt`, `pip`, `uv`, `npm`, `cargo`, `make`, `cmake` and others) get a timeout of at least 900 s.
- **Edit guards** **NEW** (`edit_guards.py`, 1,885 lines, 10-25): a file must have been shown before it is edited; the bookkeeping is keyed by the repository-relative path, so a file read in the main checkout counts as read in its worktree, and a `cat`, `sed -n`, `grep -n`, `nl`, `head` or `tail` of one file counts as a read. Shell rewrites of an existing file are refused with the `Edit` call to make instead (`sed -i` with a newline in its script, a Python here-document that reads, `.replace()`s and writes), as is a `cat` or `tee` here-document into an existing file or into a path only the shell can resolve (10-28). A rewrite that converts exactly (a here-document copy with a literal body, a `.replace` rewrite, a one-substitution `sed -i`) is applied as the `Write` or `Edit` it stands for when the run has both tools. An `Edit` refusal carries the 10 lines around the string (section 7).
- **Summary cadence guard** **NEW** (`seas/base/base_sea.py`, the root SEA layer): at every 10th step of a run that has the `summary` tool a summary comes due. Until `summary` is called, every other call except `finish` still runs, with the note `A summary is due ...` appended to its result. It was first a refusal (10-05), was restored on 10-26 and has been a note since 11-02, because each refusal cost a model step to re-issue.

### Configuration knobs

**Environment-backed `Config` fields** (`src/kiss/core/config.py`, 723 lines). `Config` has 37 fields; 36 read the environment when the process starts, and the 37th, `max_budget`, defaults to 100.0. Boolean flags are off for `0`, `false`, `no` and `off`.

| Variable | Default | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `KISS_CLAUDE_CODE_USE_SUBSCRIPTION` | on | `cc/` models bill the Claude subscription of the `claude` CLI; API keys are kept out of its environment (section 8) **NEW** |
| `KISS_CODEX_USE_SUBSCRIPTION` | on | the same for `codex/` models and the ChatGPT subscription (section 8) **NEW** |
| `KISS_READ_DEDUPE` | on | `Read` returns a one-line "unchanged" stub for a range still in the context |
| `KISS_READ_OUTLINE_LINES` | 2000 | on the host, a `Read` of a longer file from line 1 with `max_lines` of 2,000 or more (the defaults) returns a symbol outline when the file has at least 5 recognised definitions (headings in a Markdown file), the first 400 listed and the rest counted, and is an ordinary whole-file read otherwise; 0 disables |
| `KISS_TOOL_OUTPUT_COMPACTION` | on | compaction of old tool outputs and arguments |
| `KISS_COMPACTION_START_TOKENS` | 100000 | context size of the first compaction |
| `KISS_COMPACTION_STEP_TOKENS` | 100000 | context growth before the next one |
| `KISS_COMPACTION_TARGET_FRACTION` | 0.3 | share of the window past which every step considers a compaction, never less than `KISS_COMPACTION_START_TOKENS`; 0 disables the target |
| `KISS_REASONING_KEEP_TURNS` | 0 | reasoning retention asked of the provider: on the Anthropic API the number of turns whose thinking is kept; on an OpenAI model from GPT-5.6 on (Responses API adapter) any positive value keeps the current turn's reasoning only; 0 asks for nothing |
| `KISS_REASONING_TURN_STEPS` | 5 | steps per reasoning turn while the model's requests carry such a request; 0 never starts a new turn |
| `KISS_CONTEXT_EDIT_TRIGGER_TOKENS` | 250000 | context tokens past which the agent clears a Claude model's old tool calls and reasoning, at fifty cache reads a cache write (scaled to the model's prices); 0 leaves the model to the compaction thresholds |
| `KISS_CONTEXT_EDIT_KEEP_TOOL_USES` | 20 | tool calls the clearing keeps |
| `KISS_CONTEXT_EDIT_CLEAR_AT_LEAST` | 50000 | tokens of tool calls and reasoning a clearing must remove to be applied (scaled like the trigger) |
| `KISS_ROUTINE_TURN_EFFORT` | empty | reasoning effort of a routine turn; empty keeps the model's effort |
| `KISS_TOOL_OUTPUT_MAX_CHARS` | 50000 | ceiling of `max_output_chars` for one `Bash` or `memory_list` result |
| `KISS_TOOL_OUTPUT_DEFAULT_CHARS` | 8000 | default budget of a `Bash` result (head and tail are kept; on the host the whole output is also written to a file under `tmp/tool-out/`), of a whole-file `Read` on the host (the lines that fit, then a continuation note; a container `Read` is cut head and tail like a `Bash` result) and of a `memory_list` listing (cut at a page boundary, with the count of pages left out) (section 7) **NEW** |
| `KISS_BASH_DETACH_SECONDS` | 120 | a foreground host `Bash` with a longer timeout, no `until` poll and `on_timeout` left at `kill` continues as a background job after this grace period; 0 disables (section 7) **NEW** |
| `KISS_CONTEXT_LIMIT_FRACTION` | 0.7 | share of the window at which a session hands off |
| `KISS_TOOL_PROFILES` | on | reviewer sub-agents get the read-only `review` profile |
| `KISS_CHAT_HISTORY_DIGEST` | on | digest of the chat history in the prompt |
| `KISS_DISPATCH_PATH_REWRITE` | on | sub-agent task text naming the parent repository is rewritten to the active worktree path (paths under the checkout's `.venv`, the environment the worktree shares, stay) when the sub-agent inherits from the caller and runs on this machine; a channel, an `inherit: false` call and a run on another machine get the text as given (section 10) |

The other 15 fields are credentials, each read from the environment variable of its own name and empty by default: seven API keys and a workspace id (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE_ID`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `GEMINI_API_KEY`, `TOGETHER_API_KEY`, `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, `ZAI_API_KEY`, `MOONSHOT_API_KEY`), the Amazon Bedrock credentials (`AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK`, or `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID` with `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` and optional `AWS_SESSION_TOKEN`, and `AWS_REGION`, in whose place the Bedrock adapter uses `us-east-1` while it is empty) and the two CLI tokens `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` and `CODEX_ACCESS_TOKEN` **NEW**. The app stores them in `~/.kiss/api_keys.env`, never in `config.json` (section 8).

Read outside `Config`: `KISS_HOME` (state directory, default `~/.kiss`), `KISS_USE_MEMORY` (when non-empty it wins over the stored `use_memory` key for the process; a run's own `use_memory` argument wins over both, within the gates of section 13), `KISS_DISABLE_TASK_CLASSIFIER=1` (the test suite's switch, section 11) and `KISS_WORKDIR` (the directory the `sorcar` wrapper was invoked from). `KISS_DISABLE_WORKTREE_POOL` was removed on 10-09.

**`~/.kiss/config.json`** (`src/kiss/core/user_config.py`, 1,796 lines **NEW**, 10-14; before that the settings lived with the VS Code extension). The file is written atomically with mode 0600 under a lock. `DEFAULTS` has 17 keys:

| Key | Default | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `max_budget` | 100.0 | budget in USD of a task started from the app |
| `custom_endpoint`, `custom_api_key`, `custom_headers` | empty | custom model endpoint (section 8) |
| `use_web_browser` | true | browser tools (section 17) |
| `remote_password` | empty | remote-access password; empty admits loopback clients only (section 14) |
| `classify_tasks` | true | pre-run task classification (section 11) |
| `classify_with_decisions` | true | use of the decisions model, which also needs an OpenRouter key and the model's catalogue entry: the classifier asks it first, and a run whose tool profile keeps `decide` gets that tool (section 11) |
| `use_memory` | true | persistent memory (section 13) |
| `memory_dir` | empty | memory pages directory; empty means `~/.kiss/memories` |
| `compaction` | true | global compaction switch, applied to running tasks too; off overrides every run's own option **NEW** |
| `chrome_bridge` | false | start the Chrome bridge server at daemon boot (section 17) **NEW** |
| `claude_code_use_subscription` | true | settings-panel form of `KISS_CLAUDE_CODE_USE_SUBSCRIPTION` |
| `codex_use_subscription` | true | settings-panel form of `KISS_CODEX_USE_SUBSCRIPTION` |
| `work_dir` | empty | work dir setting |
| `last_model` | empty | the model last picked |
| `last_machine` | empty | the machine a new chat's tasks run on; empty means this machine (section 15) **NEW** |

`compaction` and the two subscription switches take their defaults from the environment variables of the same meaning, and `max_budget`, `compaction` and the two switches are mirrored into `Config` by `apply_config_to_env`. Keys outside `DEFAULTS` pass through: `coding_gate_reviewer` (empty), `tunnel_token`, `skill_permissions`, `mcp_permissions`, `mcp_upfront_tool_chars` (absent; **NEW** 11-12), `email` and `recent_work_dirs` (30 at most). `demo_mode`, `is_parallel` and `remote_password_hash` are retired and dropped on load. `auto_commit_mode` and `is_worktree` left the settings on 10-09, with the change that introduced the worktree tools (section 12). The voice options, which the previous inventory listed here, are not keys of this file: the web page keeps them in the browser's local storage (section 18).

## 7. Built-in tools and tool profiles

| Group | Tools | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Shell (3) | `Bash`, `bash_job`, `run_commands_parallel` | `Bash(command, description, timeout_seconds=300, max_output_chars=8000, background, machine, on_timeout, until, poll_seconds=5)` runs each call in a fresh shell in the work dir. A result keeps 8,000 characters by default (`KISS_TOOL_OUTPUT_DEFAULT_CHARS`), head and tail, and on the host names the file under `tmp/tool-out/` that holds the whole output; a secret of the key store that a tool prints is shown as its variable's name before the output is stored, sent or cut (`src/kiss/core/secret_redaction.py`: every value of at least 8 characters whatever its variable is called, the region `AWS_REGION` excepted; a value once read stays covered while the daemon runs, so a key rotated while a command still prints the old one is replaced all the same) **NEW** (11-11); `max_output_chars` raises the budget to at most 50,000 (`KISS_TOOL_OUTPUT_MAX_CHARS`) **CHANGED**. `background=true` detaches the command like `nohup`, logs to `tmp/bash_jobs/` and returns a job id; `machine=` starts that job on a registered SSH machine (section 15) and streams its output into the local log **NEW**. `on_timeout="background"` starts the command as a job and waits `timeout_seconds` for it, so a command that outlives its deadline keeps running; without it, a foreground host call given more than 120 s (`KISS_BASH_DETACH_SECONDS`, 0 disables) and no `until` poll that is still running after 120 s continues as a job **NEW**. `until=<regex>` re-runs the command every `poll_seconds` (at least 1) until its output matches or `timeout_seconds` pass, so a poll costs one call **NEW**; on the host, a run whose output is too long to hold whole (the capture then keeps its first and last 1,000,000 characters) ends the poll unsearched. A command killed at its deadline returns what it had printed **NEW**. `bash_job(job_id, action, timeout_seconds=300, tail_lines=50, max_output_chars=50000, until)` tails, waits for or kills a job; `until=<regex>` ends a wait as soon as the log matches **NEW**. On the host, an `until` poll and a `bash_job` wait also end early once every sub-agent job of the task has finished with a result unread (each such result ends one wait), naming `agent_job('', 'wait_any')` as the call that returns it (section 10) **NEW** (11-09). `run_commands_parallel(commands, max_workers=0, timeout_seconds=1800, max_output_chars=5000, description)` runs a JSON list of commands in threads (`max_workers=0` runs all at once) and reports a tally line, then each command's exit code and output; on the host, a report over 50,000 characters is written to `tmp/tool-out/` and a digest is returned in its place (each command's status and the last 1,200 characters of each failure) **NEW**. |
| Files (3) | `Read`, `Edit`, `Write` | `Read(file_path, max_lines=2000, start_line=1, force)`: a whole-file read of a file over 2,000 lines (`KISS_READ_OUTLINE_LINES`) returns an outline of its definitions or headings when it has at least five; any other whole-file read stops at the 8,000-character budget and says where to continue **NEW**; a window whose text an earlier `Read` already returned, from any file or range, comes back as a one-line note naming that step, and `force=True` re-reads it **CHANGED**; a directory returns its listing, an image, PDF, audio or video file of up to 20 MB is attached for the model, and a missing path gets the closest existing name. `Edit(file_path, old_string, new_string, replace_all, edits, exact)`: `edits` is a JSON list of `{old_string, new_string, replace_all}` objects applied in order, all or none **NEW**; when `old_string` is not in the file verbatim and `replace_all` is off, the one place that matches once runs of blanks are collapsed and trailing whitespace is dropped is replaced instead, re-indented to the file (`exact=true` forbids the retry), and a miss names the closest region with its line numbers **NEW**; the result shows the lines around each replacement **NEW**. `Write(file_path, content)` creates or overwrites a file. |
| Checkpoints (2) | `checkpoint`, `rollback` | **NEW**. `checkpoint(name)` saves every file of the work dir's git working tree that git does not ignore, tracked or not, as a git tree object written through a private index: HEAD, the branch, the index and the stash are never touched. `rollback(name)` makes those files match the checkpoint again (changed and deleted files rewritten, files created since deleted, ignored files left alone) after saving the replaced state as `before-rollback`; it changes nothing when a file no checkpoint holds is in the way, and refuses a checkpoint taken before `create_worktree` moved the work dir. Names are 1 to 40 letters, digits, `_` or `-`; `checkpoint` refuses the reserved `before-rollback`. Top-level tasks on the host only: a sub-agent edits its caller's tree in place. |
| Worktree (2) | `create_worktree`, `commit_and_merge` | **NEW** as agent-called tools; section 12. `create_worktree(path)` isolates the repository of `path` (default: the work dir) on a private branch; `commit_and_merge()` commits every worktree and squash-merges it into the branch the task started from. Not in Docker. |
| Browser (15) | `go_to_url`, `click`, `type_text`, `press_key`, `scroll`, `screenshot`, `get_page_content`, `show_browser`, `close_browser`, `evaluate_script`, `list_console_messages`, `list_network_requests`, `wait_for`, `attach_to_chrome`, `use_my_chrome` | Section 17. Patchright/Chromium; `click(element_id, action)` clicks or hovers, `type_text(element_id, text, press_enter)`, `scroll(direction, amount)`, `screenshot(file_path)`, `get_page_content(text_only)`; `show_browser(visible)` streams the page into the KISS Browser tab on every surface; sub-agents get an ephemeral profile; only in profiles that include the `browser` group (`full`, `review`, `browser` and composites) with "Use web tools" on. DevTools-grade observation **NEW**: `evaluate_script(script)` runs a function in the page (awaited, JSON result capped at 20,000 characters), `list_console_messages(clear)` and `list_network_requests(url_filter, clear)` read per-page ring buffers (200 console entries, 300 requests, with `clear=` for before/after attribution), `wait_for(text, timeout=15, disappear)` waits for visible text (hidden copies and `content-visibility:hidden` ignored, text split across inline markup found, 120 s cap). Two routes into the user's own browser **NEW**: `attach_to_chrome(cdp_url)` adopts the newest tab of a Chrome started with `--remote-debugging-port` (`close_browser` detaches without closing it; `show_browser` carries only the URL out of it, never its cookies), and `use_my_chrome(action, tab_id, url, script, uid, text, timeout=30)` drives the user's everyday default-profile Chrome (which Chrome 136+ no longer exposes over CDP) through the bundled "KISS Sorcar Bridge" MV3 extension, with ten actions: `list_tabs`, `open_tab`, `navigate`, `activate_tab`, `close_tab`, `evaluate`, `screenshot` (saved under the work dir's `tmp/`), `snapshot` of interactive elements with uids, `click` and `type_text` by uid. |
| Memory (7) | `memory_search`, `memory_pull`, `memory_read`, `memory_write`, `memory_list`, `memory_delete`, `memory_refresh` | Section 13. `memory_search(query, k=5, memory)` returns page names with scores, `memory_pull(query, k=3, memory)` returns the matching pages whole within a 24,000-character budget (a first page over the budget is cut to it with a pointer to `memory_read`, a later page that does not fit ends the result, and the pages left out are counted), `memory_write(name, content, title, summary)` creates or replaces a page and warns when its searchable text passes the 8,192 bytes the index embeds, `memory_list(memory, max_output_chars)` keeps the listing within an 8,000-character default budget **NEW**, `memory_refresh(stale_days=30, duplicate_threshold=0.9, memory)` re-indexes and reports stale and near-duplicate pages; `memory=` narrows to the general or a domain memory. Not registered in Docker runs or in a profile without a memory tool. |
| Dispatch (4) | `run_agent`, `agent_job`, `run_parallel`, `number_of_cores` | Section 10. `run_agent(task, agent, model, tool_profile, max_budget, timeout, options, wait, machine, description)` runs one sub-task as a plain sub-agent or a SEA, on this host or with `machine=` on a registered machine (section 15) **CHANGED**; `wait="false"` returns a job id at once, and `agent_job(job_id, action, timeout_seconds)` tails, waits for or kills such jobs, its `wait_any` action returning the results of every finished job not yet read **NEW**; `run_parallel(tasks, agent, model, tool_profile, max_budget, timeout, options, description)` fans a JSON list of tasks out **CHANGED**. `run_parallel` and `number_of_cores()` exist only in parallel mode. |
| MCP (2, plus server tools) | `connect_mcp_server`, `finish_mcp_server_connect`, the tools of configured MCP servers | Servers are read from `~/.kiss/mcp.json`, `<work dir>/.mcp.json` and `<work dir>/.kiss/mcp.json` (the later file wins; `stdio`, `http` or `sse`). Each server tool is offered as `<server>_<tool>` (numbered on a clash with a built-in or earlier name), cut to 64 characters ending in a digest when longer **NEW**, with the server's own input schema declared through `@tool_schema` **NEW**, after the `mcp_permissions` wildcard rules. Servers can be deferred, off by default **NEW** (11-12): `"alwaysLoad": false` in a server's entry, or an `mcp_upfront_tool_chars` budget in `config.json` under which servers load upfront from the smallest up while their definitions fit; the deferred servers' definitions leave the request and their tools are reached through `mcp_tool_search` (their names in its description, definitions on demand) and `mcp_tool_call` (runs one whose definition came back to the model in an earlier response of the same conversation; a call sent before that, or together with the search that fetches the definition, gets the definition or a note back and does not run; a search or call for a tool that is not deferred, such as `finish`, is told to call it directly). With 11 servers and 205 tools a 40,000 budget cut a run's prompt tokens by 65% and the right tool was called in 114 of 120 runs against 120 of 120 with everything upfront; on reasoning problems the answers were right as often, within chance, with the definitions in the request as without, 485 against 489 and 482 of 560 (`reports/mcp-tool-deferral.html`). Limits: 60 s to connect and 300 s per call; each connect closes the other connections left idle for over 600 s, then the least recently used beyond 8 open, sparing those mid-handshake or with a call in flight. `connect_mcp_server(name, url, transport)` and `finish_mcp_server_connect(name)` run the OAuth sign-in of a remote server (Notion, Linear, Asana, Zoom or any URL): the sign-in page opens in the Browser tab on every surface, else in the default browser, and the server's tools load in the next task. Credentials are kept per server name and URL under `~/.kiss/mcp_auth/` with mode 0600 **CHANGED**. |
| Skills (0 or 1) | `skill` | `skill(name)` loads the body of a `SKILL.md` and lists up to 50 of its resource files, with a note when the listing is cut; the names and descriptions of the available skills sit in the tool's docstring. Skills are discovered, lowest precedence first, in Claude Code plugins dropped into the git-ignored folder `src/kiss/agents/claude_skills/<plugin>/skills/<name>/` (named `<plugin>:<name>`), `~/.claude/skills`, `~/.agents/skills`, `~/.kiss/skills` and the work dir's `.claude/skills`, `.agents/skills` and `.kiss/skills`; the `skill_permissions` wildcard rules hide denied skills. The tool is registered only when a user or project skill exists. |
| Interaction (6) | `ask_user_question`, `talk`, `set_model`, `decide`, `summary`, `finish` | `ask_user_question(question)` waits for the user's answer, and returns an error at once in an unattended cron run (section 20); `talk(language, text, emotion)` speaks on every device with the task's tab open (section 18); `set_model(model_name)` switches the task's model and is refused on a daemon started with `KISS_PIN_TASK_MODEL` **NEW**; `decide(state, questions)` puts typed questions (`noul`, `choice`, `score`) to the Jev decisions model and is offered only when `classify_with_decisions` is on, `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` is set and the catalogue has the decisions entry (section 11); `summary(description)` is the ten-step recap (section 6); `finish(success, is_continue, summary_in_html, suggested_next_task, evidence)` ends the run, `evidence` listing the commands whose exit code backs a success claim **NEW** (section 6). |

A full-profile top-level run on the host, in parallel mode with web tools and memory on and the decisions model available, offers 44 tool functions: shell 3, files 3, checkpoints 2, worktree 2, browser 15, memory 7, dispatch 4, MCP sign-in 2 and interaction 6. `skill`, the MCP servers' tools and the tools a SEA's `tools()` hook adds (section 9) come on top. A sub-agent has 42 (no `checkpoint`, `rollback`), a serial run 42 (no `run_parallel`, `number_of_cores`), a run with web tools off 29, and a Docker run 33 (no checkpoints, no worktree tools, no memory).

Guards and rules that apply across the shell and file tools:

- **Shell guards** **CHANGED** (`shell_guards.py`; `Bash` and `run_commands_parallel`, on the host and in Docker): a command that kills every process (`kill -1`, `pkill -f .`) or deletes `/` or the work dir is refused; an install or build (`apt`, `pip`, `uv`, `npm`, `cargo`, `make`, `cmake` and similar) gets a timeout of at least 900 s.
- **Waiting and batching** **NEW**: a foreground `sleep` of at most 30 s runs as written. A longer one is refused when it exceeds the call's `timeout_seconds`; otherwise it is spent waiting on the task's most recently started running background job, after which the rest of the command runs; a sleep that paces a loop, or that cannot be cut out of the command text, runs as written, as does any sleep with no such job, where the result names `Bash(until=...)` and `bash_job` as the one-call waits. `run_commands_parallel` refuses any `sleep` over 30 s. A short read-only `Bash` call gets a hint to batch independent commands, at most once in 20 calls.
- **Shell rewrites of files** **NEW** (`edit_guards.py`, read with the quote-aware `shell_scan`): a `cat`/`tee` here-document copy into an existing file, a Python here-document that reads one file, applies `.replace()` with literal strings and writes it back, and a one-substitution `sed -i` are applied as the `Write` or `Edit` they stand for when the file was shown in the session (`ToolRedirect`; the rest of a compound command runs as a follow-up `Bash` call). A `sed -i` with a newline in its script, any other Python here-document that reads, substitutes and writes a file (`re.sub`, a loop, two files), and a here-document into a path only the shell can resolve are refused with the call to make instead. Scratch files under `tmp/` are exempt, and a here-document may create a new file at a literal path.
- **Read before modify**: `Edit` and `Write` refuse an existing file that was not shown in the session. Shown means a `Read`, or a `cat`, `sed -n`, `grep -n`, `nl`, `head` or `tail` whose single operand is that file **NEW**; reads are keyed by repository-relative path, so a file read in the main checkout stays read in its worktree **NEW**; a refused `Edit` returns the region around `old_string`, which then counts as read **NEW**. New files and scratch files (under a `tmp/` directory inside the work dir, or under `~/.kiss/cron/work`) are exempt.
- **Worktree rules** (section 12): inside a worktree a command that names the main checkout's path, other than under its `.venv`, is refused; `uv run <program>` is rewritten to the main checkout's environment binary with the worktree's sources on `PYTHONPATH` when the rewrite is unambiguous (that environment has the program; no `uv` option with a value such as `--with` or `--directory`), and any other `uv run`, `uv sync`, `uv add` and `uv remove` are refused **NEW**.
- **Docker and the exec bridge** (section 21): the six shell and file tools run inside the container. `Bash` defaults to a 30 s timeout there and has no `machine` and no detach after 120 s; `background`, `on_timeout` and `bash_job` work through `ContainerJobs`, which keeps each job's log, pid and exit code under `/tmp/kiss-jobs` (a sub-agent's under `kiss-jobs/` of its private folder, deleted with it), and `until` re-runs the command inside the container **NEW**; a `Bash` output over its budget is cut with no file under `tmp/tool-out/`, and a `run_commands_parallel` report is returned whole whatever its length; `Read` returns at most `max_lines` lines from `start_line` with a count of the lines left, cut head and tail like a `Bash` output at its 8,000-character default, with no outline, and answers a directory or a missing path with `Error: File not found`; the file tools have no read-before-modify guard, and an `edits` batch stops at its first failure with the earlier edits applied. A `docker_image` of the form `exec:http://127.0.0.1:<port>` runs the same tools over an exec bridge, an HTTP endpoint that executes commands in a remote sandbox **NEW**.
- **Tool schemas** **NEW**: the schema a model sees is derived from a tool function's signature and docstring; `@tool_schema(parameters=..., description=...)` (`src/kiss/core/tool_schema.py`, re-exported by `base_sea.py`) declares an object JSON schema instead, which is how MCP tools keep their arrays, enums and nested objects. `@unlisted` (same module, **NEW** 11-12) registers a tool the agent runs when called and no model request lists.

Tool profiles (`TOOL_PROFILES` and `TOOL_GROUPS` in `sorcar_agent.py`):

| Profile | Tools kept | Used by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `full` | everything above | default |
| `review` **CHANGED** | `shell` + `browser` groups, `memory_search`, `memory_pull`, `memory_read`, `memory_list`, `decide`, `summary`, `talk` (26 tools); aliases `readonly`, `read_only`, `read-only` | read-only reviewer sub-agents (section 10): named by the caller, or inferred for a sub-agent in a reviewer's sub-tree whose task asks for no change (`KISS_TOOL_PROFILES=0` turns the inference off) |
| `assistant` | `shell` + `user` + `decide` + `control` groups (9 tools) | conversational runs without file edits; one of the three profiles (`shell`, `assistant`, `review`) that `/rsi7d` proposes when a full-toolset SEA used fewer tools |
| `none` **NEW** | nothing built in (`finish` plus what the SEA's `tools()` hook returns) | `/ask`, `/install`, `/machine`, `/good_morning`, `/recommendations`, `/sync_history_and_memory` (all locked) |
| `bash` | `Bash` | `/sh` (locked), `/remember`, `/forget`, `/task_update` |
| `shell` | `Bash`, `bash_job`, `Read`, `run_commands_parallel` | `/skillopt` |
| `edit` **NEW** | `Edit`, `Write`, `create_worktree`, `commit_and_merge`, `checkpoint`, `rollback` | `/sync` runs on `shell+edit+agents` (locked) |
| `browser` **NEW** | the fifteen browser tools | |
| `memory` **NEW** | the seven `memory_*` tools | |
| `agents` **NEW** | `run_agent`, `agent_job`, `run_parallel`, `number_of_cores` | |
| `mcp` **NEW** | `connect_mcp_server`, `finish_mcp_server_connect` and, with them, the configured MCP servers' tools | |
| `skills` **NEW** | `skill` | |
| `user` **NEW** | `ask_user_question`, `talk` | |
| `decide` **NEW** | `decide` | |
| `control` **NEW** | `summary`, `set_model` | |

Profiles compose **NEW**: a profile name may join any number of the fifteen keys with `+` (`tool_profile="shell+edit+memory"`), and `resolve_tool_profile` keeps the union of their tools; `full` in a composite means everything, and an unknown key is refused with the list of keys and the closest spelling. The name is accepted everywhere a profile is named: `sorcar.run(tool_profile=...)`, the `tool_profile` argument of `run_agent` and `run_parallel`, and the `tool_profile` key of a SEA's `settings()`, where `"locked": ["tool_profile"]` makes a call that asks for another profile fail instead of widening it. Per-run switches still intersect with the profile: "Use web tools" off removes the browser, Docker removes the checkpoint, worktree and memory tools, a sub-agent has no `checkpoint` or `rollback`, serial mode removes `run_parallel` and `number_of_cores`, and a missing skill, MCP server or decisions model removes `skill`, the server tools or `decide`. `finish` is always added. A restricted run gets `RESTRICTED_PROFILE_NOTE` in its system prompt listing the tools it has, and a run whose profile holds none of the `edit` tools uses `SYSTEM_LITE.md` without being classified (section 11).

## 8. Models, routing and cost accounting

`src/kiss/core/models/` holds the catalogue (`MODEL_INFO.json`, 12,463 lines), its loader, router and price calculator (`model_info.py`, 3,122 lines) and one adapter per transport. `src/kiss/scripts/update_models.py` (3,596 lines) maintains the catalogue and regenerates [MODELS.md](MODELS.md), the per-provider list. The SEA contract behind the picker routers is section 9; the picker and Settings controls are section 16.

- **Catalogue**: 1,077 entries (706 on 09-30): 1,033 generation, 885 with function calling, 7 embedding and 30 typed-decision (`dec`) entries; 7 OpenRouter entries have every capability flag off. 623 are base models and 454 are effort aliases of one (`alias_of`). The prefix of a name selects its provider:

  | Provider | Name prefix | Credential | Entries | Base | Aliases |
  |---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
  | OpenRouter | `openrouter/` | `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` | 496 | 363 | 133 |
  | Amazon Bedrock | `bedrock/` | AWS credentials | 144 | 32 | 112 |
  | Together AI | `Qwen/`, `meta-llama/`, `deepseek-ai/`, `moonshotai/`, `zai-org/`, `openai/gpt-oss` and eight more | `TOGETHER_API_KEY` | 109 | 95 | 14 |
  | OpenAI | `gpt`, `o1`, `o3`, `o4`, `text-embedding`, `codex`, `computer-use` | `OPENAI_API_KEY` | 89 | 49 | 40 |
  | Anthropic | `claude-` | `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` | 82 | 18 | 64 |
  | Claude Code CLI | `cc/` | the `claude` executable | 79 | 18 | 61 |
  | Gemini | `gemini-` | `GEMINI_API_KEY` | 49 | 22 | 27 |
  | Codex CLI | `codex/` | the `codex` executable | 11 | 11 | 0 |
  | Moonshot | `kimi-`, `moonshot-` | `MOONSHOT_API_KEY` | 10 | 7 | 3 |
  | Z.AI | `glm-` | `ZAI_API_KEY` | 8 | 8 | 0 |

  Families: OpenAI `gpt-*` 84, `o3` 2, `text-embedding-*` 3; Anthropic Opus 37, Sonnet 19, Fable 18, Haiku 8; Together Qwen 24, Meta Llama 16, DeepSeek 14, Moonshot 14, Z.ai 10, `openai/gpt-oss` 8, DeepCogito 6, Mistral 6, NVIDIA 4, Gemma 3 and four single-entry vendors; OpenRouter 71 vendor segments (`openai` 87, `anthropic` 78, `qwen` 42, `google` 31), 40 rolling `openrouter/~vendor/*` entries and all 30 `dec` entries; Bedrock 17 `us.` and 15 `global.` Claude inference-profile ids; `cc/opus`, `cc/sonnet`, `cc/haiku` and 15 `cc/claude-*` names.
- **Entry fields and loading**: `context_length`, `input_price_per_1M` and `output_price_per_1M` are required and must be finite, non-negative numbers; the flags are `gen`, `fc`, `emb` and `dec`; optional fields are cache-read and cache-write prices, audio input and output prices, `image_output_price_per_1M`, `prompt_tiers` **NEW**, `thinking`, `alias_of`, `fallback`, `use_responses_api`, `extended_thinking` and `adaptive_thinking`. The updater stores 500,000 for any context window of 1,000,000 tokens or more (587 entries). The catalogue is read once at import. `KISS_MODEL_INFO_PATH`, when set, decides on an installed copy and in a git checkout alike: the file it names is read, or the bundled file when it names none that exists. Without it, an installed copy reads `~/.kiss/MODEL_INFO.json` (seeded by the installer) when that file exists, and every other run reads the bundled file. An unreadable or invalid override or user copy falls back to the bundled one.
- **Custom models**: `~/.kiss/MY_MODELS.json` (seeded with a documentation block and an example) overrides or adds entries. An entry with an `endpoint`, and optionally `api_key` and `headers`, is an OpenAI-compatible model served from that endpoint; a new entry whose name resolves in the catalogue copies that entry's prices and limits, otherwise it gets 128,000 tokens at $0. Settings writes the file under a file lock (`saveMyModel`, `deleteMyModel`); a name added there is selectable and priced without a daemon restart **CHANGED** (11-03) and an endpoint, key or header edit applies from the next run, but every other field of a name the daemon loaded at start (a bundled name, or a custom entry present then), prices and limits included, keeps its loaded value until the restart; a malformed entry is skipped with a warning. One more endpoint can be set in `config.json` (`custom_endpoint`, `custom_api_key`, `custom_headers`) and is listed as `custom/<last path segment>`.
- **OpenAI-compatible adapter**: OpenRouter, OpenAI, Together, Z.AI and Moonshot share `OpenAICompatibleModel` (Chat Completions) and `OpenAICompatibleModel2` (the Responses API). A tool-bearing request to `api.openai.com` goes to `/v1/responses`, because Chat Completions there rejects `tools` together with `reasoning_effort`; only the model config's `use_responses_api` forces either transport. The catalogue flag of the same name (true on 523 entries) puts every generation request of a model on the Responses API, but only at the default endpoint of the vendor the name routes to; at any other endpoint (a custom gateway, another vendor's) it is ignored and the Chat Completions adapter is used, except that an endpoint model in `MY_MODELS.json` whose name none of the OpenAI-compatible vendors routes (a `claude-` or `gemini-` name included) follows its own entry's flag. For Z.AI and Moonshot the adapter keeps the effort and learns from the vendor's reply whether tools accept it. OpenRouter Claude routes, the rolling `~anthropic` aliases included (10-18), get a top-level `cache_control` so prompt caching applies. A harbor-style `openai/`, `anthropic/` or `google/` prefix on a directly routed name is stripped.
- **Anthropic adapter**: `AnthropicModel` sends an effort level as `output_config.effort` **NEW** (10-07; `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`, `max`), taken from the model config's `reasoning_effort`, else its `output_config.effort`, else the catalogue `thinking` of the alias or base entry. `ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE_ID` serves identity-linked keys. Anthropic has no embeddings API.
- **Amazon Bedrock** **NEW** (10-16): `bedrock/<id>` runs a Claude model through the `AnthropicBedrock` client in `AWS_REGION` (default `us-east-1`). The id is Bedrock's own model or inference-profile id, or its ARN, with an optional effort suffix. Credentials are a bearer token (`AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK`), an access-key pair with an optional session token, or an ambient boto3 source that can be detected without a network call (a profile in `~/.aws`, a web-identity token, ECS container credentials; EC2 instance metadata is not detected). An id is priced as its `claude-*` twin, else that model's OpenRouter entry, else a sibling profile, times 1.1 for a geographic (`us.`, `eu.`, `apac.`, `jp.`, `au.`) or in-region profile of Claude 4.5 or later; `global.` profiles and older models bill the base rate. Bedrock rejects a top-level `cache_control`, so the adapter moves a cache breakpoint to the last cacheable block each turn (10-21).
- **Gemini adapter**: thinking levels **NEW** (11-05): the model config's `reasoning_effort` is sent as `thinking_level` (`xhigh` and `max` become `high`) and replaces a level or budget set in its `thinking_config`; without it a configured `thinking_level` or `thinking_budget` is kept, and only when neither is set does the catalogue `thinking` of the entry apply (`high` for `gemini-4-argon-high`). Thought summaries are on unless the config sets `include_thoughts` to false. Image output tokens are split from text output and billed at the image rate **NEW** (10-24); audio input bills at the audio rate.
- **CLI-backed models** **CHANGED** (10-18): `cc/*` and `codex/*` used to hand the whole task to the Claude Code or Codex CLI, which ran its own tools on the host with permission checks bypassed. Both are now plain models driven turn by turn with the KISS system prompt and KISS tools.
  - `cc/*` runs `claude --print --safe-mode --strict-mcp-config --tools "" --no-session-persistence`: no native tools, MCP servers, `CLAUDE.md`, skills, plugins or hooks. Tool calls travel as text (the `tool_calls` protocol, and Claude's own `<invoke>` form since 11-07) and the CLI is stopped at the end of the first complete run of calls. An effort alias is passed as `--effort` (11-08) and thinking text is shown (11-07).
  - `codex/*` runs `codex exec --json --skip-git-repo-check` with `--ephemeral`, `--ignore-user-config` and `--sandbox read-only`, and with the shell, web search, apps, plugins, skill search, memories and hooks switched off. The KISS system prompt is passed as `model_instructions_file`; reasoning summaries are shown (11-08).
  - **Codex MCP bridge** **NEW** (10-17, `codex_mcp_bridge.py`): every tool but `finish` and a tool that must run between turns (`set_model`) is served to Codex as the MCP server `kiss`. `ToolBridge` listens on an ephemeral loopback port, Codex spawns the stdio proxy `python -m kiss.core.models.codex_mcp_bridge <port>`, each request carries a secret from `KISS_MCP_BRIDGE_TOKEN`, and calls run one at a time in the agent's own process. Codex may call several tools in one turn; the calls are replayed into the conversation as ordinary tool messages, so a later model switch keeps a valid transcript.
  - **Billing** **NEW**: both CLIs default to the subscription (`claude_code_use_subscription`, `codex_use_subscription`). The API-key variables are hidden from the CLI, the turn costs $0 (every `cc/*` and `codex/*` entry is priced $0) and a Codex CLI logged in with an API key is refused. With the switch off and an API key present, a turn is priced at the catalogue rate of the API model behind it, cache tiers included (10-19). A `cc/*` turn is priced request by request, so long-prompt rates and Anthropic's fast-mode and US-inference modifiers apply per request; usage the stream did not attribute to a request (all of it in a multi-request turn without per-request usage) is priced from the summed counts at short-prompt rates with the turn's modifiers. For a model the catalogue lacks or a turn that used several models, the CLI's own `total_cost_usd` is billed when the result carries one. A `codex/*` turn is priced from the turn's summed usage, without OpenAI's processing-tier multipliers and with long-prompt rates only when it made a single request; a model the catalogue cannot price stays at $0 with one warning.
- **Typed decisions and embeddings**: a `dec` entry answers `noul`, `choice` and `score` questions through OpenRouter's Decisions API (`POST /api/alpha/decisions`) instead of generating text; it backs the `decide` tool (section 7) and the Jev classifier (section 11). `get_embedding` serves the 7 `emb` entries (3 OpenAI, 2 Gemini, 2 Together-hosted).
- **Effort aliases** **CHANGED**: a `<base>-<level>` entry copies its base entry's prices and limits and sets `thinking`; the base entry holds the highest supported level, capped at `high`. The 454 aliases are 109 `-high`, 107 `-low`, 103 `-medium`, 71 `-xhigh` and 64 `-max`. Ladders: OpenAI `low`/`medium`/`high`/`xhigh`; Claude up to `low` to `max` depending on the generation (10-07), mirrored onto `bedrock/`, `openrouter/anthropic/claude-*` (10-28) and `cc/claude-*` names; Gemini `low`/`medium`/`high` (11-05); Kimi K3 `low`/`high`/`max`; Grok 4.3 and 4.5 `low`/`medium`/`high` (6 aliases); GLM-5.2 `high`/`max` (4 aliases). The updater also holds a `low`/`high` ladder for Grok 3 Mini, which has no catalogue entry. On 09-30 only OpenAI, OpenRouter, Together and Moonshot names had aliases (118).
- **Prices**: `calculate_cost` prices input, output, cache-read, cache-write (a default and a long lifetime: 5 minutes and 1 hour on Anthropic, 30 minutes and 12 hours on OpenAI), audio and image-output tokens separately.
  - Cache prices an entry states are kept and the others are derived per provider (for an `openrouter/` entry that states a read price only the long-lifetime write is derived; its unstated default write bills at the input price): Anthropic reads at 0.1x the input price (0.05x for Opus 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5, 0.025x for Fable and Mythos 5.1 and, since 11-10, 5.2), writes at 1.25x and 1-hour writes at 2x; OpenAI, its `openrouter/openai/` and rolling `openrouter/~openai/` routes included, reads at 0.1x for most GPT-5 and GPT-6 names, 0.05x for GPT-6.1, `gpt-sol-latest` and `gpt-terra-latest`, 0.25x or 0.5x for older families and the full input price for `-pro` names, with free writes before GPT-5.6 and writes at 1.25x (30-minute) and 2.5x (12-hour) for GPT-5.6, GPT-6 and the rolling aliases that track them; Gemini reads at 0.1x; Moonshot reads at 0.25x.
  - Long prompts **CHANGED**: `prompt_tiers` (10-30; 107 OpenRouter entries, imported from OpenRouter's `pricing.overrides`) replace an entry's prices for prompts strictly longer than `min_prompt_tokens`. Entries without tiers use a name-based uplift on the whole request: 2x input and cache and 1.5x output above 272,000 prompt tokens for `gpt-5.4` (not mini or nano), `gpt-5.5`, `gpt-5.6-*`, `gpt-6-*` and `gpt-6.1-*`, and above 200,000 for `gemini-3.1-pro`, `gemini-2.5-pro` and Claude Sonnet 4.5 (10-29); 5x every rate above 100,000 for Claude Haiku 5.5.
  - Modifiers **NEW**: Anthropic fast mode (`usage.speed` `fast`) bills 2x and US-only inference (`usage.inference_geo` `us`, Claude 4.6 and later) 1.1x; the two stack and neither applies on Bedrock (10-21). OpenAI processing tiers are read from the response's `service_tier`: 0.5x for Flex, 2x for priority or fast with per-family exceptions (2.5x for `gpt-5.5`), 6x for ultrafast (10-08).
  - OpenRouter calls are billed from the provider-reported `usage.cost` (BYOK upstream cost is added only when `is_byok`); a reported cost always replaces the catalogue estimate. A call that fails or is stopped mid-stream is billed for the usage its adapter had received by then; with none, nothing is billed.
- **Default model and fail-over**: with no model named, `get_default_model` takes the first configured provider in the order Anthropic, OpenAI, Gemini, OpenRouter, Together, Z.AI, Moonshot, Bedrock, Claude Code CLI, Codex CLI (`claude-opus-5-5`, `gpt-6.1-sol-medium`, `gemini-3.8-flash`, and so on); `get_fast_model` picks the cheap counterpart (`claude-sonnet-5-5`, `gpt-6-luna`). A run fails over at most once. A non-retryable provider error switches to the entry's declared `fallback` (12 entries: Fable 5 falls back to Opus 4.8) or, with an OpenRouter key, to the model's OpenRouter twin, at the same effort level when that alias exists there and on the base twin otherwise. A safety refusal, content-filter stop, output-cap cut-off or repeated empty turn prefers a model of another family **NEW** (11-01, `model_families.py`): the declared `fallback` when it is of another family, else the first configured of 11 second-opinion models that is of another family (the list is headed by `gpt-6-astra`, `claude-opus-5-5` and `gemini-3.1-pro-preview`), else the same-family `fallback` or twin. With no candidate there is no switch. The conversation is copied over and a warning names both models. The same family table picks the finish-gate reviewer and reviewer sub-agents of another family (section 10).
- **Vision bridge** **NEW** (11-01, `vision_bridge.py`): for a model that cannot read images, each image or PDF a tool returns is described by one call to the first configured of five vision models (`claude-sonnet-5-5`, `gpt-6-luna`, `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` and two OpenRouter twins). The description replaces the attachment, and the call is charged to the task under the vision model's name.
- **Credentials and Settings**: keys live in `~/.kiss/api_keys.env` (one `export KEY=value` line each, mode 0600), loaded at daemon start and applied to the running process when saved, so a newly configured provider's models are listed at once; an empty value deletes the key. Settings has five collapsible subpanels: API Keys (Anthropic, Anthropic workspace id, OpenAI, Z.AI, Moonshot, OpenRouter, Together, Gemini), Custom Models, Bedrock **NEW** (bearer token, access key id, secret access key, session token, region), Claude Code **NEW** (login state from `claude auth status`, the subscription switch, a `claude setup-token` token) and Codex **NEW** (login state from `codex login status`, the subscription switch, "Sign in with ChatGPT" device-code login, an access token). With a registered machine selected, the Settings sheet reads and writes that machine's configuration, custom models and CLI login state (section 15).
- **Model picker**: the daemon lists the function-calling models whose provider is configured, grouped by vendor (Anthropic with `cc/`, Bedrock, OpenAI with `codex/`, Gemini, Z.AI, Moonshot, OpenRouter, Together AI) and sorted by descending price within a group, each row with its input and output price and its use count (table `model_usage`). Routers head the list, followed by custom models. A pick sets the tab's model and the daemon default (`last_model` in `config.json`). With a registered machine selected the picker shows that machine's list **NEW** (10-30).
- **Picker routers**: five SEAs register as models (`register_as_model`). A picked router is the first-applied layer of every run of its tab, and the tab runs on the router's starting model.

  | Router | Starts on | Main model or tiers | Reviewer | |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `bestrouter` | `claude-opus-5-5-medium` | `claude-fable-5-2-max` | `gpt-6-astra-xhigh` | **CHANGED** |
  | `orbestrouter` | `openrouter/anthropic/claude-opus-5.5-medium` | `openrouter/anthropic/claude-fable-5.2-max` | `openrouter/openai/gpt-6-astra-xhigh` | **NEW** |
  | `ccxbestrouter` | `cc/claude-opus-5-5-medium` | `cc/claude-fable-5-2-max` | `codex/gpt-6-astra-xhigh` | **NEW** (11-10) |
  | `autorouter` | `claude-opus-5-5-medium` | 10 small, 11 medium, 6 frontier candidates | chosen by tier | **CHANGED** |
  | `orautorouter` | `openrouter/anthropic/claude-opus-5.5-medium` | 6 small, 8 medium, 3 frontier, all `openrouter/` | chosen by tier | **NEW** |

  - The three best-routers share one prompt: the starting model answers simple questions and queries, the main model does every other task, and the reviewer checks the main model's work read-only through a non-blocking `run_agent(tool_profile="review")` on at most 75% of the task budget; a second round gets the first round's findings and the diff since then. A task that mostly dispatches sub-agents may dispatch, wait and log on the starting model, but the prompt has it call `set_model` to the main model before it triages a failed test run, acts on a sub-agent result that reports a failure or a warning, or writes the final summary **NEW** (11-11). On 09-30 `bestrouter` ran everything on `claude-fable-5-1` and reviewed through `run_parallel`; the starting model arrived 11-03, Fable 5.2 on 11-09 and the reviewers' `-xhigh` suffix on 11-10 (`9878ef72f1`, later than this inventory's HEAD). `codex/gpt-6-astra-xhigh` is not a catalogue entry: the Codex adapter runs `codex/gpt-6-astra` with `model_reasoning_effort="xhigh"`, and the name is sized and priced as that entry.
  - The two auto-routers share another: split the task into units with a mechanical acceptance check; classify each with `decide` (tier, and whether it is guarded work, which always goes to frontier); take the tier's first candidate whose provider is configured with `pick_model`; dispatch it with `run_agent(model=...)`; verify the check; on failure escalate one tier (two after a reasoning failure, at most twice per unit); log every dispatch and outcome to `~/.kiss/MODEL_DECISIONS.md` with `log_decision`. The prompt also sets budget rules, which no code enforces: an estimate prices one model call and a sub-agent is budgeted at about 50 of them, and a pick whose estimate exceeds 25% of the remaining budget is split, or dropped one tier when the classifier allows (guarded work stays on frontier), or put to the user first. `model_menu` and `estimate_cost` price candidates from the catalogue, and `observed_call_costs` reports what each model's calls cost, read from the `llm_call` events of the tasks launched in the last 7 days (argument `days`; the newest 5,000 tasks at most), so a call is dated by its task's launch, not by its own time. Per-model evidence in `~/.kiss/AUTOROUTER.md` (capped at 2,500 characters, rewritten weekly by `/rsi7d`) is included in the prompt. On 09-30 the orchestrator was the first runnable frontier candidate and the tiers held 8, 10 and 2 candidates; it is now fixed and frontier has six.
  - Picking a router fires its `on_picked_as_model` hook (15 s cap). The auto-routers' hook ensures the weekly cron job that runs `/rsi7d autorouter`: an enabled job is left as it is, a paused one is resumed, and a missing one is created for Saturday 01:00 Pacific on `claude-fable-5-2-max` (when that model is not runnable: the first runnable frontier candidate, else the first runnable function-calling model in the picker's order, else the default model) with a $30 budget, $25 of it for the nested `/rsi7d` run.
- **Cost pipeline**: per call, the adapter reports up to nine token classes (input, output, cache read, cache write, 1-hour cache write, audio input, audio output, cached audio, image output). `KISSAgent` prices them with `calculate_cost` times the response's price multiplier unless the provider reported a cost, and commits tokens and cost as one snapshot. `RelentlessAgent` keeps an append-only usage ledger (`_UsageLedger`, folded every 200 records) from which budget, tokens and steps are derived; a retried source counts once, and `reset_usage()` opens a new epoch so a previous run's late spend cannot land in the current one. Classifier, merge-agent, TTS, side-channel and late-arriving sub-agent spend fold into the owning task via `_net_totals` and `_add_late_task_usage`, which also credits finished ancestors; a sub-task stopped on timeout still charges its caller (`StoppedOnTimeoutError`). Each task row stores `model_costs` **NEW** (10-23): the per-model split of its own calls plus the charges of model calls made outside the agent loop (finish-gate review, commit message, speech synthesis, `decide`; 11-05). The Spend subpanel (all-time total, daily heatmap, cost-by-model bars; `getSpendReport`) splits each top-level task's cost across the models its tree used, so each day's sum still equals its listed tasks. A sub-agent that ran on another machine is represented by a `remote_subtasks` record **NEW** (section 15).
- **Live accounting**: cost, tokens, steps and remaining budget are printed in every tool result and shown in the status bar and Task Info; the daily cost-calculation audit is a cron job on the development machine. The tool-result footer also gives the step's tool-output size, the cost of the step's model call, and its output tokens and stop reason **NEW** (10-25); `Budget: $spent/$max` includes sub-agents. Every agent-loop model call that reports usage sends an `llm_call` event (`model`, `step`, `duration_ms`, token counts and `cost`, plus `routine`, `effort` and `stop_reason` **NEW**) to the run's printer, and the daemon's printer persists it; a run without a printer (a quiet `KISSAgent` run) emits none. A call made outside the loop leaves a `gate_stage` record with `stage` `charge` instead. `usage_info` events carry the exact `cost_usd` beside a four-decimal string; the page shows two decimals, four for a positive amount below half a cent, and `<$0.0001` for one below $0.00005 (10-09). The Result panel header lost its tokens and cost 10-12. The budget defaults to $100 per task in Settings (`max_budget`) and to $200 for a run that states none.
- **Model updates**: the "Update Models" button in Settings runs `python -m kiss.scripts.update_models --model-info ~/.kiss/MODEL_INFO.json` as a detached process, one at a time, logging to `~/.kiss/update_models.log`; the running daemon keeps the catalogue it loaded until it restarts. The script lists models from OpenRouter (models and decisions), Together, Gemini, Anthropic, OpenAI and, since 10-21, Bedrock, and Codex slugs from the Codex repository's `models.json`; `cc/` names are derived from the Anthropic listing. Only the OpenRouter and Together listings carry prices (with context lengths, which Gemini's gives too); the Anthropic, OpenAI and Bedrock listings give ids alone. A new direct OpenAI, Anthropic or Gemini entry therefore copies the prices of its OpenRouter twin or, without one, is added at $0 with the comment `NEW: needs pricing` and bills nothing until a later run finds a price. A direct entry that has a price is never repriced, so a vendor's price change reaches it only through a hand edit, and no listing covers the direct Moonshot and Z.AI entries. The script probes each new model for generation, embeddings, decisions, function calling, the Responses API and the highest effort level it accepts, except new `cc/`, `codex/` and `bedrock/` candidates, which are not probed (a CLI name is entered as a function-calling generator, a Bedrock id copies its Claude twin's flags); it then writes the alias entries and replaces the file atomically. It never lists MiniMax models, nor OpenAI `-pro` models since 10-18. Options: `--model-info PATH`, `--dry-run`, `--skip-test` (new models are not probed: their capabilities are taken from the vendor listings), `--test-existing` (re-probes the stored entries of configured providers except aliases, deprecated names and `bedrock/` ids, `cc/` and `codex/` entries included, with or without `--skip-test`), `--scrub-only`, `--verbose`. Run against the repository catalogue, it also rewrites the totals in `README.md` and the lists in `MODELS.md` (split out of the README 09-30). On the development machine a daily cron job runs it at 06:00.
- **`cost_report`** **CHANGED** (10-25): `python -m kiss.scripts.cost_report [--db PATH] [--hours 24] [--since T] [--until T] [--baseline FILE] [--json]` reads `task_history` and `events` of `~/.kiss/history.db` and prints 43 KPIs, among them cost per model call, median and 90th-percentile cost of a task tree, repeated file reads, steps and cost by context-size bucket, the reviewer share of a tree, sub-agents used as shell wrappers, cache hit ratio and refused tool calls by reason. `--since`, `--until` and `--baseline`, which prints each KPI beside an earlier run's, are new; three counts (refused tool calls, `summary` calls made by the model, tool outputs of 10,000 characters or more) are also given per 1,000 model calls.

## 9. Sorcar Extension Agents and slash commands

A Sorcar Extension Agent (SEA) is a Python file that defines exactly one class of its own deriving from `kiss.agents.seas.base.base_sea.BaseSea` (`base_sea.py`, 356 lines) and overrides only the methods it changes. The layout `<name>/<name>_sea.py` is the convention that registers a SEA as a slash command (below); any other `.py` file with such a class runs as a SEA when loaded by path (`run_agent(agent=<path>)`, `run_sea`). The class-based contract replaced the module-level getters of the previous inventory: `base_sea.py` landed 10-05 and the base classes 10-07. **NEW**

**Methods.** Every method has a pass-through default.

- `settings(self, settings)` returns the run's settings dict (`settings | {...}`); it is data only.
- `prompt(self, task)` returns the prompt body; `{task_id}` in it is replaced by the calling task's id. A method that changes the text to an empty one is an error.
- `system_prompt(self, system_prompt)` receives the assembled system prompt and returns the run's: its own text to replace it, `system_prompt + more` to append.
- `tools(self, tools)` receives the built-in toolset plus the caller's tools and returns the run's.
- `tool_call_hook(self, name, args)` runs before every tool call, `llm_call_hook(self, new_messages)` before every model call, `llm_response_hook(self, reply)` after every model reply.
- `description(self)` returns the one sentence `/<name> help` prints; a registered command must return a non-empty one.
- `register_as_model(self)` and `on_picked_as_model(self, work_dir)` serve model-picker SEAs (section 8).

**Chaining.** A SEA extends another by Python inheritance (`class MySea(ShSea)`, or `class MySea(sea_class("sh"))`). The launcher applies each method of every class of the chain base first (`sea_commands._chain` over the MRO), so methods never call `super()`: `settings()` starts from `{}`, `prompt()` from the task, `system_prompt()` and `tools()` from the assembled prompt and toolset. `description()`, `register_as_model()` and `on_picked_as_model()` are ordinary inheritance: the most derived definition is the one called. Each result is type-checked and a wrong type is a `SeaError` naming the SEA and the method.

**Root layer.** `BaseSea` heads every chain, and a plain chat prompt runs the bare `BaseSea`, so editing `base_sea.py` customises every run (the daemon imports it once; SEA files are re-executed on every run). Its one rule of its own is the `summary` cadence (`SUMMARY_EVERY_STEPS = 10`): once a summary is due, every call but `summary` and `finish` still runs, with a reminder appended to its result. The reminder replaced a refusal on 11-02.

**Verdicts** (`src/kiss/core/tool_verdict.py`, 219 lines). `tool_call_hook` returns a typed `Verdict`:

- `ALLOW` lets the call run; `allow_with_note(text)` lets it run and appends `text` to its result.
- `refuse(text)` stops the call; the model reads `REFUSED: the <tool> call did NOT run and had no effect. <text>`. The first refusal in the chain wins.
- `end_run(result, success=True)` ends the whole run with `result` as its outcome: the call and the remaining calls of the same turn do not run. **NEW** (10-31)

**Response hook.** `llm_response_hook` receives an `LLMReply` (`text`, `tool_calls`, `usage` with tokens, cost and stop reason, `step`, `budget_used`, `total_tokens_used`) after every model reply and before any tool call of that reply runs. It returns `ALLOW` or `end_run(...)`; a refusal is a `SeaError`. **NEW** (10-31)

**Tool schemas.** `@tool_schema(parameters, description="")` declares the JSON schema of a tool's parameters (an object schema, else `ValueError`); the declared schema is what every model sees, in native tool definitions and in the text catalogue of CLI-backed models alike. **NEW** (10-31) `@unlisted` leaves a tool out of both while the agent still runs it when called; the deferred MCP tools use it. **NEW** (11-12)

**Settings** (`sea_settings.SETTING_TYPES`, 14 keys). Eleven are per-run parameters: `work_dir`, `model`, `chat_id`, `max_budget`, `model_config`, `use_web_tools`, `auto_classify`, `use_memory`, `compaction` (added 11-05), `tool_profile`, `docker_image`. Three belong to the dispatcher:

- `timeout`: seconds a `run_agent` or `run_parallel` call waits (call's value, else the setting, else 3,600). On expiry the run is not stopped; the call returns its job id (section 10). A `/<name>` command has no such timer.
- `locked`: keys an explicit call argument or option may not change. A different value is refused with an error, never silently overruled.
- `hidden`: the literal `True` in the class's own `settings()` keeps the SEA out of the command list and out of `run_agent` agent names; it stays loadable by path and as a base class. The registry reads the literal from the source, so a computed value is ignored.

One precedence rule covers every setting of a `run_agent`, `run_parallel` or `/<name>` run (`sea_settings.PRECEDENCE_RULE`): an explicit call argument or option, then the SEA's `settings()`, then what the calling task passes on (for `/<name>`, the chat panel's settings), then the user's defaults. `None`, and `""` for string keys, mean "no override". A relative `work_dir` is a path under the calling task's directory. The rule has one exception: the global `compaction` setting, when off, turns compaction off for every run whatever a SEA or a call passes; the per-run `compaction` default is on (section 6).

**Base classes** replaced the `kind` and `channel` settings on 10-07:

| Class | Defaults it lays under the SEA's own keys | Behaviour |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `BaseSea` | none | An ordinary session |
| `WorkerSea` | `auto_classify`, `use_web_tools`, `use_memory` off | A tool-bound run on the caller's tree as it is; `create_worktree` and `commit_and_merge` are dropped from its toolset |
| `ChannelSea` | the worker defaults plus `work_dir = ~/.kiss/channel_work`, all locked | Six behaviours in `sea_settings.CHANNEL_BEHAVIOURS`: worker, scratch directory, no inheritance from the calling task, a channel workspace held for the run's lifetime, the channel preamble, listing among the installed channels (section 19) |

**Removed keys.** `sea_settings.REMOVED_SETTINGS` lists 14 keys that are now a `SeaError` naming the replacement, among them `kind`, `channel` and `allow_fan_out` (removed 10-07), `extends` (replaced by inheritance 10-05), `preset`, `inherit`, and `use_worktree` and `auto_commit` (removed 10-09: the agent isolates its own edits with the worktree tools, section 12). `classify_tasks` and `model_name` are refused as renamed (`auto_classify`, `model`).

**`model_config` flags.** A `model_config` replaces, as a whole, the configuration that would otherwise apply, the settings panel's custom endpoint included; since 11-03 the daemon instead adds a dict holding only framework-only keys (the 8 of `model.FRAMEWORK_ONLY_CONFIG_KEYS`, among them `plain_io` and `coding_gate`) to that endpoint's configuration, so the endpoint stays.

- `plain_io: true` leaves the framework's additions out of what the model reads: the system prompt is the SEA's `system_prompt()` return alone (the memory protocol stays while the run has the memory tools, and a CLI-backed model keeps its text tool catalogue) and tool results carry no usage line. **NEW** (10-31)
- `base_url` (with `api_key`) routes every model call of the run to an OpenAI-compatible endpoint; `model` must still be a catalogued name so calls are priced.
- `coding_gate: false` exempts the run from the finish gate (section 6). **NEW** (11-03)

**Headless runs.** `kiss.agents.sorcar.headless.run_sea(sea, task, *, model="", work_dir="", max_budget=None, save_trajectories=True, verbose=False, printer=None)` (203 lines) runs a SEA in the calling process, without a daemon or a UI, through the daemon's own evaluation path, and returns a `SeaRunResult` (`result`, `success`, `error`, `budget_used`, `total_tokens_used`, `total_steps`, `trajectory_paths`). Its `model`, `work_dir` and `max_budget` arguments rank below the SEA's settings: each applies only when the SEA sets none. `run_agent` and `run_parallel` need the daemon, so a SEA meant for headless runs leaves the `agents` tool group out of its profile. `scripts/run_sea.py` (73 lines) is the command-line form. `examples/rl_episode/rl_episode_sea.py` (220 lines) uses run-ending verdicts, the response hook, a declared tool schema and `plain_io` together as a guess-the-number episode, and `wire_check.py` next to it checks that the model reads the system prompt byte for byte. **NEW** (10-31)

**Lint and generated docs** (console script `sea`, added 10-05). **NEW**

- `sea lint [paths] [--fix] [--registered]` (`sea_lint.py`, 1,022 lines) has 13 rule codes: `broken`, `renamed-key`, `removed-key`, `base-class`, `verdict`, `redundant-key`, `no-description`, `unknown-model`, `lock-without-value`, `hidden-not-literal`, `stale-docstring`, `home-literal`, `stale-prose`. It refuses a file that does not load or breaks the contract, renamed and removed keys, a `tool_call_hook` that returns a literal `None` or string instead of a `Verdict`, a key that only repeats a base class's value, a registered command without `description()`, a `model` that is neither catalogued nor a picker SEA, a `locked` key with no value, a `hidden` that evaluates to `True` without being written as the literal `True`, a docstring that mentions a removed getter, a home path written as a literal in code, and stale sentences about SEA semantics in the generated documentation pages. `--fix` rewrites four of them (`renamed-key`, `base-class`, `removed-key`, `verdict`).
- `sea docs [--check]` (`sea_docs.py`, 274 lines) regenerates six tables (`precedence`, `settings`, `bases`, `channel`, `options`, `commands`) inside the blocks that the HTML comments `sea-docs: NAME` and `/sea-docs` delimit in three files: `src/kiss/server/README.md` and the website's `sea-commands.md` and `cli.md`.
- `uv run check` runs both (section 22).

### Registry and slash commands

`src/kiss/agents/sorcar/sea_commands.py` (1,905 lines) keeps the registry.

- **What registers.** A folder `<name>/` becomes `/<name>` only when it holds `<name>_sea.py`; loose `*_sea.py` files, the `base` folder and hidden SEAs do not register.
- **Sources**, highest precedence first: `third_party_agents/`; the folders listed in `~/.kiss/SEAS.md` (a later line beats an earlier one; `#` comments, `~` and environment variables are handled); the bundled `seas/`; the built-in `/cron` (`cron_agent.CronAgentSea`, section 20), added as the fourth and lowest source on 10-04.
- **Watcher.** A thread rescans every 2 s and open chats receive the new command list; a lookup that misses triggers an immediate rescan.
- **Running.** `/xxx text` at the first character of a prompt runs the SEA in the tab's own run: its settings, system prompt, tools and hooks apply to that very run, and the chat keeps the raw `/xxx text` as the display prompt. The previous inventory's rewrite into a `run_agent` directive was removed on 10-04. **CHANGED**
- **Not a command.** An unknown name runs as typed. A bare `/xxx` runs the SEA only when its `prompt()` turns the empty task into text (`/sync` does); otherwise it too runs as typed.
- **`help` and `check`.** `/xxx help` answers with `description()` and `/xxx check` with a diagnostic: the description, class chain, effective settings, model, tools added, methods defined and a sample prompt. **NEW** (`check`, 10-04) Neither runs the SEA nor calls a model; a broken SEA answers `/xxx is broken: <error>`.
- **Picker SEAs.** A model-picker SEA picked on a tab is the first-applied layer of every run of that tab whose model names it (`task_runner._picker_sea`, `sea_commands.sea_layers`). A `/xxx` command typed there runs its own SEA on top: one whose `system_prompt()` appends keeps the routing protocol, one that replaces the prompt (`/sh`) drops it. A `run_agent` child inherits the real model the picker resolved to, not the picker layer. A `model` setting that names a picker is resolved to the picker's own `model` setting, without the picker's protocol.
- **Help for named commands.** A plain task the user wrote gets, after its text, a `# Slash commands the user named` block with one `` - `/xxx`: <description> `` line per command its text or the chat's earlier tasks mention (`sea_commands.named_commands_help`). Paths, URLs and link targets do not count, and a SEA run or a sub-agent gets no block. **NEW** (10-30)
- **Loading.** Every SEA file of a run is executed once by the one loader `sea_settings.execute_python_file`, and `sea_commands.load_sea` instantiates the one `BaseSea` subclass it defines; none or several is an error.
- **By name.** `run_agent(agent=...)` resolves names through the same registry, ignoring case, spaces, hyphens and underscores; an empty name or a generic one (`general`, `assistant`, `sorcar`, ...) runs the hidden `sorcar` SEA (section 10).

### Bundled SEAs

`src/kiss/agents/seas/` holds 31 folders: `base` and 30 SEAs, of which 28 are commands, 2 are hidden and 5 are model-picker entries. In the table, "caller's" means the setting is left to the tab or the calling task; W marks a `WorkerSea`, all others derive from `BaseSea`. Timeouts apply to `run_agent` and `run_parallel` callers only.
