Since 11-07 the review starts from a one-sentence statement of the paper and the causal evidence for its central claim, a single run with no baseline or ablation caps the rating at reject, the prompt carries an ICLR 2027 calibration, and `check_review` adds an evidence-words line that never fails | caller's, web on: `read_paper`, `check_review` | `bestrouter` → `claude-opus-5-5-medium` (the caller's until 10-26) | caller's | 7,200 s | **CHANGED** |

Since 11-07 the contribution is written down before the outline (research question, central claim, the controlled comparison that tests it), and `check_paper` gates concessions in the abstract (one scope sentence at most), engineering trivia in the abstract and introduction, and hedged or question headings, and lists the concessions in the body | caller's, web on: `check_paper`, `build_paper` | caller's | caller's | 21,600 s | **CHANGED** |

`third_party_agents/` adds 44 SEA folders in the same layout (section 19), and `src/kiss/agents/sorcar/cron_prompt_sea.py` (81 lines) is the SEA every prompt cron job runs under (section 20); it sits outside a `<name>/` folder and is no command.

Files these SEAs keep under `~/.kiss`: `AGENTS.md`, `SEAS.md`, `AUTOROUTER.md`, `MODEL_DECISIONS.md`, `recommendations.json`, `machines.json`, `memories/<repo>/knowledge.sqlite3`, `channel_work/`, `cron/jobs.json` (the weekly rsi7d, daily knowledge, recommendations and good-morning jobs), and read access to `history.db`.

## 10. Sub-agents and parallelism

One dispatcher (`src/kiss/agents/sorcar/agent_dispatch.py`, 3,422 lines, with the guards of `fanout_guard.py`, 441 lines) serves the `agents` tool group: `run_agent`, `agent_job`, `run_parallel` and `number_of_cores`. A sub-task runs on the daemon as a nested sub-agent of the calling task, with its own task row and its own tab. Its cost, tokens and steps are folded into the caller's, including spend that arrives after the caller's row has closed (section 8).

- **`run_agent`** **CHANGED**: `run_agent(task, agent="", model="", tool_profile="", max_budget="", timeout="", options="", wait="", machine="", description="")` runs one SEA (section 9) on one task; every argument is a string. It returns YAML: a `ran:` line, then `success` and `summary` (of a run of several sessions, the last session's summary only).
- **Agent resolution** **CHANGED** (`resolve_agent`): three rules lead to one SEA path. Empty, or one of 10 generic labels (`general`, `agent`, `sorcar`, `kiss`, `analysis`, `analyst`, `assistant`, `default`, `llm`, `model`), is the plain sub-agent, the hidden `sorcar` SEA. A path (a `.py` suffix or a separator, relative to the calling task's work dir) is that file. Anything else is a registered slash-command name with case, spaces, `-` and `_` ignored: a bundled SEA, a `SEAS.md` folder, an installed channel or `cron`. `reviewer`, `review`, `codereview` and `codereviewer` are refused with a pointer to `tool_profile="review"`; any other unknown name, `worker` included, is an error that lists the registered names, with a did-you-mean suggestion when one of them is spelled closely.
- **`options`** **NEW**: a JSON object in the SEA settings vocabulary. `agent_dispatch.OPTION_TYPES` has 12 keys: `work_dir` (relative to the calling task's), `chat_id`, `model_config`, `docker_image`, the booleans `use_web_tools`, `use_memory`, `auto_classify` and `compaction` (`false`: the sub-task never compacts its context), and four call-only keys, `inherit`, `workspace` (the account of a multi-account channel), `add_to_prompt` and `add_to_system_prompt`. `model`, `tool_profile`, `max_budget` and `timeout` are arguments and are refused inside `options`. A renamed key (`classify_tasks`, `model_name`, `append_to_prompt`, `append_to_system_prompt`) is refused with its new name, a removed one (`use_worktree`, `auto_commit`, `allow_fan_out`, `is_parallel`, `kind`, `channel` and others) with the reason, any other unknown key with the known keys and, when one is spelled closely, a did-you-mean suggestion. A run setting passed as a keyword argument gets a hint that shows the `options` object to pass.
- **Precedence** **NEW** (`sea_settings.PRECEDENCE_RULE`, one rule for every dispatch): an explicit argument or `options` key wins, then the SEA's `settings()`, then what the calling task passes on, then the user's defaults. A call that passes a different value for a key the SEA lists in `locked` is refused, never silently overruled; the daemon checks the locks again when it applies the run (`sea_apply.apply_run`).
- **Inheritance** **CHANGED** (`inherit_from_parent`, the one table behind `run_agent` and `run_parallel`): a sub-task takes the caller's model (and its `model_config` when it runs the caller's launch model and the SEA picks none), a budget share, the chat, the `base_system_prompt` and `add_to_system_prompt` text of the caller's run, the appended prompt text, the extra tools, `use_web_tools`, `use_memory`, `compaction`, the Docker container or exec bridge the caller runs in, and the caller's worktrees, which it edits instead of isolating the repositories again (section 12). The prompt that the caller's SEA returned from `system_prompt()` is not passed on: a sub-task starts from the inherited base or its own default (section 11), appends the inherited text and applies its own SEA's method. A sub-task on another machine (`machine=`) does not take the chat, the container or exec bridge, the extra tools or the worktrees, which exist only on the caller's machine. A channel SEA inherits nothing and runs in `~/.kiss/channel_work` (`cron` in `~/.kiss/cron/work`); `inherit: false` opts any other SEA out. A sub-task of a scheduled run keeps the run's no-questions rule and is recorded as a cron task too (section 20). `allow_fan_out`, `use_worktree` and `auto_commit` are no longer inherited: the three settings were removed on 10-07 and 10-09.
- **Budget** **CHANGED**: a sub-task whose `max_budget` neither the call nor the SEA names gets the caller's remaining budget, less what the caller's still-running jobs of other calls hold, divided by the number of tasks in the call plus one: half for one `run_agent`, a third each for a `run_parallel` of two. A budget that the call or the SEA names is capped at the same unreserved remainder (`budget_cap`, applied by `sea_apply.cap_budget`); with nothing left the sub-task is refused. A review's share is at most $5 plus $5 per 1,000 changed lines of the work dir (`git diff --numstat HEAD` plus untracked files; `fanout_guard.review_budget`, 10-25); the limit does not apply to a budget that the call or the SEA names.
- **Timeouts and background jobs** **CHANGED**: `timeout` bounds the call, not the sub-task. The wait is the `timeout` argument, else the SEA's `timeout` setting, else 3,600 s (300 s on 09-30). At expiry the sub-task keeps running as a job and the call returns a notice with the job id (`agent-` plus 8 hex digits). `wait="false"` returns the notice as soon as the sub-task's tab exists, at most 30 s later.
- **`agent_job`** **NEW**: `agent_job(job_id, action="tail", timeout_seconds="")` follows a job. `tail` says whether it runs, or returns its result once it has finished; `wait` blocks until it finishes or `timeout_seconds` pass; `kill` stops it. `wait_any` (11-09) waits on several ids, or on every job of the task when `job_id` is empty, and returns every finished result not yet read plus the names of the jobs still running. A task sees only its own jobs. On the host, a `Bash(until=)` poll and a `bash_job` wait end early once every job of the task has finished with a result unread (section 7) **NEW** (11-09).
- **End of the caller's run**: `finish` and `commit_and_merge` are each refused once while jobs are still running, with their ids; a job still running when the run ends is cancelled. Stop on the caller cancels the sub-task its call is waiting on, and what a stopped sub-task spent is still charged to the caller.
- **Clean-up when a sub-agent ends** **NEW** (11-11): a sub-agent on the host or in an attached container gets a private folder, `<system temp dir>/kiss-sub-<8 characters>`. It is the `Scratch dir` of its Task Settings, the `TMPDIR`, `TMP` and `TEMP` of every command its tools start, and the place of its job logs and spilled output, so none of these lands under the work tree's `tmp/`. When its run ends (finish, failure, Stop or the caller's kill) its background jobs are killed, then every process whose start environment still carries `KISS_SUBAGENT_DIR=<folder>` (`processes.kill_env_marked`, which also finds a process that left its group through `setsid` or `nohup`), and the folder is deleted. A top-level task keeps `./tmp/<task-id>/` and its jobs. Not covered: a process started with a scrubbed environment or as another user, exec-bridge runs, and the folder of a sub-agent whose daemon died.
- **Same-model rule** **NEW** (11-01, by model and effort since 11-11, `fanout_guard.same_model_conflict`): a sub-task that would run on the caller's own model at the caller's own reasoning effort (whatever route serves it, a Bedrock id being the Claude model behind it; the effort is the `reasoning_effort` of the model configuration, else the catalog effort of the name) is refused, and the refusal names a model to pass that the rule lets through as the sub-task would run it (`model_families.other_worker_model`: the strongest model of another family, else the caller's model at another effort, else a strong model of its own family). Four cases pass: a review; a fan-out of two or more tasks that each name a piece of their own (paths, directories, dotted names, URLs, backticked spans; `tmp/` paths and a piece named by every task of a fan-out of three or more do not count); a caller whose own task asks for sub-agents (it mentions sub-agents, sub-tasks, `run_agent`, `run_parallel`, parallel agents, adversarial testing or training, a second opinion or a reviewer); another model, or another effort of the same model, named by the call or by the SEA, whatever its vendor. Channels, `inherit: false` calls and runs on another machine are not checked.
- **Second-opinion reviewer** **NEW** (11-01): a review whose model neither the call nor the SEA names, and that would run on the caller's family, is moved to the first of the 11 `SECOND_OPINION_MODELS` of another family that has a credential (`gpt-6-astra`, `claude-opus-5-5` and `gemini-3.1-pro-preview` lead the list). The `ran:` line says so, or says that no other family is configured.
- **Inferred review profile** **CHANGED**: with no `tool_profile`, a sub-task of a reviewer, and a task that reads as a review, get the read-only `review` profile (section 7) unless the task contains one of 19 change verbs such as fix, implement, write or refactor. A task reads as a review when it matches the review words of `fanout_guard.py`: review, audit, critique, inspect, regression, read-only, bug-hunt, vulnerability, adversarial, or a search for bugs, defects, flaws, issues or mistakes. The `ran:` line then shows `tools=review(inferred)`. `KISS_TOOL_PROFILES=0` turns the inference off.
- **Sandboxed and pinned callers** **NEW** (10-30): a caller that runs in a Docker container or behind an exec bridge may only start plain sub-agents in the same sandbox. `inherit: false`, `machine`, `docker_image`, `work_dir`, any other SEA, `use_web_tools: true`, `use_memory: true` and a task that begins with a registered slash command are refused. A daemon started with `KISS_PIN_TASK_MODEL` (set by the Terminal-Bench harness, `src/kiss/benchmarks/terminal_bench/daemon.py`) keeps every sub-task on the caller's model: a call or a SEA that names another model or a `model_config` is refused, and so is `set_model`.
- **Other machines** **NEW** (10-12): `machine=<registered name or host>` runs the sub-task on that machine's daemon, and the `ran:` line ends `machine=<name>` (section 15).
- **`description`** **NEW** (10-12): a short title shown as the header of the call in the chat, on `run_agent`, `run_parallel` and `run_commands_parallel`.
- **The `ran:` line** **NEW** (`run_config.run_config_line`): the sub-task's effective configuration on one line, for example `sh model=gpt-5 tools=bash budget=$1.00 timeout=3600s inherited=model,chat_id,max_budget pinned=use_web_tools(True->False)`. It names the SEA (`sub-agent` for the plain one, `(channel)` after a channel), the model, the tool profile, the budget, the wait, the keys taken from the caller and the values the SEA's settings replaced. A SEA reached by path that is also a command gets `(also agent="name")`.
- **Path rewrite**: task text that names the caller's main repository is rewritten to the caller's worktree for an inheriting sub-task on the same daemon (`dispatch_path_rewrite`, on by default; `KISS_DISPATCH_PATH_REWRITE=0` turns it off).
- **`run_parallel`** **CHANGED** (10-07): `run_parallel(tasks, agent="", model="", tool_profile="", max_budget="", timeout="", options="", description="")` is one `run_agent` call per task of a JSON array, started together and waited for together; the `max_workers` argument is gone. The SEA is resolved and checked once and every task is planned before any starts, so an error starts nothing. The result is a YAML list in input order, and a child still running at `timeout` is returned as a job notice. A report over `tool_output_max_chars` (50,000) is written to a file and replaced by a digest of one line per child (11-02). `tasks` is parsed leniently (raw newlines and lone backslashes are repaired) and a `$(...)` substitution is rejected. `run_parallel` and `number_of_cores` are offered only to a run in parallel mode: the web client always asks for it, `is_parallel=False` in the Python API's `run()` (section 6) turns it off, and the sub-tasks of a sequential caller do not get it either. The prompt asks for at most two levels of nesting; the code sets no limit.
- **`run_commands_parallel`**: `run_commands_parallel(commands, max_workers=0, timeout_seconds=1800, max_output_chars=5000, description="")` runs shell commands concurrently in threads with no model in the loop (section 7). A report too long for the context is written under `tmp/tool-out/` and replaced by a digest.
- **Sub-agent tabs**: a sub-agent's tab has the deterministic id `{parent_tab_id}__sub_{task_id}`. It opens on every connected client while the sub-agent runs and is closed everywhere by `subagentDone` when the sub-agent finishes, or by `closeSubagentTab` when any client closes it. The user's close is remembered, so a replay does not reopen the tab. Expanding a finished sub-agent's panel in the parent's transcript reopens its tab on every surface until a client closes it again **NEW** (10-30); the tab of a finished `/ask` answerer stays closed. Sub-agents of a task on another machine are relayed the same way (section 15).
- 31,442 of the 41,127 recorded task rows are sub-agents; 7,644 of the 9,685 top-level tasks ran in parallel mode. 5,609 `run_parallel`, 3,066 `run_agent` and 872 `agent_job` calls are recorded.

## 11. Task classification, tags and chat summaries

- **Pre-run classifier** **CHANGED** (`task_classifier.py`, 1,030 lines): before a run, one call decides a single verdict, `is_simple`: the task involves neither software development nor Internet search. A simple task runs on `SYSTEM_LITE.md` (53 lines) instead of `SYSTEM.md` (209 lines); a failed or disabled classification means the full prompt. The need for a worktree is no longer classified (`is_development` removed 10-09): the agent calls `create_worktree` itself (section 12). A run that cannot edit files (no built-in toolset, or a profile without the edit tools such as `review` or `shell`) gets the lite prompt without a classification (10-25). The verdict is cached in `~/.kiss/task_classifier_cache.json` (2,000 entries, 7 days, keyed by criteria, model, model config and task), and the classifier's spend is folded into the run's totals. The tab shows "Classifying task…" while the call runs; the "Preparing worktree…" phase was removed on 10-09.
- **Switches**: `KISS_DISABLE_TASK_CLASSIFIER=1` turns the classifier off. A run's `auto_classify` (a SEA setting, an `options` key of `run_agent`, an argument of the Python API) overrides the `classify_tasks` config key, which is on by default. The "Classify tasks before running" checkbox was removed from Settings on 10-15; a value stored in `config.json` still counts.
- **LLM classifier**: one non-agentic call on the run's model, whose prompt asks for a bare JSON verdict. For Gemini and the OpenAI-compatible providers (OpenAI, OpenRouter, Together, Z.AI, Moonshot) the call also enforces the verdict's JSON schema, and one prompt-only attempt follows when it yields no verdict; every other provider, Anthropic included, gets a single prompt-only attempt. The task is cut to 20,000 characters, and each attempt is configured with an output cap of 1,000 tokens (`max_tokens`), a `stream_stall_timeout` of 60 s and a nominal budget of $1, which is checked before the single request and cannot stop it. The `cc/` and `codex/` CLI backends apply neither the cap nor that timeout: their CLIs get no output limit, and a turn is abandoned after `timeout` seconds of silence (a `model_config` key, 300 by default).
- **Jev (decisions model)** **CHANGED**: when `decisions_tool_available()` holds, the classifier asks `openrouter/~typesafe/jev-latest` instead. The three conditions are the `classify_with_decisions` config key (on by default since 10-03), an `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, and a catalogue entry marked `is_decisions_supported`. Jev answers one typed `choice` question with five kinds: `development`, `git_only`, `internet`, `simple` and `ambiguous`. `git_only` and `simple` count as simple, and a choice with a probability under 0.6 counts as `ambiguous`. The call has a 5 s timeout; when it fails, the LLM classifier answers and both spends are charged. The same three conditions offer the agent the `decide(state, questions)` tool (section 7). The "Use Jev (decisions model)" checkbox was removed from Settings on 10-15.
- **Tags** **CHANGED** (`task_metadata.py`, 644 lines): when a task finishes, regexes (no model call) write up to 3 comma-separated tags to `task_history.tags` (`MAX_TAGS`; 6 until 10-12). The vocabulary has 22 tags (`ALL_TAGS`): `work` or `personal` first (`work` by default), then `secret`, `chore`, `question`, 15 activity tags (`coding`, `testing`, `debugging`, `review`, `research`, `paper`, `writing`, `docs`, `data`, `devops`, `messaging`, `browsing`, `shopping`, `finance`, `scheduling`), `subagent` and `failed`. The scope tag, `secret` and `failed` are never dropped by the cap; the others fill the remaining slots in that order. The Chats panel's Tag filter, applied in SQL, offers the vocabulary without `subagent`; a task row no longer lists its tags **CHANGED** (11-12; section 16).
- **Secret scanner** **NEW** (10-12, `has_secret_value`): a task was already tagged `secret` when its text names a secret (a password, API key, credential, private key, credit card, `.env` and similar); it is now also tagged when its trajectory holds a credential-like value. The trajectory is the task text, the result and every persisted event, tool arguments and outputs included. The values are a private-key block, an `sk-` key, a GitHub, AWS, Slack or Google token, a JWT, a `Bearer` token, a `scheme://user:password@` URL, and a password, secret, token or API-key assignment. Placeholders such as `<token>`, `${TOKEN}`, `changeme` and `****` do not count. The tag is a label and a filter; nothing else reads it.
- **SEA column**: `task_history.sea` holds the file stem of the SEA that ran the task (`bestrouter_sea`, `review_paper_sea`, `cron_agent`, `slack_sea`, ...; empty for a plain run), taken from the run's SEA path and back-filled for sub-agents from their parent's `run_agent` call. The Chats panel's SEA filter offers `All`, `None` and one entry per script seen; a task row no longer names its SEA **CHANGED** (11-12).
- **`task_history` columns** **CHANGED**: a newly created table has 26 columns. Four were added since 09-30: `success` (the `finish` verdict, NULL after a crash or a Stop; 10-11), `is_cron` (a run the scheduler started, or a sub-task of one; 10-17), `model_costs` (JSON of cost and tokens per model, read by the Spend panel; 10-23) and `machine` (the short host name of the machine a synchronised row ran on, empty for this one; 11-08). `history_columns.py` (250 lines, standard library only, 10-17) adds missing columns to an older database, so the daemon and `sync_db` run the same code (section 15); it drops none, so a table created with the 09-30 schema ends with 28 columns: `is_worktree` and `auto_commit_mode`, which left the schema on 10-09, stay in it. `is_cron` is back-filled from the cron marker text or a `/cron/runs/` work dir, and `machine` from each task's first events, once per database.
- **Chat summaries** **CHANGED** (`chat_summary.py`, 210 lines): `chat_summaries(chat_id, summary, last_launched)` stores a title of up to 8 words per chat (target 6-8, derived from the chat's first five top-level tasks, sometimes shorter or empty); History shows it as the group header when present and the first task's text otherwise (4,927 rows on the development machine, 4,764 of them with a non-empty summary and none with a title typed by the user). The title is built without a model: the tasks' leading words in order, a slash command's name as words, greetings and politeness dropped, URLs reduced to their host. The table has two more columns now: `last_finished` (the end instant of the chat's newest task, 0 while it runs; 10-16) and `user_summary`. The row is refreshed when a task of the chat finishes, by the backfill, and by `sync_db` after a merge.
- **Chat rename** **NEW** (10-15): a title the user types is stored in `user_summary` and wins over the computed one on every recompute; an empty title brings the computed one back. The command is `setChatSummary`, sent by the pencil button or a double-click on the title in the Chats panel (section 16); every surface refreshes.
- **Chat and task delete** **NEW** (10-20): `deleteTask` removes a task, its sub-tasks and their events and recomputes the chat's summary; `deleteChat` removes every task of the chat and its summary row. Both are refused while a task they would remove is running. Every removed task id is recorded as a tombstone in `deleted_tasks`, and `deleteChat` also records the chat in `deleted_chats`, so the deletion reaches synchronised machines (section 15).
- **Backfill**: `uv run python -m kiss.scripts.backfill_task_metadata [--db PATH] [--refresh]` (`src/kiss/scripts/backfill_task_metadata.py`, 72 lines) adds missing columns, then fills rows without tags and chats without a summary in batches of 500 and sub-agent rows without a SEA one parent task at a time (from the parent's `run_agent` calls), while the daemon runs. `--refresh` recomputes every tag and every derived summary and keeps the user's titles.

## 12. Git worktrees, merging and auto-commit

`GitWorktreeOps` (`src/kiss/agents/sorcar/git_worktree.py`, 4,319 lines) is the git plumbing, `SorcarAgent` (`sorcar_agent.py`) carries the two worktree tools and the merge engine, and `src/kiss/server/merge_flow.py` (929 lines, 2,212 before 10-09) serves the buttons.

- **Agent-driven isolation** **CHANGED** (10-09; folded into `SorcarAgent` 10-10): the launcher no longer creates a worktree. Every run (web app, CLI, Python API, cron job) starts in the caller's work dir, and the agent isolates its own edits with two tools of the `edit` group (section 7): `create_worktree(path="")` and `commit_and_merge()`. The worktree protocol in `SYSTEM.md` and `SYSTEM_LITE.md` has it call the first once per repository before its first change to a file git does not ignore, and the second once the work is verified; a task that edits no repository file gets no worktree. Removed 10-09: the "Use worktree" and "Auto commit" settings, the `use_worktree` and `auto_commit` SEA settings (now refused by name), the classifier's `is_development` verdict (it decides only `is_simple`, section 11), the "Preparing worktree…" launch phase and the automatic commit and merge after a successful task. `WorktreeSorcarAgent` was removed 10-10.
- **`create_worktree`**: under the repository's locks, `git worktree add -b kiss/wt-<epoch>-<8 hex>` checks a new branch out at `<repo>/.kiss-worktrees/kiss_wt-<epoch>-<8 hex>` from the checkout's current branch; the branch's git config records the branch to merge back into (`kiss-original`) and the owning process (`kiss-owner-pid`, `kiss-owner-identity`). `copy_dirty_state` mirrors the checkout's uncommitted state (staged, unstaged and untracked files, deletions, renames) into the worktree and commits it as `kiss: baseline from dirty state` (`kiss-baseline`), so the task builds on what the user sees and the merge later applies only what the task added. Each ignored `node_modules` directory whose parent directory exists in the worktree is symlinked in, a file named `.venv` is written there and into every directory with a tracked `pyproject.toml` (where the repository un-ignores the name, a directory that ignores itself instead; only when the main checkout has a `.venv` to share) so that no `uv` (typed, inside `bash -c` or in a script) builds the worktree a Python environment of its own (`GitWorktreeOps.block_own_environment`; a typed `uv run` is rewritten to the main checkout's environment, whose interpreter the Task Settings of every agent and the first line of every pytest run name) **NEW** (11-11), `.kiss-worktrees/` goes into the repository's `info/exclude`, `core.untrackedCache` is set to `true` when unset, and the work dir moves into the worktree at the same sub-directory offset (event `worktree_created`).
- **One worktree per repository** **NEW** (10-10): `create_worktree("<path in another repository>")` isolates that repository as well; the work dir stays put and the agent edits the files under the returned directory. A task holds at most one worktree per repository (`SorcarAgent.worktrees`), and `commit_and_merge` merges each into its own repository's branch.
- **No worktree**: outside a git repository, on a detached HEAD, in a repository without commits, on a git failure or with a dirty tracked submodule the tool returns the reason and the task edits in place. A Docker run is not offered the tools, and `WorkerSea` and `ChannelSea` runs (section 9) drop them: a worker works on the caller's tree, a channel SEA in `~/.kiss/channel_work` by default (section 19).
- **Write guard** **NEW** (`_guard_worktree`): while the run has the tools, `Write` or `Edit` of a checkout path that git does not ignore is refused until the repository has a worktree, and the refusal names the `create_worktree` call to make. A path of a repository whose worktree the work dir is not in is refused with the worktree path to use; an absolute checkout path of the work dir's own worktree is remapped into it. Ignored paths (`git check-ignore`), paths inside a worktree and repositories whose worktree setup failed pass. `rollback` is refused while the work dir is in a checkout (checkpoints, section 7). The first `finish` with an unmerged worktree is refused once. `Bash` is outside this guard, except for a shell rewrite that it applies as a `Write` or an `Edit` (section 7), which is judged like one: any other `Bash` write goes straight into the checkout, and the worktree rules of `Bash` (section 7) apply only inside a worktree.
- **Sub-agents**: a sub-agent inherits the worktrees of its calling tasks (`inherit_worktrees`, `isolated_repos`, wire field `isolatedRepos`), edits them in place and never creates a second worktree for the same repository; the calling task commits and merges. Checkout paths in a sub-agent's task text are rewritten to the worktree, except those under the checkout's `.venv`, the one environment the worktree runs on (section 10).
- **`commit_and_merge`**: for each worktree, (1) stage and commit everything with a generated message, and anything that arrives during the commit in a second one (`kiss: commit late-arriving changes`); (2) remove the worktree and move the work dir back; (3) under the repository's locks, stash the checkout's own uncommitted changes (`git stash push --include-untracked`), check out the original branch when the checkout sits on another, squash-merge, pop the stash and delete the task branch. A run that changed code first passes the coding gate's verifier and review, and the first call while a `run_agent` job is still running is refused once (section 6). When a step fails after the directory was removed, the branch is checked out again at the same path (`GitWorktreeOps.reattach`) so the agent can fix the problem and call the tool again. Each outcome is broadcast as `worktree_result`.
- **Squash merge**: the work lands as one commit on the original branch, with the task branch's last commit message and a `User prompt:` and a `Result:` block ensured for each of the two values the agent holds (an HTML result is converted to Markdown): `commit_and_merge` runs before the task has a result, so its commit carries the prompt alone, a merge from the bar after the task carries both, and a `SorcarAgent` outside a chat (`run_sea`, section 9) holds neither. A worktree made from a clean checkout is merged with `git merge --squash`; one with a baseline is merged as a synthetic commit (`git commit-tree <branch>^{tree} -p <baseline>`) applied by one `git cherry-pick --no-commit`, with `-X theirs` only while the original branch still sits at the baseline's parent. Outcomes (`MergeResult`): `SUCCESS`, `CONFLICT`, `CHECKOUT_FAILED`, `MERGE_FAILED` (the squash commit failed, for example on a rejecting hook; the tree is reset and the branch kept) and `STASH_FAILED` (the checkout's changes could not be stashed; nothing is touched and the blocking files are listed). The stash is popped after `SUCCESS` and `CHECKOUT_FAILED` and stays in `git stash` after a failed merge, whose message gives the manual steps. A merge that would overwrite a file git ignores in the checkout (a never-committed `.env`) is refused as a conflict **NEW** (11-04). A `kiss-scratch` merge driver keeps the incoming version of `PROGRESS.md` and `src/kiss/INJECTIONS.md`.
- **Conflict resolution** (`merge_conflict_resolver.py`, 246 lines; in `src/kiss/server/` until 10-09): on `CONFLICT`, `commit_and_merge` and the bar's merge button re-apply the branch with the conflicts left in the tree and run the bundled `/merge` SEA (`src/kiss/agents/seas/merge/merge_sea.py`, a `WorkerSea` with a $5 budget and its own system prompt) as a sub-agent in the checkout, on the parent's model unless the SEA names one, its cost attributed to the parent task. The merge is committed only when `HEAD` has not moved, no path is unmerged, no `<<<<<<<` or `>>>>>>>` line remains in the files that conflicted and something is staged; otherwise the tree is restored and the result gives the manual commands. `/merge <task>` typed into an idle tab runs the same SEA on the tab's work dir.
- **Unmerged worktrees**: a task that ends, finished, failed or stopped, with a worktree still pending marks its branch preserved, and the daemon broadcasts `worktree_done` when the worktree differs from its baseline commit or, without one, from its merge base with the original branch (committed, staged, unstaged or untracked changes; a worktree git cannot read counts as changed). A verified clean one is discarded. The chat shows a bar with three buttons, sent as `worktreeAction`: "Auto-commit and merge" (`merge`: the same commit, squash and conflict resolver, the merge agent's cost added to the task's row), "Do nothing" (`nothing`: branch, directory and uncommitted changes stay as they are) and "Discard" (`discard`, after a confirmation: worktree and branch are deleted). The bar comes back when the session is reloaded, acts only while no task runs on the tab, and for a tab bound to a machine is forwarded to it (section 15). A new task on the tab, a new chat or closing the tab retires a pending worktree: its changes are committed to its `kiss/wt-*` branch, the directory is removed and the branch stays unmerged, named in a warning. After a daemon restart no bar is shown; the branch and directory remain for a manual merge. The CLI names the unmerged branch and directory on stderr; a library caller uses `agent.merge()`, `agent.discard()` or `agent.leave_as_is()`.
- **Git-ignored files**: a worktree's git-ignored files (`tmp/`, build output, a `.env`) are in no commit, and nothing copies them into the checkout: a merge, a retirement, a discard and the orphan reclaim remove them with the worktree directory.
- **Commit messages** (`commit_message.py`, 172 lines): one non-agentic call to the fast model (section 8) on the staged diff, in conventional-commit form, followed by a `User prompt:` and a `Result:` block under the same condition as on the squash commit; without a model or on a failed call the subject is `kiss: auto-commit agent work`. The diff is capped at 200,000 bytes: a larger one is sent as a truncation note with the `--shortstat` totals, the head of the `--stat` list and the head of the patch. Emptiness is tested with `git diff --cached --quiet`. The call's cost goes to the task.
- **What auto-commit means now**: no setting and no step after the task. The name survives in the commit `commit_and_merge` makes, in the bar's "Auto-commit and merge" button and in the wire names of the Git Commit button (`autocommitAction`, `autocommit_done`). Edits made outside a worktree are not committed when the task ends; the Git Commit button and `/sync` (both below) commit them on request.
- **Git Commit** (footer menu of the chat): stages everything (`git add -A`) in the folder the chat header shows for the tab, generates a message from the diff alone and commits. A tree that changed while the message was generated is not committed (the reply asks to press the button again), and one commit runs per tab at a time. When the header names a registered machine the command is forwarded and that machine commits its own folder **NEW** (10-18; 900 s timeout, section 15).
- **Source Control data** (`src/kiss/server/explorer.py`, 1,081 lines; the view is in section 16): `git_status` lists the changes of every worktree of the repository, main checkout first, and reads them with `git --no-optional-locks status` so the view's poll never takes `index.lock` from a running task **NEW** (10-30). The view's badge counts only the worktree the tab's task runs in **NEW** (10-20). A file under Changes opens as a side-by-side diff of `HEAD` against that worktree's file **CHANGED** (11-07), as a file of a commit in the graph does against the commit's parent (`git_file_diff`). The graph's context menu is the only writer (`git_action`: detached checkout, new branch, tag, cherry-pick, under the repository lock). For a tab bound to a machine `gitStatus`, `gitLog`, `gitShow` and `gitAction` are answered by that machine **NEW** (10-14, section 15).
- **Locks and timeouts**: worktree setup (with its reclaim and sweep), re-attachment after a failed merge, the merge into the checkout (from the stash to the branch deletion) and a discard each take a per-repository lock in the process and then a file lock on `kiss-reclaim.lock` in the git directory (`flock`; `msvcrt.locking` on Windows), so the daemon and a CLI run cannot interleave them; a wait of more than 300 s for another process ends in an error instead of a hang **NEW** (10-19). The steps of `commit_and_merge` before the merge are outside the file lock: the commit in the task's worktree takes neither lock and the removal of its directory only the in-process one. Each git call has a 300 s timeout and ignores inherited `GIT_DIR`-style variables. A tab with a merge, discard or disposal in flight refuses a new run ("Cannot run a task while a merge is in progress. Wait for it to finish first."), and a claim whose thread died is released at the next check.
- **Orphan recovery** **CHANGED**: there is no pass at daemon start any more; each `create_worktree` first runs `reclaim_orphaned_worktrees` and `sweep_orphaned_state` on its repository. The reclaim takes a `kiss/wt-*` worktree whose process died mid-task: it commits the changes (`kiss: reclaim orphan worktree`), squash-merges the branch into its recorded original branch and removes it, but only while the checkout is clean, not detached and on that branch; a failed merge keeps the worktree. It never touches a worktree stamped by another live process (pid plus start time and executable, so a recycled pid does not count), one of a live task in the same process, or one marked preserved; every worktree left unmerged at the end of a run gets that marker (a file under `kiss-preserve/` in the git directory since 10-13). The sweep deletes `kiss/wt-*` branches that have no worktree and no commits of their own, their config sections, stale markers and unregistered `kiss_wt-*` directories that hold no `.git` entry. A task that has removed its worktree directory and not yet merged keeps its branch: the sweep skips one that a live task of the same process holds or another live process stamped **NEW** (10-30). Task rows have their own recovery: the `owner` column of `task_history` holds the creating process's token, and at daemon start rows of dead owners that still carry `Agent Failed Abruptly` are rewritten to `Task terminated unexpectedly (process killed)` or restored from the result journal (section 14).
- **`/sync`** (section 9): commits the checkout's uncommitted changes, fetches and merges `origin/main` (or a named ref) and pushes, forcing nothing; with registered machines it syncs each one before and after (section 15).
- **Removed 10-09**: the worktree pool (`worktree_pool.py`, `prewarm_worktree_pool`, `KISS_DISABLE_WORKTREE_POOL`; the reclaim discards a spare left by an older release), the main-tree admission gate (a non-worktree task blocking a merge, "A worktree merge is in progress", deferred merges) and the `is_worktree` and `auto_commit_mode` columns of `task_history`, which a database created earlier keeps unwritten.
- Recorded since the two tools arrived on 10-09: 580 `create_worktree` calls in 525 tasks and 675 `commit_and_merge` calls in 502 tasks. The `is_worktree` and `auto_commit_mode` columns that earlier inventories counted are no longer written, and a database created today does not have them; on the development machine they stop at 3,532 worktree rows (the last on 10-09) and at 4,585 auto-commit tasks (the last on 10-10) among the 7,337 top-level tasks launched up to that one.

## 13. Persistent memory and repository knowledge

`kiss.core.memoryfield` (`pages.py`, `index.py`, `tools.py`, `evaluate.py`; 2,720 lines with `__init__.py`):

- **Pages**: a flat directory of Markdown files with YAML frontmatter (`title`, `uuid`, `summary`, `created`, `updated`), names `^[a-z0-9](?:[a-z0-9-]*[a-z0-9])?$`, an 8 KB guideline (larger pages are saved with a warning and only the first 8,192 bytes are embedded). A write keeps the stored `uuid` and `created`, refuses an empty body and publishes atomically; a delete writes `.tombstones/<name>` before it removes the page, so a sync cannot resurrect it. Writes and deletes of one memory are serialised across processes by the lock file `<memory>.lock` beside its directory (60 s wait, then the tool returns an error) **NEW** (10-18). Symlinked pages, index files and tombstone directories are refused; sync and editor debris (`.DS_Store`, `desktop.ini`, `Thumbs.db`, `*~`, `*.sync-conflict-*`) is ignored.
- **Index**: one SQLite file per embedder inside the memory directory (`text-embedding-3-small.sqlite3` when `OPENAI_API_KEY` is set, otherwise the offline 1,024-dimension `hashed-bow-v1.sqlite3`), schema v3 with a read-only fast path. The embedded text is title, summary and body, without `uuid` or timestamps. Git-style racy-clean stat keys (size and mtime, plus ctime and inode on POSIX; 3 s window) let a sync skip unchanged pages unread (racy files and `memory_refresh` verification still hash); embeddings are batched (256 inputs / 250,000 bytes per batch through `Model.get_embeddings`); rows are written by compare-and-swap on a per-row `revision`, and a batch that finds the index cleared and rebuilt since its snapshot is skipped. Search is a cached numpy float32 matrix scan with exact `math.sumprod` rescoring and a 64-entry query cache; pages whose cosine similarity is not above 0 are dropped; near-duplicate detection reports pairs at cosine 0.9 or more.
- **Tools**: `memory_search(query, k=5, memory)` (score, name, title and summary per hit), `memory_pull(query, k=3, memory)` (the matching pages whole within a budget of 24,000 characters per call: a first page over the budget is cut to it, a later page that does not fit ends the result; section 7), `memory_read`, `memory_write` (rejects bad names, warns on oversize pages and on ephemeral names such as `round-3`), `memory_list(memory, max_output_chars)` (cut at the tool-output budget with a count of the pages not shown; the budget defaults to 8,000 characters and its ceiling to 50,000, `KISS_TOOL_OUTPUT_DEFAULT_CHARS` and `KISS_TOOL_OUTPUT_MAX_CHARS` of section 6) **NEW** (10-25), `memory_delete`, `memory_refresh(stale_days=30, duplicate_threshold=0.9, memory)` (re-hashes every page; lists at most 20 near-duplicate pairs and 20 stale pages per memory). Each memory is synced and searched on its own worker thread; the task thread waits for them interruptibly and a cancelled sync starts no further embedding batch, so Stop returns at once **NEW** (10-30). The `memory` tool group holds all seven and the `review` profile keeps the four read-only ones (section 7).
- **Protocol and recall**: a run with memory gets `MEMORY_PROTOCOL` in its system prompt (four rules, plus a fifth that lists the domain memories) and, appended to its task, the block `# Memory pages relevant to this task`: the five best pages for the task text, one line each with name, date, title and summary (`MemoryTools.recall`; a task over 4,000 characters is searched by its first and last 2,000; an empty or failed search adds nothing) **NEW** (10-25). Rule 1 no longer demands a `memory_search` as the first turn **CHANGED** (10-25): the model reads the listed pages it needs, searches only for a sub-topic the list does not cover and, when a page updated in the last 30 days answers a factual question, re-verifies only the facts that change (prices, versions, dates) on at most three sites. The other rules are as before: record durable knowledge one topic per page under about 8 KB with sources; store no secrets, credentials or raw transcripts and keep per-round notes in `./tmp/PROGRESS.md`; call `memory_refresh` when results look redundant or outdated.
- **Location and domains**: the general memory is `~/.kiss/memories/`, or the directory named by the `memory_dir` key of `~/.kiss/config.json`. A run whose work directory is inside a git repository also gets that repository's domain memory `<memory root>/<slug of the main checkout's directory name>/` (`general` maps to `general-repo`), shared by its worktrees (`git rev-parse --git-common-dir`) and addressed as `<memory>/<page>`; searches span every memory unless `memory=` narrows them. Two repositories whose directories have the same name share one domain memory. On the development machine (pages created before 2026-11-10 UTC): 949 general pages, `kiss/` 1,805 pages, and 76 pages in 18 further domain memories.
- **Switches and gates**: "Use persistent memory" in the settings panel (config key `use_memory`, default on) yields to a non-empty `KISS_USE_MEMORY` (`0`, `false`, `no`, `off` disable; anything else enables), and both yield to a per-run `use_memory` (`SorcarAgent.run`, `daemon_client.run`, the `useMemory` wire field, a SEA's `use_memory` setting). Worker SEAs default to off; the `rsi7d`, `autorouter` and `orautorouter` SEAs turn it on, and `/recommendations` adds read-only `memory_search`, `memory_pull` and `memory_read` over the general memory alone while the switch is on **NEW** (10-12; section 9). Sub-agents inherit the parent's override unless `options.use_memory` is given, and a sandboxed task may not start a sub-task with it on (section 10). Four hard gates beat every switch **CHANGED**: no basic tools; a tool profile without a memory tool (since 10-26: `assistant`, `bash`, `none`, and any group combination without `memory`); a Docker, attached-container or exec-bridge run (`docker_image` set); a caller-supplied `system_instruction`. The `cc/`/`codex/` gate was removed 10-18, so those models get the memory tools like any other.
- **Repository knowledge** (`/git_extract_knowledge <path or clone URL>`, with `update <repo>` and `ask <question>` modes; section 9): two tiers in the repository's domain memory. The agent writes curated pages (`overview`, `domain-glossary`, `domain-concepts`, `architecture`, `conventions`, `history`, `module-<slug>`, `faq`, `recent-changes`) and a tool writes `knowledge-lookup`; the FTS5 block store `knowledge.sqlite3` holds kinds `repo, dir, file, chunk, symbol, commit, change, tag, branch, author, note` (`note` mirrors the curated pages). Indexing is deterministic and incremental: blob hashes, the set difference between `git rev-list --all` and the stored commits (every branch and tag; commits dropped by a rewritten history are deleted), children-first writes, chunks of at most 80 lines and 6,000 characters (a longer line is split into numbered parts), symbol patterns for 11 language families, patches in 8,000-character `change` blocks, one commit's diff cut at 64 MB. Binary files (a NUL byte in the first 8 KB), generated files (`.min.js`, `.min.css`, `.map`) and text files over 64 MB get a metadata-only `file` block, without `chunk` or `symbol` blocks. Search is BM25 with title 4x / key 2x and history rank factors 0.6 (`change`) and 0.8 (`commit`), an all-words pass then an any-word pass, at most 100 hits. Ten tools and a nine-command CLI (`python -m kiss.agents.seas.git_extract_knowledge.git_extract_knowledge_sea index|status|search|read|pages|read-page|write-page|delete-page|schedule`) serve the SEA, its fan-out sub-agents and any shell; the SEA itself runs with the `full` tool profile and no memory tools. Clone URLs are checked out under `~/.kiss/knowledge/checkouts/`; the cron job `git-knowledge daily update: <slug>` refreshes the memory at 04:00 America/Los_Angeles on the pinned `claude-fable-5-1` ($25 + $2 relay, 4 h timeout; section 20). The block store stays on its machine: neither sync below copies `*.sqlite3` files.
- **Standing instructions**: `~/.kiss/AGENTS.md` is not a memory page; it is appended to the system prompt of every `SorcarAgent` run except a `plain_io` one, and a caller-supplied `system_instruction` replaces the composed prompt, standing instructions included (section 6). It is edited by `/remember` and `/forget` (section 9) through `agents_md.py` (196 lines): one bullet per instruction under `# User instructions`, duplicates recognised regardless of case and spacing, hand-written text kept byte for byte, updates under `AGENTS.md.lock` with an atomic replace. The file was renamed from `SORCAR.md` **CHANGED** (10-03); one left under the old name is adopted on first use.
- **Memory sync on deploy**: `rsorcar` step 4c (section 21) runs `scripts/sync-memory.sh user@host` (268 lines, usable on its own): pages and tombstones travel both ways as tar over ssh, without the `*.sqlite3*` files, and `merge_memory_pages.py` (505 lines, standard library only, run on the remote by its system `python3`) merges them: the newest `updated` wins, mtime breaks a tie for hand edits, same-second conflicts keep each side's copy and are named on stdout, a tombstone removes an older page and yields to a newer one, replacements are atomic. Clock skew between the machines is measured first and widens `--tolerance`; each side's `memory_dir` is honoured; the merge holds the memory's mutation locks **CHANGED** (11-08). Step 4d syncs the autorouter's notes `AUTOROUTER.md` and `MODEL_DECISIONS.md` with `sync_kiss_notes.py` (sections 8 and 21).
- **Sync across machines** **NEW** (`/sync_history_and_memory`, 10-25; section 15): the memory half of the SEA compares SHA-1 fingerprints of pages and tombstones with every SSH machine, sends only the entries that differ as a gzip'd tar, and merges them by the same `merge_memory_pages` rules under the memory's locks, first from every peer into this machine and then from this machine into every peer. The tolerance is the spread of the clocks measured at connect; writes closer together than that are conflicts, kept on both sides and named. A peer's home is the directory whose endpoint file names the registered daemon's port, looked for under this install's brand directory, then `~/.kiss`, then every other `~/.*` (11-04), so a white-label install and a stock one sync with each other. When no endpoint file matches (a stopped daemon removes its file) or the port is unknown, the home is the first of those two directories that holds a `history.db`, else the first that exists, else the first. The helper scripts travel as text and run under the peer's system `python3`; a dry run only counts what would travel. It syncs `<home>/memories` on both sides and ignores `memory_dir`.
- **Recall evaluation**: `python -m kiss.core.memoryfield.evaluate` builds a memory from the most recent successful top-level tasks of `~/.kiss/history.db` (`--limit 300`, `--llm-probes 40`) and reports Recall@1/3/5 and MRR for vector, hashed, BM25 and hybrid (reciprocal rank fusion) retrievers; `src/kiss/scripts/memory_search_eval.py <memory dir>` scores a memory's own question set `eval/questions.yaml` (76 questions for the `kiss/` memory on the development machine) and exits 1 when Recall@5 is below `--min-recall5` or a gold page is missing. The public-benchmark harness `benchmarkings/memoryfield/bench_public.py` (LoCoMo, LongMemEval-S) left the repository with `benchmarkings/` (removed 09-29 by the author-local date of `b9ed868023`, 09-30 UTC and inside the window of section 2).

## 14. The kiss-web daemon and remote web app

`src/kiss/server/` (37 modules, 45,818 lines; `web_server.py` 11,812). One daemon per machine owns the agents, the tab registry, the cron scheduler (section 20), the SEA slash-command watcher (section 9), the browser and terminal tab services, the file index and the daemon side of voice (section 18); the spare-worktree pool it used to prewarm was removed 10-09 (section 12). Entry points: `kiss-web` (`--url`, `--trust-ca`, `--workdir`) and `kiss-app`. The page the daemon serves is section 16; one page over the daemons of several machines is section 15.

- **Transports** **CHANGED**: one port (8787, every interface) carries the page, its media and the WebSocket `/ws`. The first byte of a connection picks the protocol: a TLS handshake gets TLS, anything else is plain HTTP, served to loopback peers only (a 400 for everyone else); loopback has been plain HTTP since 10-15, where TLS used to be mandatory. The Unix socket `~/.kiss/sorcar.sock` was removed 10-01: local clients (among them the Python client API of section 5, `run_agent` sub-tasks and cron prompt jobs, which all go through `daemon_client.run`) read `~/.kiss/sorcar-local.json` (mode 0600; `url`, `token`, `ca`, `pid`; a new 256-bit token at every daemon start) and authenticate with the token (`agents/sorcar/local_endpoint.py`). A frame over 65,536 characters goes out as `part` frames to a client that declared `parts`, each later part held until the bytes that client has not yet taken are down to 65,536 (local clients are not held) **NEW**; a peer that reads nothing for 30 s is dropped. Every 15 s the daemon pings each client, closes one that stays silent for 10 s and follows a successful ping with a `heartbeat` frame for the client's half-open detector.
- **Authentication** **CHANGED**: three kinds of client (`AuthKind`): `local`, the endpoint token from a loopback address or a registered machine's link with an enrolled client certificate (section 15); `loopback` **NEW**, a page opened at a loopback address (`http://127.0.0.1:8787` or `localhost`) and reaching the daemon directly, admitted without a password; `remote`, the `remote_password` of `~/.kiss/config.json`. `install.sh` generates a 20-character password when none is stored and keeps it across reinstalls **NEW** (`python -m kiss.core.remote_password --show` or `--rotate`); it is stored as typed and compared in constant time. Every frame of a `remote` client carries the password and is checked against the stored one, so a changed password shuts out the other sessions at their next frame **NEW**. With no password stored the daemon serves only loopback peers and machine links with an enrolled client certificate: other peers get a 403 and no tunnel is started. Five wrong passwords from one address within 60 s lock that address out (`auth_locked` with `retry_after`).
- **Command catalogue**: `sorcar.py` `API` lists 88 commands (41 forwarded unchanged to the backend, 47 with their own `ServerApi` handler); `validate_command` refuses an unknown name or a missing required field before `ServerApi.dispatch` routes the command. The page's whitelist (`media/api.js` `SORCAR_API_COMMANDS`) holds 82 of them; the other six are `auth` and `ping` (sent by the page's transport itself), `run` (local clients), `activeTasksQuery` (the installer), `browserList` and `getTaskUsage` (another machine's daemon, section 15). Since 09-30 the catalogue gained 25 commands and lost 21, among them `voiceWakeStart`/`voiceWakeStop`, `getFrequentTasks` and `generateCommitMessage`.
- **Tab registry and replay**: `TabRegistry` persists the top-level chat tabs atomically to `~/.kiss/tabs.json` (cap 512) and broadcasts a `tabs_state` snapshot after every change, which every client mirrors. A chat id is bound to at most one tab (the newest bind displaces the older). Sub-agent tabs are not registered: they open on `openSubagentTab` and close on `subagentDone`; file, directory and terminal tabs stay local to the client. Events are recorded and persisted per task and fanned out only to the tabs subscribed to that task (`json_printer.py`), so one chat's events never reach another chat's view. `ready` answers the connecting client only (registry, models, input history, configuration, SEA commands, browser tabs, tips, promptlets). Since 10-12 it replays the transcript of the one tab that client shows and marks every other bound tab `replay_deferred`, fetched by a scoped `resumeSession` when the tab is opened **NEW**. A replay is bounded (the text of a `thinking_delta`, `text_delta` or `system_output` event, when longer than 200,000 characters, cut to its first and last 100,000 joined by a note counting the omitted ones; a 16 MiB budget for the serialised events, past which the oldest are left out with a note while the newest is always kept), and embedded pictures travel as handles the page fetches with `getReplayImage` **NEW**. Replay snapshots are taken under the delivery lock with a reserved send slot, so no event is duplicated or lost.
- **Browser tab**: `BrowserTabService` launches the daemon machine's browser with a persistent profile (`~/.kiss/browser-tab-profile`), streams each page as a `browser__*` tab (1280×800, JPEG quality 60, at most 15 frames/s, 90 s open timeout) and relays clicks, keys and viewport changes (`browserOpen`, `browserClose`, `browserNavigate`, `browserInput`, `browserViewport`). Per-viewer flow control (`frame_flow.py`, 10-31) keeps at most 2 frames unacknowledged per viewer (`browserFrameAck`, 5 s grace) and holds only the newest meanwhile, so a slow link shows a late page instead of losing the tab **NEW**. When the browser cannot start, Playwright's bundled Chromium is used instead (11-03) **NEW**. Agents receive the service as `live_browser`: `show_browser()` moves the agent's headless session into such a tab, and third-party sign-in pages open there through `browser_handoff.set_browser_tab_opener` (section 17). For a chat bound to a registered machine the page, or the shell, opens on that machine (section 15).
- **Terminal tab** **NEW** (10-05): `terminal_tab.py` runs the user's shell (`$SHELL`, else `bash`, else `/bin/sh`) in one pty per tab on the daemon's machine: commands `terminalOpen`, `terminalInput`, `terminalResize`, `terminalClose`; events `terminalOpened`, `terminalData`, `terminalExit`, `terminalError`. A shell outlives a dropped connection by 60 s, so the tab re-attaches after a reconnect. `terminal_rc.py` (10-09) starts bash and zsh with start-up files under `~/.kiss/terminal/` that load the user's own and append each command to the history file at once, so terminal tabs share history. Windows has no pty: the tab reports an error.
- **Sidebar data**: `getCronJobs` with `cronAction` **NEW** (Schedule: `create`, `update`, `run_now`, `pause`, `resume`, `remove`; section 20), `getAppsStatus` (Apps; every channel module probed in one subprocess, cached 30 s), `getSpendReport` (Spend; total, per day, per model from persisted `cost`/`tokens`, sub-agent rows excluded), `getRecommendations` **NEW** (ten suggested tasks from the last `/recommendations` run, a default 6-hourly cron job), `getMachines` with `machineAction` **NEW** (section 15), `getHistory` (Chats, 50 rows per page). `getTaskUpdate` **CHANGED** 10-03: Task update is the `/ask` agent's answer to "What has this task done so far, and what are its partial results?", first asked when the task is 60 s old and again once the answer is 600 s old or on Refresh, capped at $1 and run as a side-channel sub-agent whose spend is charged to the task; only a poll starts a run. The `/task_update` SEA remains as a chat command.
- **Files**: `openFile` answers the requesting connection with `fileContent`: text up to 2,000,000 bytes, PDFs and images up to 24 MiB as base64, a directory as a text listing; an HTML page above the text cap opens read-only instead of being refused (10-26) **NEW**. `saveFile` replaces a file atomically and refuses with `conflict` when it changed on disk since it was opened, unless `force`. Explorer and Source Control are served by `explorer.py` (`listDir` capped at 2,000 entries, `gitStatus`, `gitLog`, `gitShow`, `gitAction`) and `fs_actions.py` (`fsAction`: `newFile`, `newFolder`, `rename`, `delete`, `copy`, `move`, `findInFolder`, `compare`).
- **TLS**: connections from other machines use a certificate issued by a machine-local CA (`kiss/core/tls_certs.py`, moved out of `server/`; files in `~/.kiss/tls/`): an ECDSA P-256 root valid 3,650 days and a server certificate valid 820 days naming `localhost`, the host name and the current loopback and LAN addresses. It is re-issued when missing, within 30 days of expiry, not signed by the current CA or lacking a current LAN address; the watchdog checks every 15 s and loads the new one without dropping connections. `kiss-web --trust-ca` installs the CA into NSS databases, the macOS login keychain or the Windows user store; other devices fetch it from `GET /ca.crt`. The same directory holds the client certificate and `trusted-clients/` of machine links **NEW** (section 15).
- **File index**: a persistent home-wide index (`~/.kiss/file-index`; depth 12; 1,000,000-entry cap, directories included; dot-directories, `node_modules`, `__pycache__`, `venv`, `site-packages` and the literal, non-negated entries of every `.gitignore` on the way down skipped; bulk data directories collapsed) is warmed at startup and serves the `@` picker with previously mentioned paths first, then `./` matches from the work dir, then `~/` matches from the rest of home (20 suggestions, up to 5 places kept for home), refreshed by directory mtime once 60 s stale. A query starting with `./` searches the work dir only, one starting with `~` the whole home directory only. Since 10-31 the contents of a directory the OS refuses to list, or whose `.gitignore` cannot be read within 5 s, are left out until the daemon restarts instead of blocking the picker, the home index skips `~/Library` on macOS, and after a restart the index is published from the persisted listings before the rescan **NEW**.
- **Prompt shortcuts**: a single-line prompt that is only the path of an existing regular file, typed in a tab with no running task and no pending question, opens the file (`promptOpened`, then `fileContent`) instead of starting a task; a directory name still starts one. The daemon also serves the Inject promptlets (`~/.kiss/MY_INJECTION.md`, then the bundled `INJECTIONS.md`; `addTrick`, `editTrick`, `deleteTrick`), ghost-text completion (`complete`), the 22 tips of `TIPS.md` (`tipsOptOut` writes `~/.kiss/TIPS_DISABLED`) and an input history of 500 entries (section 5).
- **Branding and brand services**: `kiss/core/brand.py` reads `webapp/media/brand.json` (`product_name`, `short_name`, `tagline`, `identity`, and `home_dir` **NEW** 10-03: the state directory under `$HOME`, `.kiss` for the stock brand); missing keys fall back one by one. `render_brand` fills the double-braced placeholders `PRODUCT_NAME`, `SHORT_NAME`, `TAGLINE`, `IDENTITY`, `HOME_DIR` and `VERSION` in the prompt files and tips; the page template receives `brand.css`, `PRODUCT_NAME`, `TAGLINE`, `HOME_DIR`, `BRAND_JSON` and a title naming the machine, and `/manifest.webmanifest` **NEW** names the installable app after the brand. `install.sh` overlays a git-ignored `.brand/` directory onto the installed media for white-label builds (section 21). Two hooks extend the daemon without code changes: executables in `~/.kiss/services.d/` are started once the local endpoint exists and restarted with a back-off of 1 s doubling to 60 s (`services.py`, 10-29) **NEW**; an executable `~/.kiss/signin` makes every page show a sign-in window, one button per provider, until it reports the user signed in (`signin.py`, 10-20; `signinLogin`, `signinPaste`, `signinCancel`) **NEW**. Without the hook, or with a broken one, nothing is shown.
- **HTTP** **CHANGED**: since 10-29 `http_speed.py` gzips text responses of 1,024 bytes or more (cached by content hash up to 64 MiB) and marks `/media/<file>?v=<hash>` as `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable`; everything else stays `no-store`. The service worker (`media/sw.js`, cache `kiss-shell-<version>`) precaches the shell, serves `/media/*` cache-first and `/` network-first, falling back after 4 s to the cached shell marked offline; `/ws`, `/api/*`, `/trajectories` and the voice model are never intercepted. `GET /api/daemon` **NEW** returns the daemon's instance id and version, and a page that reconnects to a different instance reloads itself (10-23). `GET /webclip.mobileconfig` **NEW** (11-03) serves an iOS Web Clip profile, signed through the local CA, that puts the web app on the home screen. The daemon also serves the trajectory visualiser (`/trajectories`, `kiss/viz_trajectory`); its data routes `/api/jobs` and `/api/jobs/<job>/trajectories` now ask anyone but a direct loopback peer or an enrolled machine link for the remote password as an HTTP Basic credential **NEW**.
- **Remote reach**: an optional `cloudflared` tunnel publishes the daemon: a quick tunnel with a random `*.trycloudflare.com` URL, or a named tunnel with a fixed URL when `CLOUDFLARE_TUNNEL_TOKEN` (or `tunnel_token` in `config.json`) is set. The active URL is written to `~/.kiss/remote-url.json` (`kiss-web --url` prints it), broadcast as `remote_url` and, while a tunnel is in use, posted to the machine's `ntfy.sh` topic with the machine name appended. A tunnel the daemon lost track of is found and adopted again, which keeps the URL stable across restarts (10-03) **NEW**.
- **Restart and update handling**: the daemon polls PyPI hourly and broadcasts `update_available`. `runUpdate` runs the bootstrap `~/.kiss/kiss_ai/scripts/install.sh`, which syncs the clone and hands over to its `install.sh` (section 21), detached (log `~/.kiss/update.log`), refuses new task submits while the installer runs and reports a failed installer to the window that asked; `updateWhenIdle` waits until no task runs, `snoozeUpdate` hides the offer for 24 h, `updateModels` refreshes `~/.kiss/MODEL_INFO.json` (section 8). `serverReset` (Settings, Reset Server) sends the daemon SIGTERM so that the LaunchAgent or systemd unit respawns it on the same port and tunnel. `install.sh` never restarts a daemon with a task in flight: it leaves `~/.kiss/.kiss-web.restart-pending`, and the daemon runs `install.sh --restart-only --non-interactive` itself once idle (`deferred_restart.py`, 10-30) **NEW**. Sticky notifications report an install that finished with problems (`~/.kiss/.install-problems`, shown for 7 days) **NEW** and the retirement of the VS Code extension (removed 10-14; `dismissNotice` hides it) **NEW**.
- **Desktop app window** **NEW** (10-16): `kiss-app` (`standalone_app.py`) opens the web app in a Chromium-family browser in app mode under its own profile (`~/.kiss/webapp-profile`); `--install-launcher` registers a `.desktop` entry on Linux or an application bundle in `~/Applications` on macOS with the brand's name and icon (section 21). The daemon counts app-window connections (`/ws?appWindow=1`, `GET /api/app-window`) and on macOS quits a browser left without a window.
- **Resilience**: a watchdog runs every 15 s: it restarts a dead tunnel, rewrites a deleted `remote-url.json`, reacts to changed network addresses, re-binds the macOS loopback alias, pings every WebSocket, refreshes the TLS certificate and, after the host slept, drops the dead links to registered machines. A stall watchdog dumps every thread's stack to stderr after 60 s of a blocked GIL (`faulthandler`, disarmed at shutdown). SIGTERM stops in-flight tasks before the event loop unwinds. Limits: prompts of 1,000,000 bytes, 32 attachments, 64 MiB per frame, 64 executor threads.
- **Chat export** **CHANGED**: the chat menu's Share chat became Save chat on 10-19 (section 16). `shareChat` writes a self-contained page (`main.css`, `brand.css`, both highlight themes and `share.js` inlined) to `reports/chat-<title-slug>-<chat id>.html` under the tab's work dir and answers `share_done`. The page's last line records the version and git commit of the daemon that wrote it, `unknown` for one it cannot determine **NEW** (11-12). `shareChatTasks` supplies every task of the chat, oldest first, with its sub-agents nested and its metrics (tokens, cost, steps, start and end time), within a 24 MiB reply budget that drops the oldest tasks first and flags `truncated`. `share.js` gives the saved page collapsible event panels, nested `run_parallel` groups, a sub-agent tab bar and a light/dark theme.

## 15. Machines: one web app over several computers

**NEW**: the whole section is new since 09-30; registered machines first appeared on 10-10.

`src/kiss/agents/sorcar/machines.py` (2,301 lines: registry, certificate pins, SSH enrolment and forwards, `MachineLink`, merged history), `src/kiss/server/remote_tabs.py` (1,335), `remote_views.py` (824), `machine_stream.py` (148) and the forwarders of `sidebar_panels.py` (950), with four SEA folders under `src/kiss/agents/seas/`: `machine/` (146), `sync/` (824), `install/` (203) and `sync_history_and_memory/` (1,238). A daemon keeps a registry of other computers' daemons and drives them from its own web app: their chats, files, settings, terminals and browser pages appear on the local surfaces, and a registered machine's own web UI is never opened.

- **Registry**: `~/.kiss/machines.json` (mode 0600) holds one entry per machine with `name`, `url`, `password` and `ssh`; a name matches `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,62}$` and defaults to the address's host. `/machine add [name] <address> [password]`, `/machine remove <name>` and `/machine list` edit it (a $1 worker whose only tools are `machine_add`, `machine_remove` and `machine_list`; section 9), and so do the **+** form and the delete button of the Machines subpanel (`machineAction`). `add_machine` stages the certificate pin, completes one authenticated handshake and only then publishes the pin and the entry, so a wrong address or password changes nothing. The daemon reads the file's size and mtime every 2 s (`MACHINES_REGISTRY_POLL_SECONDS`): a change retires the links and ssh forwards of the machines whose entry changed and pushes a fresh `machines` list to every connection.
- **URL machines**: `https://host:port` (or `host[:port]`; the port defaults to 8787) is registered with that daemon's remote password. Registration pins the remote's local CA (`GET /ca.crt`), or its leaf certificate when it serves none, as `~/.kiss/machines/<name>.pem` (`pin_certificate`); every later connection verifies the chain against that file alone, with host-name verification off because a daemon's generated certificate names its LAN addresses.
- **SSH machines**: `user@host[:port]` (or `ssh://[user@]host[:port]`) needs no password. One `ssh` login (`ENROLL_COMMAND`) finds the home directory of the daemon running there, copies this daemon's CA into its `tls/trusted-clients/` and prints back the daemon's port and CA, which is pinned. Every later connection goes through one `ssh -N -L` forward per login (`SshTunnel`, reopened when it dies) to `127.0.0.1:<port>` on the machine and presents the client certificate `~/.kiss/tls/client.pem`, which the remote admits like its own local token whatever its remote password; its daemon port need not be open to the network. The login must work non-interactively (`BatchMode=yes`), allow TCP forwarding and land in a POSIX shell; an unknown host key is accepted on first use; `~/.ssh/config` applies, or the file `$KISS_SSH_CONFIG` names. Revoking a machine means removing its ssh access there.
- **Links**: `MachineLink` is a persistent authenticated WebSocket per machine and lane, dialled on first use and again after a drop. A link answers one request at a time, so slow traffic has lanes of its own beside the default one: `panels` (sidebar polls, Task update, pictures, share), `actions` (Git and file actions), `commit`, `login` and `status`. A request's reply times out after 8 s unless its forwarder allows more (`REQUEST_TIMEOUT`), and each `part` frame or `more` piece of a long reply restarts the wait; waiting for the link's turn (the same time again at most), a dial's handshake (8 s at most) and the opening of an ssh forward (30 s, `SSH_TIMEOUT`) have budgets of their own. Sockets negotiate permessage-deflate, receive a frame above 64 KiB in `part` frames and are pinged every 20 s. After a failure the link reports the machine down without dialling for 5 s, doubling to 30 s (`FIRST_BACKOFF`, `DOWN_BACKOFF`); a rejected password waits the full 30 s so the remote's brute-force lockout is not tripped. `forget_after_sleep` drops the cached sockets and ssh forwards once the host has slept 30 s or more (`SLEEP_GAP`). Each request carries a fresh `generation`: a reply that echoes one must echo this one, and the five replies of `CORRELATED_REPLIES` (`models`, `history`, `myModelsData`, `machines`, `historyDeleted`) must echo it when the remote's `auth_ok` carries `echoesGeneration`, so a list the remote pushed for another client's edit is not taken for the answer.
- **Chat header drop-downs**: every chat header has a machine drop-down and, next to it, a working-directory drop-down. The first lists this machine and every reachable registered machine, labelled by the host name its daemon reports when it was registered under the default name; a remote pick shows green, and red once a `machines` reply has shown it down. The pick for a new chat is one global setting (`setMachine` stores `last_machine` in `config.json` and broadcasts `machineChanged`), so the surfaces agree; a tab that shows a chat keeps that chat's machine. The drop-down stays enabled while the chat runs: the running task is not moved, the pick is where the next prompt goes. The second lists the picked machine's directories (`getMachineWorkDirs` returns its `workDir`, `recentWorkDirs` and `maxBudget`), and a pick there sends `setWorkDir` with `machine`, which that machine's daemon adopts.
- **Bound tabs** (`remote_tabs.py`): a `submit`, or the API's `run`, that names a machine binds its tab to it. `RemoteTabs` keeps one authenticated stream per bound tab and forwards `submit`, `run`, `stop`, `userAnswer`, `appendUserMessage`, `interruptTool` and `worktreeAction` unchanged under the same `tabId`, so the remote daemon runs, queues and stops the tab's tasks as for a page of its own; a forwarded `submit` drops the local `workDir`, so the task runs in the machine's working directory. Every frame the remote stamps with the tab id is handed to the local clients as it is; the task, its events and its history row live on the remote only. `tabMachine {tabId, machine}` tells every surface the binding. The stream also relays the task's sub-agent tabs (`new_tab`, `subagentDone`), replays the chat to a surface that connects or reloads, and opens the file of a path-only prompt from the machine. A machine's chat shows in one tab (a resume into a second tab closes the first on every surface); `closeTab` is forwarded while the task keeps running there; a lost socket ends the tab's running state with an `error` naming the machine.
- **Terminal and Browser** (`remote_views.py`): in a chat bound to a machine the Terminal and Browser buttons open a shell, or a page of the browser, on that machine. One `RemoteViewStream` per machine forwards the four terminal and six browser commands and relays the events stamped `machine`, with browser tab ids namespaced `browser__N@machine`. The stream stays open while a tab is bound to the machine, so a page the machine's agent opens for the user (`show_browser`, a sign-in hand-off; section 17) reaches the local surfaces; a stream lost meanwhile is reopened after 3 s (`REOPEN_DELAY`), and one that opens lists the pages the machine's browser already shows. Every browser frame is acknowledged (`browserFrameAck`), so at most two frames of each page are in flight on the stream, and to each local connection watching the page. A lost stream ends its terminals with a `terminalError` naming the machine and closes its browser tabs on every surface.
- **Forwarded requests**: 35 commands are answered by the machine's daemon when they carry `machine` (`MACHINE_FORWARDERS`), and the page stamps them with the machine the active tab's header shows, so the sidebar, the model picker and the Settings sheet follow the chat in view. They are the Explorer, Source Control and editor (`listDir`, `gitStatus`, `gitLog`, `gitShow`, `gitAction`, `fsAction`, `openFile`, `saveFile`, `checkPaths`), the Settings sheet (`getConfig`, `saveConfig`, `getMyModels`, `saveMyModel`, `deleteMyModel`, `getClaudeCodeStatus`, `getCodexStatus`, `startCodexLogin`), the model picker (`getModels`, `selectModel`), the `@` picker (`getFiles`, `recordFileUsage`), the Schedule, Machines, Apps, Spend and Recommendations subpanels (seven commands), Task update (`getTaskUpdate`), a replayed transcript's pictures (`getReplayImage`), chat sharing (`shareChatTasks`) and the four CHATS writes below. The Explorer of a bound chat shows the machine's working directory alone and hides **Add Folder to Explorer...**, and a file opened from it saves back to the machine; a remote machine's Settings sheet is headed `Settings of <label>` and hides this daemon's URLs and the **Update**, **Reset Server** and **Update Models** buttons; **Git Commit** runs in the machine's folder (`machine_autocommit`, waited for up to 900 s).
- **Machines subpanel**: `getMachines` returns `machines_report`: this machine first, then every registered one pinged in parallel over its link, each row with `name`, `label`, `address`, `ip`, `port`, `local`, `status` and `error`. The report is cached for 60 s, or for 10 s while any machine is unreachable. A row shows a status dot, the label, the address and its reachability, a copy-address button and a delete button with inline confirmation (disabled on the local row); the header's **+** opens the add form (optional name, address, password for a URL machine). A machine registered elsewhere, by `/machine add` or on another surface, unfolds the subpanel on every surface.
- **Merged CHATS**: `getHistory` runs `merged_history`: every registered machine is asked for its rows from the first to the end of the requested page of 50 rows, 50 per request (same query and tag, its own rows only, `machine: "__local__"`, so two daemons registered with each other do not echo each other), and the union is ranked with one copy per task id, running tasks first, then newest first, and cut to that page. The merge waits 2 s (`HISTORY_FANOUT_WAIT`); a slower machine contributes the page it last answered with, and a `tasks_updated` follows when the rows it then brings differ. Remote rows carry a machine chip, and a machine filter (all, this machine, each registered machine) appears once a machine is registered. A click resumes the chat in a local tab bound to the machine. The favourite star, title edit and delete buttons of a remote row act on that machine's `history.db` (`setFavorite`, `setChatSummary`, `deleteTask`, `deleteChat`). A task that a history sync copied in is listed under the machine it ran on: `task_history.machine` holds that machine's short host name, matched to a registered machine through `~/.kiss/machine_hosts.json`.
- **Agents across machines**: `run_agent(machine=<name or host>)` runs the sub-task on that machine's daemon with its tools, files and models (section 10): `work_dir` is a path there, paths in the task are not rewritten, a command name, or the path of a SEA registered as a command here, runs the machine's copy of that SEA (`remote_sea`), the plain sub-agent (an empty or generic `agent`, or the path of the hidden `sorcar` SEA) is sent as a run without a SEA, and any other `.py` path must exist at the same path there. Naming this machine itself is an ordinary local call. The sub-task inherits the model, budget share and prompt suffixes but not the caller's chat, container or extra tools; its tab opens under the caller's on every surface; a sandboxed caller is refused. `run_parallel` takes no `machine`. The Python API's `run(machine=)` forwards a top-level run the same way (`daemon_client.py`). `Bash(background=True, machine=<SSH machine>)` runs a command there through `ssh` with its output streamed into the local job log (section 7); a URL machine is refused. The spend of a remote sub-task is folded into its local parent and recorded in `remote_subtasks`; `sync_remote_subtask_usage` later asks the machine (`getTaskUsage`, 500 task ids per request) for spend charged after the fold, for runs of the last 7 days, with a sweep of every recorded run at most hourly.
- **Keeping machines in step** (SEAs, section 9): `/sync` commits, merges `origin/main` and pushes on every registered machine one at a time (`run_agent(agent="sync", machine=...)` on that machine's checkout, found by the repository's URL), then here, then on every machine again, with a budget of $2 × (1 + 2 × machines); `/sync this machine only` skips the machines. `/sync_history_and_memory` brings `history.db` and `memories/` of this machine and every SSH machine (or only those its message names) to the same state in three waves (probe, pull, push) over one multiplexed ssh connection per machine: history travels as the rows the other side lacks (task rows matched by `id`, events by `(task_id, seq)`), memory pages by newest `updated` stamp with tombstones (section 13), tasks still running are held back until they finish, and URL machines are reported as skipped.
