Deletions travel as well: the tombstones of `deleted_tasks` and `deleted_chats` (section 11) make the receiving database drop the deleted tasks' rows and events (a task still running there keeps its row and events until a later sync, and a tombstone older than the task row does not apply) and the summary row of a deleted chat left without tasks; section 21 has the other merge rules. Two writes of a page no further apart than the spread of the machines' clocks, measured in the first wave, are not ordered (pages with equal stamps are compared by file modification time, under the same rule): each side keeps its own copy and the page is reported as a conflict. A message that asks for a dry run or a preview changes no history or memory and reports what would travel between each machine and this one as it stands. `/install` runs `./install.sh --non-interactive` on every SSH machine concurrently, or on the machines it names, in the clone of the private development repository it finds in the login's home by its `origin` URL. `./rsorcar user@ip-address` deploys a checkout to a host and syncs history and memory with it without registering it (section 21).
- **A brand's hub**: a white-label brand can let any device reach a daemon's web app through its own hub, without a tunnel. For its relay the daemon runs every executable file in `~/.kiss/services.d/`, dot files excepted, for its own lifetime, restarting one that exits after 1 s, doubling to 60 s while it keeps failing (`services.py`; section 14), and each task's `task_settings` event records the `host` it ran on, so a hub sync sends only the tasks of the sending machine.

## 16. Chat client features

One page serves every client: `chat.html` (837 lines, 41 template placeholders), `main.js` (32,016 lines), `main.css` (5,280 lines) and `remote-codex.css` (1,061 lines) in `src/kiss/webapp/media/`, with one script per feature next to them (`voice.js` 1,379 lines, `transport.js` 1,332, `contentContextMenu.js` 865, `pdfView.js` 774, `share.js` 703, `panelCopy.js` 654, `tips.js` 628, `browserTab.js` 552, `terminalTab.js` 412, `mobileSwipe.js` 211). The VS Code extension was removed on 10-14, so a browser tab, the phone's home-screen icon and the `kiss-app` window (section 21) all show this page. What the daemon does behind it is section 14, other computers are section 15, and the page's 407 `*.test.js` files in `src/kiss/webapp/test/` are section 22.

- **Layout and tabs**: at 900 px or wider the CHATS sidebar is docked on the left of the chat and the task-info sidebar on its right. **NEW** (10-08): chat and content are split panes. The content pane appears on the right of the chat while a file, browser or terminal tab is open and has its own tab row; the task-info sidebar then has no column of its own: it slides off the window's right edge when a tab is shown in the pane or the pane is pressed, and a drawer tab on that edge brings it back on top of the pane. The divider drags between 20% and 80% of the width (50% at first; arrow keys move it, a double-click resets it), and the width and a folded content pane are remembered in the browser (`kiss-chat-pane-pct`, `kiss-content-pane-folded`). There is no row of chat tabs: a chat is opened from CHATS, and a tab strip shows above the chat only when the chat has sub-agent tabs to switch to; on a phone the file screen has the strip to itself: it lists the open file, browser and terminal tabs and nothing else, and shows for a single one too (11-12). A tab carries a spinner while its task runs, then a green tick or a red cross, a `?` badge while the agent waits for an answer, a dot for unsaved edits and a close button; its menu offers Close, Close Others, Close All and Close Inactive. A sub-agent's tab closes when the sub-agent finishes.
- **Chat header** **NEW** (10-11, 10-12): two drop-downs above the transcript, machine and working directory. The machine list is this computer and the registered machines (section 15); the pick is one setting shared by every client (10-19) and decides where the next task, the Terminal and Browser tabs and Git Commit run. The directory list holds the directories selected as the working directory on that machine so far, most recent first (`recent_work_dirs` of `~/.kiss/config.json`, up to 30, plus this computer's current directory; the folders that earlier tasks ran in are not offered), then `Choose ...`, which opens an in-page folder picker on the picked machine; `No working directory` shows until one is chosen.
- **Transcript**: a task starts with a Task panel that scrolls with the thread **CHANGED** (10-07). Reasoning streams into a Thinking panel (called Thoughts until 10-08), and text renders as sanitised Markdown with highlighted code. Each tool call is a panel named after the tool; it starts folded to a one-line preview (the description of a Bash, `run_agent`, `run_parallel` or `run_commands_parallel` call, the path of a Read or Write) **CHANGED** (10-08; before, it started open) and has a Stop button that interrupts that one call, except that a Question and a `run_agent` or `run_parallel` panel start open and a `finish` panel has no Stop button. Bash output streams into the panel and a failed result is marked Failed. **NEW** (10-08): the panel of the call in flight pulses and counts the elapsed time. While a task runs a sweep folds every panel older than the two newest (`STREAM_OPEN_PANELS`), except a panel the user opened and a `run_agent` or `run_parallel` panel whose sub-agent tabs are open; it never folds the Task panel, the Result, Thinking, questions, user messages, `/ask` answers and panels with pictures. A `summary` call works differently: it gathers the panels since the previous summary (or the Task panel) under one folded digest, Thinking included, and leaves questions, user messages, `/ask` answers and other panels with pictures outside it. Both rules are **CHANGED** (10-06, 10-07; before, the sweep also folded Thinking and answered questions, and a digest took in questions and pictures). **NEW** (10-07): when the task ends, its event panels fold into a single Trajectory panel ("N events") between the task and the result. The Result panel shows the summary as sanitised HTML with a `Status: FAILED` or `Status: Continue` line when it applies; tokens and cost left its header on 10-12 **CHANGED** and are read in Task Info. Prompt and System Prompt panels start folded, and panels carry a copy button and a timestamp. File paths in the text are links that open the file in the content pane, resolved on the chat's machine. **NEW**: a web link opens in a new browser tab, or in the default browser when the page runs in the app window (10-20), and image paths in results and answers load inline (10-24). Scrolling up stops the auto-follow and a "New output ↓" button returns to the end; scrolling past either end loads the neighbouring task of the chat; an opened chat lands at its end when it is running and on its task panel otherwise **CHANGED** (10-27). A "Suggested next" bar puts the agent's follow-up prompt into the composer.
- **Questions**: an `ask_user_question` call is a Question panel. Until it is answered its header reads "(answer in the chat box below)", the composer is the answer box ("Type your answer and press Enter") with the half-typed prompt parked and restored afterwards, the page switches to the asking tab and the tab shows the `?` badge. There is no toast and no system notification **CHANGED** (10-06). An answer being typed survives a page reload.
- **Composer**: Enter sends, Shift+Enter breaks the line, Up and Down walk the prompt history, starting from an empty box, and Tab accepts the grey ghost completion, which is requested 300 ms after typing stops (two or more characters, cursor at the end); on a touch screen a right swipe accepts it and vertical swipes walk the history. Typing also opens a list grouped History, Frequent, Suggestions (the promptlets) and From editor. `@` opens the file picker (Frequent, Files, Home), answered by the chat's machine **NEW** (10-29). `/` lists the slash commands of section 9 for a `/word` at the start of the text or after any whitespace, ranked **NEW** (10-18): Recent (the 3 run last, `_RECENT_COMMANDS`), Frequent (others run at least twice, `_FREQUENT_COMMAND_MIN_RUNS`), then the rest under Commands. While one of these three lists is open the keys go to it first: its first row starts selected, Up and Down move the selection, Tab accepts the selected row (the first when Up has cleared the selection), Escape closes the list, and Enter inserts the selected file of the `@` picker but otherwise closes the list and sends the text as typed. Images, HEIC and HEIF included, and PDFs attach through Attach files, a paste or a drop on the composer. The browser re-encodes a picture as a JPEG of at most 1,568 px on its longer edge when it is HEIC or HEIF, of a type other than JPEG, PNG, WebP or GIF, or a JPEG, PNG or WebP larger than 1,500 KiB; a GIF is sent as it is, and a picture the browser cannot decode (HEIC anywhere but Safari 17 or later, by the code's note) is dropped with an error chip. While a task runs, a sent message goes to the running agent as a steering message, with its attachments since 10-20 **CHANGED**; a prompt the daemon refuses comes back into the composer. A Stop agent button sits next to Send for as long as the task runs: it stops the whole task, where a tool panel's Stop interrupts one call, and once pressed it pulses until the agent has stopped. A "Listening ..." overlay covers the box during voice capture (section 18).
- **Promptlets and tips**: the Inject button opens a sheet with a search box, a box that adds a promptlet to `~/.kiss/MY_INJECTION.md`, and the list, the user's own entries first and then the 2 bundled in `src/kiss/INJECTIONS.md`. A click inserts the text at the cursor; every row has a copy button, and the user's rows an in-place editor and a two-step delete. The Tips popup (22 tips in `src/kiss/TIPS.md`; Previous and Next, arrow keys, Copy buttons on code) opens once per browser after each update and from Settings; its opt-out is stored in the browser and as a `TIPS_DISABLED` marker on the daemon.
- **Footer and "..." menu**: under the composer sit the Chats and Task info drawer buttons (shown only in a narrow window on a device without touch), **+** for a new chat (an untouched new chat is reused), Inject, the model picker and **...**, whose 11 entries are Voice trigger, Save chat, Attach files, Git Commit, Browser, Terminal, Dark mode or Light mode, High contrast, Auto mode, iPhone Home Screen and Settings. Save chat (Share chat until 10-19) writes the whole chat as one standalone page, `reports/chat-<title-slug>-<chatId>.html` in the working directory; a chat over the daemon's reply budget (section 14) is saved with its most recent tasks only, under a warning, and a page too large to upload is refused with an error. Git Commit commits the folder and machine of the chat header (10-18) and reports "Staging changes…", "Generating commit message…" and "Committing…" on one line; the flow is section 12. iPhone Home Screen **NEW** (11-03) loads `/webclip.mobileconfig`, which Safari on a phone passes to the profile installer. The Working directory entry is gone: it is the chat-header drop-down.
- **Model picker**: a pill in the footer opens a searchable list with Recently Used first, most used on top, then one group per vendor, each row with its input and output price per million tokens; the five router entries are SEAs and show a cost label. The pick becomes the chat's model and the default of the machine in the chat header, whose list this is **NEW** (10-30). When the agent calls `set_model`, the pill shows the new model until the task ends. Models and routing are section 8.
- **CHATS panel**: the left sidebar, a drawer on a phone. On top are a refresh button that reloads the whole page **NEW** (10-23), a search box, a Flat list or Group by chat toggle and a folded Filters drawer: chips Running, Errored and Succeeded (on), Favorites (off) and Cron (off, so tasks started by scheduled jobs stay hidden until it is ticked) **NEW** (10-17), a From and To date range, and selects for SEA (All, None, each SEA), Machine **NEW** (10-10), WORKDIR **NEW** (10-12, in place of the workspace-only chip) and tag. Search, tag, machine and working directory are answered by the daemon, 50 tasks a page with more loaded on scroll; the other filters act on the rows already loaded. A WORKDIR pick is a folder of this computer and lists this daemon's tasks only, so while one is picked the Machine select is reset to All and disabled. Chats sort by completion time **CHANGED** (10-14) under a Running section **NEW** (10-07), then Today, Yesterday and one section per earlier day. A chat is one group whose header shows the stored chat summary (section 11) or the first task, an age and, when one applies, the last task's status mark **NEW** (10-07): a spinner while it runs, a `?` while it waits for an answer (10-06), a red cross once it failed and a green tick only when it finished while this page was open; a task that succeeded before the page loaded has no mark. Clicking the header opens the chat **CHANGED** (10-08); a chevron unfolds its tasks; a pencil or a double-click renames it **NEW** (10-15: Enter saves, Escape cancels, an empty box restores the computed summary); a trash button deletes it after a second click **NEW** (10-20). A task row has the same mark, the text, favourite, copy and delete (**NEW** 10-20, not while the task runs), and age and machine labels (the tag and SEA labels left on 11-12); unfolded it adds metrics, working directory, model, budget and the chat, task and parent ids with copy buttons. Chats of every registered machine are merged in **NEW** (10-10, 10-24), open in a tab bound to that machine (10-13), and their rename, favourite and delete are sent to the machine that owns them (11-08).
- **Right sidebar**: nine sections, up from five on 09-30 **CHANGED**, each collapsible and resizable with its state kept in the browser. The first page load after an install or update, once per browser, leaves only Recommendations open **NEW** (10-12). A click on the header of a folded section opens it and folds every other section, while a click on an open section folds only that one **CHANGED** (10-11; before, a click toggled its own section alone). A task that starts in the chat on screen opens Task Info and Task update and folds the other sections **NEW** (10-09); in the split layout it folds the content pane away too. Schedule, Machines, Apps, Spend and Recommendations show the machine picked in the chat header **NEW** (10-18) and are re-read every 30 s while the page is visible.

| Section | Shows | Actions |
|---|---|---|
| Task Info | Tokens, Cost, Steps, Time, Machine, Workdir, Max budget, Date, Base model, Chat id, Task id; Parent task and Agent when they apply. Time is `Running 2m 5s` while the task runs and the time it took (`2m 5s`) once it is over, however it ended **CHANGED** (11-12; it read `Done (2m 5s)`, `Error` or `Stopped` after a live end and `Ready` for a finished task opened from the history list), and `—` when the task's row records no end. The Parallel mode row left on 10-12 | none |
| Task update **CHANGED** (10-03) | the /ask agent's answer to what the task has done so far: first asked 60 s after the start, then when the last answer is 10 minutes old, each run capped at $1; read every 5 s while on screen; also for a task on another machine (10-24) | refresh runs it now |
| Explorer, Source Control | see the next bullet | |
| Schedule | cron jobs: name, schedule, next or last run, a running or paused badge (section 20) | **+** opens an in-place form for a task prompt or a shell command **NEW** (10-12); edit, run now, pause or resume and delete on each row **NEW** (10-11); copy; refresh reloads the jobs |
| Machines **NEW** (10-11) | registered machines: reachability dot, address, a `local` badge (section 15) | **+** form (name, address, password); copy, delete; refresh pings again |
| Apps | messaging and third-party apps with "N of M connected" (section 19) | Connect opens a new chat and sends the sign-in task at once; refresh checks the sign-in state again |
| Spend | an all-time total, a heatmap with one cell per day (at least 98 days, weeks as columns) and one bar per model | arrows page through earlier weeks; refresh reloads the figures |
| Recommendations **NEW** (10-12) | the ten tasks the last `/recommendations` run stored (`RECOMMENDATION_COUNT`; section 9) | a click puts one in a new chat's composer unsent; refresh re-reads the stored list |

- **Explorer and Source Control**: both moved from tabs of the left sidebar into the right one on 10-08 **CHANGED**. Explorer is a lazy file tree rooted at the working directory; Add Folder to Explorer... adds more top-level folders, kept in the browser. Its menu has New File, New Folder, Cut, Copy, Paste, Rename, Delete, Copy Path, Copy Relative Path, Open to the Side, Find in Folder, Select for Compare and Compare with Selected, Compare Selected for a multi-selection **NEW** (09-30), Set as Working Directory and Remove Folder from Explorer. Source Control shows the branch, a count of uncommitted files, the changed files under Merge Changes, Staged Changes and Changes (one foldable block per worktree when the repository has several) and a commit graph of up to 24 lanes whose commits unfold to their files. A click on a file row opens a side-by-side diff: a commit's file against the commit's parent, a changed file as the working tree against `HEAD` **CHANGED** (11-07; before, a changed file opened as the plain file and a deleted one not at all). A commit's menu has Open Changes, Checkout (Detached), Create Branch, Create Tag, Cherry Pick, Compare with, Copy Commit Hash and Copy Commit Message. It stages and commits nothing: that is the Git Commit entry. For a chat bound to another machine both show that machine's files **NEW** (10-14).
- **Editors, viewers, browser and terminal**: a text file opens in Monaco 0.52.2 (loaded from jsDelivr; a read-only plain view when the CDN is unreachable) under a File, Edit, Selection, View and Go menu bar. Ctrl/Cmd+S saves, closing a tab with unsaved edits asks Save and close, Don't save or Keep editing, and a save over a file that changed on disk asks Overwrite or Reload from disk. **NEW** (10-23): every file tab has a Reload from disk button. Markdown and HTML files open rendered, with an Edit source and Preview toggle; a PDF opens in the pdf.js viewer (pdfjs-dist 6.3.289: Download link, page box, Ctrl/Cmd+0 fit width, Ctrl/Cmd with `+`/`-` zoom, Home/End, PageUp/PageDown, and Left/Right, which turn the page only while the pages are no wider than the view and pan otherwise); an image opens as a picture, a folder as a listing, any other binary as a download link, and a diff in Monaco's diff editor **NEW** (09-30). A report, that is a Markdown or HTML file that a Write call saved under a `reports` folder, opens by itself when its task ends, rendered in a tab that has no Edit source toggle until the file is opened by hand. The Browser tab streams the daemon machine's browser as JPEG frames under an address bar and sends pointer, wheel, keyboard, paste and touch input back (the agent's browser is section 17). The Terminal tab **NEW** (10-05) is xterm.js 6.0.0 on a shell of the daemon's machine, or of the chat's machine, in the chat's folder; tabs are titled Terminal, Terminal 2 and so on, belong to the client that opened them and re-attach after a reconnect, and in bash and zsh the Up key of a new tab recalls the commands entered in the other tabs (10-09; section 14).
- **Settings**: a sheet titled with the product name, the version and the commit hash (11-07). Above the controls it lists this daemon's addresses, each a link with a Copy URL button: the machine's `ntfy.sh` topic page, where a tunnel's URL is posted (the active URL itself when no topic can be derived), Local (this machine) and, when other devices can connect, LAN (local network), followed, while the daemon serves a certificate from its own CA, by a line on trusting it (`kiss-web --trust-ca`, a `ca.crt` link); a daemon in Docker lists none (10-16; section 14). Edits to the daemon's settings are saved when the sheet closes, by its close button, a click outside it or Escape: the fields edited since it opened are sent and no others **CHANGED** (10-28; before, the whole form was written back). For a chat on another machine it loads and saves that machine's settings **NEW** (10-17) and leaves the addresses out; when the chat header's machine changes while the sheet is open, the edits are first saved to the machine they were made for (10-30). Auto commit and Use worktree left the sheet on 10-09, Classify tasks and Use Jev on 10-15, and Chat in the editor with the extension on 10-14.

| Control | Default | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Remote password | 20 random letters and digits, drawn by the first `install.sh` run and kept by later ones; empty without that run | plain text behind an eye toggle, editable, with a button that fills in a new random 20-character password, stored like any edit when the sheet closes **CHANGED** (10-15 to 10-19); while it is empty only this computer can connect (section 14) |
| Max budget per task ($) | 100 | |
| Use web tools | on | |
| Use persistent memory | on | section 13 |
| Compaction **NEW** (11-05) | on | section 6 |
| Drive my Chrome **NEW** (10-28) | off | section 17 |
| Play a victory sound when a task finishes **NEW** (11-04) | on | kept per browser |
| Auto submit spoken task | Yes | kept per browser; section 18 |
| Wake phrase ("Hey Sorcar") sensitivity | 80 | slider from 0 to 100; kept per browser |
| Tips, Update, Reset Server, Update Models | buttons | Reset Server asks "Restart the server?" first when a task is running; Update, Reset Server and Update Models act on this daemon and are hidden on another machine's sheet |
| API Keys | folded | 8 fields: Anthropic key and workspace id, OpenAI, Z.AI, Moonshot, OpenRouter, Together, Gemini |
| Custom Models | folded | name, endpoint, API key and headers boxes with Add; under them the models saved in `~/.kiss/MY_MODELS.json` (section 8), each with Edit, which loads it into the boxes, swaps Add for Save and Cancel and may rename it, and a two-step Delete; closing the sheet over an unsaved model edit asks Discard changes or Keep editing |
| Bedrock **NEW** (10-17) | folded | bearer token or access key pair, session token, region |
| Claude Code **NEW** (10-17) | folded | CLI status, "Bill my Claude subscription" (on), subscription token; section 8 |
| Codex **NEW** (10-18) | folded | CLI status, "Bill my ChatGPT subscription" (on), Sign in with ChatGPT, access token; section 8 |

- **Sounds** **NEW** (11-04): `victory.mp3` plays when a task of the page finishes, on this machine or another one, and not for a sub-agent; a play that the browser's autoplay rule refuses waits for the next click or key press. A spoken task is acknowledged with `working-on-it.mp3` when it is submitted, and speech from the `talk` tool plays in the page (section 18).
- **Phone layout**: below 900 px the two sidebars are drawers. **NEW** (10-08): on a phone or tablet the page is four screens in a row (chats, chat, open file, task info) and a sideways swipe of at least 60 px moves one screen, skipping the file screen when nothing is open. A swipe right on the file screen returns to the chat, on its machine, that the screen was reached from (11-12); over a streamed browser page it does so only when the machine's daemon says that nothing under the finger had anything left to scroll that way (`browserTouch`). The two drawer buttons are hidden there and a sticky notice explains the swipe **NEW** (10-19); it returns on every page load until its "Do not show this again" box is ticked, which that browser remembers (`kiss-swipe-hint-dismissed`). The composer's text box folds away while a task runs, and **+** unfolds and focuses it (11-06).
- **Appearance**: light (VS Code Light Modern, the default) and dark (Dark Modern), plus High contrast and Auto mode **NEW** (10-17; Auto is dark from 19:00 to 07:00 on the device clock). All are "..." entries remembered per browser, and a switch also recolours Monaco, code highlighting and terminals. `main.css` defines 90 root tokens (7 font sizes, 11 spacing steps, 9 radii, 5 shadows, 3 durations, a 17-layer z-index scale and the colours), and `remote-codex.css` sets 64 custom properties on the page body from the theme palette that the daemon inlines. Text is 13 px.
- **Updates and refresh**: when the daemon finds a newer release (section 14) a sticky toast offers Update, Update when idle (installed the first time no task is running; Cancel calls it off) and Remind me later (24 hours, in every window), and the Update button in Settings carries a badge. **NEW** (10-23): the page reloads itself when it reconnects to a restarted daemon. `sw.js` keeps the page's shell on screen while the connection is down.
- **Sign-in window** **NEW** (10-20): while the brand's sign-in hook (`$KISS_HOME/signin`) reports that nobody is logged in, the daemon makes every window show a dialog with one button per provider. A click opens the provider's page in a new tab; when the sign-in completes the dialog closes in every window. A paste row takes the address of the final page for a browser on another computer than the daemon, and Not now dismisses the dialog.
- **Welcome page**: a new chat shows the product logo in a light or dark variant, "Welcome to KISS Sorcar" and the tagline from `brand.json`.

## 17. Browser, web research and sign-in hand-off

- **Agent browser** (`src/kiss/agents/sorcar/web_use_tool.py`, 3,894 lines): `WebUseTool` drives Chromium through Patchright (`patchright>=1.63.0`; stock Playwright is the import fallback) and prefers an installed Google Chrome to the bundled Chromium, which is downloaded on first use when missing (900 s deadline). A page comes back as an accessibility tree: a `Page:`/`URL:` header and interactive elements numbered `[N]`, cut at 50,000 characters. 15 tools (parameters: section 7): `go_to_url` (also `tab:list` and `tab:N`), `click`, `type_text`, `press_key`, `scroll`, `screenshot`, `get_page_content`, `evaluate_script`, `list_console_messages`, `list_network_requests`, `wait_for`, `attach_to_chrome`, `use_my_chrome`, `show_browser`, `close_browser`. A tab the page opens itself (`target="_blank"`) becomes the current one **CHANGED** (11-04); a navigation Chromium aborts with `net::ERR_NETWORK_CHANGED` is issued up to 3 times **CHANGED** (09-30); a call on a Playwright driver that has died raises at once instead of hanging, and the next call relaunches the browser **CHANGED** (10-25). With **Use web tools** unticked in Settings no browser tool is built and `WEB_TOOLS_OFF_NOTE` tells the model that the research rules do not apply.
- **Profiles**: the top-level agent uses the persistent profile `~/.kiss/browser_profile`, so cookies and logins survive tasks and restarts, under a file lock (`<profile>.lock`, 120 s); when a live Chromium of another process holds the profile, numbered siblings `browser_profile_1` to `_99` are tried. Each sub-agent gets a throwaway profile (`kiss_web_profile_*` in the temp directory, deleted when the tool closes), so concurrent children do not contend for the shared one. The tool's own Chromium aborts every request to `accounts.google.com`.
- **Display and fingerprint**: "headless", the default, is a headed Chromium on a private Xvfb display (`1920x1080x24`, one per process) on Linux machines with Xvfb, and real headless elsewhere. A real-headless launch passes Chrome's own `--user-agent` switch without the `HeadlessChrome` token **CHANGED** (10-27; before, an in-page rewrite of `navigator.userAgent` that Cloudflare detected). `AutomationControlled` is disabled in every launch and WebAuthn in unattended ones **CHANGED** (10-01), because a passkey request there opens a native dialog that nobody sees and that swallows every click. The viewport is 1280×900.
- **Human-like input**: the pointer glides along a Bézier curve to a random point 35 to 65% into the element, pauses 40 to 160 ms and holds the button 45 to 140 ms; text is typed key by key (35 to 115 ms inside a word, 90 to 260 ms at a space, 150 to 450 ms before Enter); scrolling moves in wheel notches of about 300 px, at most 50 per call.
- **Bot-protection pages** (`web_stealth.py`, 1,192 lines): 12 kinds of page are recognised from the title, the first 1,500 characters of body text and the script URLs. After `go_to_url`, and since 11-04 after `click`, `type_text` with Enter, `press_key` and a tab switch **CHANGED**, the tool waits for such a page to clear while it moves the pointer, presses the widget a person would press once it has shown for 1.2 s, and when the page stays puts a `Note:` above the tree that names the vendor and the way out (`show_browser()` or another source). Each press gives the page the table's full wait again, counted from the end of the press:

| Page | Wait | What the tool does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Cloudflare challenge ("Just a moment...") | 12 s | ticks the Turnstile checkbox once |
| Anubis proof-of-work check | 45 s **CHANGED** (10-26; 12 s before) | waits |
| PerimeterX "Press & Hold" | 12 s | holds the button 8 to 10 s, up to twice **NEW** (10-26) |
| AWS WAF token challenge **NEW** (10-31) | 12 s | waits |
| AWS WAF CAPTCHA puzzle **NEW** (10-31) | 3 s | reports it |
| Stack Overflow and Stack Exchange "Human verification" **NEW** (11-04) | 12 s | ticks the reCAPTCHA box once; stops waiting when the picture puzzle opens |
| Reddit network-policy block **NEW** (11-04) | none | reports it; the note says to log in through `show_browser()` |
| Google "unusual traffic" | none | opens the same query on Bing |
| Cloudflare block, Akamai block, Imperva Incapsula block, generic CAPTCHA | 12 s | reports it |

- A `semanticscholar.org` page that stays blocked is looked up on Crossref instead when its URL is a search with a non-empty `q` or a paper link with a title slug before the 40-hex id (`api.crossref.org/works`: 10 works for a search, 5 for a paper, rendered into the page), unless the user can see the browser **NEW** (11-01).
- **DevTools-style tools** **NEW** (10-28): `evaluate_script` runs JavaScript in the page and returns the result, a string as it is and anything else as JSON (cut at 20,000 characters); `list_console_messages` and `list_network_requests` return the page's last 200 console entries and 300 requests; `wait_for` waits up to 120 s for visible text to appear or disappear. The `/devtools` SEA (`src/kiss/agents/seas/devtools/devtools_sea.py`, 167 lines; section 9) appends an act-then-verify workflow built on them to the system prompt and switches the browser tools on.
- **The user's own Chrome** **NEW** (10-28), by two routes:
  - `attach_to_chrome(cdp_url)` attaches to a Chrome started with `--remote-debugging-port=9222`. The page tools (`go_to_url` to `wait_for` above) then act on the user's tabs until `close_browser()` detaches or `show_browser` moves the page out, both of which leave that Chrome running; a lost attachment is an error rather than a silent return to the tool's own Chromium.
  - `use_my_chrome` reaches the everyday Chrome (default profile, Gmail, SSO, passkeys) through the Chrome bridge (`chrome_bridge.py`, 756 lines): a loopback WebSocket server on an ephemeral port that the "KISS Sorcar Bridge" extension (Manifest V3) dials, every 3 s while Chrome is awake and from a 30 s alarm. The extension's two files are installed into `~/.kiss/chrome_extension/`, the folder to pick once under chrome://extensions → Developer mode → Load unpacked, beside `bridge-config.json` (mode 600: the live port and a per-start token, re-read on every connect). Ten actions: `list_tabs`, `open_tab`, `navigate`, `activate_tab`, `close_tab`, `snapshot` (the visible interactive elements, numbered), `click` and `type_text` by snapshot uid, `evaluate`, `screenshot`. The handshake is a mutual SHA-256 challenge-response, so the token never crosses the wire and a peer that does not know it (a web page, a process that occupies the port) can neither drive the browser nor pose as the daemon; the mode of `bridge-config.json` keeps the token from other OS users, not from the user's own processes. Page JavaScript runs in the page's own world and goes through `chrome.debugger` only when the page's CSP refuses it; focus-stealing commands are serialised; each command has a deadline (30 s by default, 120 s at most), and one whose connection closed or whose deadline passed while it queued does not run. The daemon serves the bridge from start-up when the Settings checkbox "Drive my Chrome" (`chrome_bridge`, off by default) is ticked (ticking it starts the bridge on save; unticking it leaves a running bridge up until the daemon stops); otherwise the first `use_my_chrome` call starts it, waits up to 5 s for the extension, and defers to a bridge another KISS process already serves. Tests: `test_chrome_bridge.py` (a scripted extension peer over a real socket), `test_chrome_extension_js.py` (the page-side JavaScript of `snapshot`, `click` and `type_text`, extracted from the shipped `background.js`), `test_web_devtools_tools.py`.
- **`show_browser`**: under the daemon the page moves into the streamed Browser tab (section 14) on every surface together with its URL, cookies and the origin's `localStorage`; the user clicks and types there while the agent keeps driving the same page over a second CDP connection. Out of a Chrome reached with `attach_to_chrome` only the URL moves: that Chrome's cookies and `localStorage` stay in it. `show_browser(visible=False)` carries the session back and closes the tab; a page still shown when the task ends stays open. That browser has its own persistent profile, `~/.kiss/browser-tab-profile`. Without a daemon the agent's profile reopens in a visible window.
- **Web research protocol** (`src/kiss/SYSTEM.md`): Google research is the default, skipped only when the agent is fully confident it can do the task without it; at least 10 distinct sites per research session, each visited with `go_to_url` (curl and wget do not count); notes and a `Websites visited: N/10` counter kept in `<scratch dir>/information.md` **CHANGED** (10-25; `./tmp/information-{id}.md` before); a real-time question must be looked up with tools and may stop at one authoritative site; when Google blocks the search, the agent opens it in the browser and asks the user to pass the bot check, then turns to other search engines.
- **Sign-in hand-off** (`src/kiss/core/browser_handoff.py`, 505 lines): `open_for_user(url)` puts a sign-in page where the user can act on it. It tries the Browser tab on every surface first, then the machine's default browser, else nothing, and returns `"browser_tab"`, `"default_browser"` or `""`; `browser_handoff_note` turns the answer into the instruction the agent reads. Only `http`, `https` and `file` URLs are opened, a URL opened in the last 120 s is not opened again, and the default-browser step is skipped in a headless environment (`KISS_HEADLESS`, a container, Linux without a display). Callers (section 19): `portal_handoff`, which opens the page and returns that note, in 12 third-party agent SEAs; the device-code and Nextcloud sign-ins of `_device_auth.py`; the WhatsApp and Signal QR pages; the Composio Google link; MCP OAuth.
- 13,092 `go_to_url`, 818 `screenshot`, 894 `click`, 322 `get_page_content` and 68 `show_browser` calls are recorded in the task database, and 211 `evaluate_script` calls since the DevTools tools arrived on 10-28.

## 18. Voice

- **Wake word** (`src/kiss/webapp/media/voice.js`, 1,379 lines, with the vendored `vosk.js`): the **Voice trigger** item of the composer's "More actions" menu makes the page listen locally for **"Hey Sorcar"** with the Vosk small English model (`vosk-model-small-en-us-0.15`, cached under `~/.kiss/models` and served to the page at `/voice-model.tar.gz`); the choice survives reloads. Two recognizers hear every audio block: one whose grammar holds only the phrase, spelled "hey sore car", "hey sir car" and "hey sar car" because the name is not in the model's vocabulary, and a grammar-free one that confirms on the word "hey" or on closing words within 3 edits of "heysorcar". A wake needs a final result from both within 2.5 s **CHANGED** (10-20: final results only, where a partial followed by a pause used to suffice; 11-08: the second recognizer). The phrase was "Abracadabra Sorcar" and then "Abracadabra" from 10-17 and is "Hey Sorcar" again since 11-08.
- **Capture and transcription**: after a wake the page waits up to 5 s for speech and gives up when nobody speaks, records from the first loud block until 2 s of silence or 30 s of recording, puts the audio from before the wake in front (2 s, plus the time, up to 2.5 s, that the wake waited for its grammar-free confirmation, in whole audio blocks) and sends the lot to the daemon as `voiceTranscribe` (16 kHz mono PCM). There (`src/kiss/server/speech.py`, 1,019 lines) `gpt-audio` transcribes it and translates it into English, with a second attempt on 1 s more silence when the reply reads like a refusal **CHANGED** (10-01); each audio request is bounded at 60 s (`KISS_VOICE_AUDIO_TIMEOUT`). The daemon then looks for the name in the transcript: a word among the first eight that is "sorcar", one of its spellings (`sorkar`, `sarkar`, `sarcar`, `soccer`, `sir car`, `sore car`, `sar car`) or one edit from it is cut together with everything before it, and a transcript without one is dropped, so a sound-alike such as "hey, so the car" lights the mic but never submits a task. Only speech reported as non-English, whose translation can lose the wake word, passes on the page's two agreeing recognizers alone **CHANGED** (11-08).
- **Speakers**: a Vosk x-vector model (`vosk-model-spk-0.4`) on the daemon numbers voices in order of first appearance (cosine distance of at most 0.6 from a known speaker's first sample reuses the number), and the agent reads `Speaker #N says in the language <tag> that: <text>`.
- **Where the words go**: a transcript belongs to the conversation that was on screen when its capture began and is dropped when that conversation is no longer on screen. It then takes the typed path: it starts a task, is added to the running task, or answers a pending question. With **Auto submit spoken task** set to No it is inserted at the cursor as bare words instead, except an answer to a pending question, which is always sent.
- **Settings** (kept in the browser's `localStorage`, followed by its other tabs): `Wake phrase ("Hey Sorcar") sensitivity`, 0 to 100 in steps of 5 with default 80 (higher accepts less confident words), and **Auto submit spoken task**, default Yes.
- **Failures**: loading the speech engine, loading the model and the microphone request are each given 30 s; a microphone that goes away, a recognizer error or a dead speech worker turns the trigger off with the reason on its button, and one sticky toast states the remedy for a denied, missing or busy microphone.
- **Spoken replies**: the system prompt tells the agent to answer a spoken message in the speaker's language with `talk(language, text, emotion)` (section 7; 12 named emotions). The text is synthesised once with `gpt-audio-1.5` and the `cedar` voice (`src/kiss/core/speech_synthesis.py`), and the MP3 travels in the `talk` event to every device that has the task's tab open, where clips play one at a time; a client stays silent when synthesis failed, the Web Speech fallback being gone. For a task that a local API client on the daemon's machine started (a `daemon_client` run such as the Python client API of section 5; the `sorcar` CLI runs its agent in its own process and never connects) the daemon plays the clip on that machine's speakers (`src/kiss/server/talk_player.py`: `afplay` on macOS, else `mpg123`, `ffplay` or `mpv`; `KISS_SORCAR_PLAY_CMD` overrides). The synthesis spend is added to the task's.
- **Sounds**: `working-on-it.mp3` plays when a spoken message is submitted. `victory.mp3` **NEW** (11-04), a half-second synthesised flourish rendered by `kiss.scripts.make_victory_sound`, plays when a top-level task ends with `task_done`, whether it succeeded or not and whether it ran on this daemon or on a registered machine (section 15); sub-agents, errors, stops and replayed transcripts are silent. The Settings checkbox "Play a victory sound when a task finishes (on this machine or on a registered one)", on by default and kept per browser, mutes it; a play the browser's autoplay policy refuses is kept for the next click or keypress.
- **Debug captures** **NEW** (11-07): every capture the daemon can decode is saved before transcription as a WAV plus a JSON note (the wake flags, then the transcript, its language and whether it was accepted) under `~/.kiss/voice-debug/`, newest 200 kept. In the page, `localStorage.kissVoiceDebug = '1'` logs recognizer results and partials, the microphone track and RMS levels to the console.
- **Removed**: the host-side listener (`voice_wake.py`, `voiceWake.ts`) went with the VS Code extension on 10-14, after the daemon's `voiceWake*` commands on 10-08; voice now lives only in the web app's page.
- 119 top-level tasks whose text starts with `Speaker #` (dictation inserted as bare words is not counted) and 272 `talk` calls recorded, 111 and 208 of them in July; 3 tasks and 7 calls are dated 10-01 or later.

## 19. Messaging and third-party agents

`src/kiss/agents/third_party_agents/` holds **44 agents**, one folder each (`<name>/<name>_sea.py`; 33,726 lines in all), plus `muse_auth/` (8 files, 4,751 lines), `auth_status.py`, `govee.py` (Govee lights CLI, not one of the 44) and shared helpers (`_channel_agent_utils.py` 2,155 lines, `_channel_cli.py`, `_kiss_web_launcher.py`, `_backend_utils.py`, `_device_auth.py`, `_oauth_apps.py`, `_composio_google.py`, `_google_workspace_utils.py`, `_overleaf_realtime.py`). The agents define 151 sign-in tools and 365 API tools between them; an agent always offers its sign-in tools and adds its API tools once it is authenticated. Each has a console script (`kiss-<name>`; `kiss-gchat` for googlechat, `kiss-ha` for homeassistant), every one except the hidden `oai` is a slash command `/<name>` (section 9), and per-agent configuration lives under `~/.kiss/third_party_agents/<name>/` (the directory is `brave_search` for brave, `openai_compat` for oai, and `google_calendar`, `google_docs`, `google_drive`, `google_sheets` for gcal, gdocs, gdrive, gsheets).

- **32 messaging and device channels** (233 API tools): bluebubbles, dingtalk, discord, email, feishu, gmail, googlechat, homeassistant, imessage, irc, line, matrix, mattermost, msteams, nextcloud, nostr, ntfy, phone, qq, signal, simplex, slack, sms, synology, telegram, tlon, twitch, webhook, wecom, weixin, whatsapp, zalo.
- **10 service APIs** (128 API tools): brave, firecrawl, gcal, gdocs, gdrive, github, gsheets, notion, **overleaf** (48 tools over the user's own `overleaf_session2` browser cookie, CSRF token, socket.io file tree and optional Git bridge token) and postgres (read-only unless its configuration says otherwise, 60 s statement timeout on every tool except `pg_execute`; `pg_query` returns one complete JSON document with text cells cut at 2,000 characters **CHANGED**, where output was sliced at 8,000 characters until 11-07).
- **2 infrastructure surfaces**: `kiss-a2a` (Agent-to-Agent protocol: an agent card at `/.well-known/agent-card.json` and JSON-RPC `message/send`, on `127.0.0.1:18091` by default, 20 messages per `contextId` per hour, every inbound POST request appended to `a2a_audit.jsonl`; tools `a2a_discover`, `a2a_call`, `a2a_get_task`) and `kiss-oai` (OpenAI-compatible HTTP server started with `kiss-oai --serve`: `GET /v1/models` and `POST /v1/chat/completions` with a bearer key). Both derive from `BaseSea`, not `ChannelSea`, so channel dispatch leaves them out.
- **Dispatch** **CHANGED**: a channel is a folder whose class derives from `ChannelSea` (`seas/base/base_sea.py`; base classes replaced the per-SEA `settings()` flag on 10-07), and `available_channels()` returns the 42 names. `run_agent(agent="<channel>", task=...)` from any task runs the channel as a sub-agent with a nested tab (section 10); `/<channel> <task>` runs it in the chat itself. Either way it works in `~/.kiss/channel_work` without the worktree tools, classifier, web tools or memory (`WORKER_DEFAULTS`; those keys and `work_dir` are locked against overrides). A dispatched channel takes nothing from the calling task (chat, model, budget share, container, prompt suffixes; `inherit: true` is refused) and receives the task text verbatim. The daemon appends `CHANNEL_PREAMBLE` to the system prompt, the SEA's `system_prompt()` then appends its `channel_system_prompt` (29 agents define one), and its `tools()` adds the agent's tools. The `options` key `workspace` (default `default`) names the account: it is published in `KISS_CHANNEL_WORKSPACE` for the run, runs of one workspace overlap and a run of another waits up to 900 s (`channel_workspace.py`). Slack is the only agent that reads it (`~/.kiss/third_party_agents/slack/<workspace>/token.json`, `kiss-slack --list-workspaces`, `--delete-workspace WS`).
- **Command line** **CHANGED**: `kiss-<channel> -t "task"` (or `-f file`; `-m` model, `-b` budget, `--workspace`) runs the agent's SEA file through `kiss.server.sorcar.run(sea_path=...)` on a daemon the CLI starts inside its own process: a loopback WSS endpoint on an ephemeral port, with its endpoint file and tab registry in a private temporary directory. The run therefore needs no `kiss-web` service and stays out of `~/.kiss/tabs.json`. That private daemon listened on a Unix socket until 10-01, and the `tools=` file contract it used was removed on 10-03.
- **Gateways and scheduled delivery**: a channel whose module passes a backend factory to `channel_main()` (25 messaging channels; not gmail, homeassistant, imessage, nostr, tlon, twitch, wecom) supports one-poll ticks `kiss-<channel> --channel=<chat> [--allow-users a,b] [--pairing] [--quiet]` and pairing admin `--approve CODE` / `--list-pending`. A tick (`ChannelRunner`) takes a non-blocking lock so overlapping ticks exit, polls up to 50 messages from a persisted cursor, admits every sender unless `--allow-users` or `--pairing` is given and then only the allow list and the approved users (with `--pairing` an unknown sender is sent a one-time code), runs one task per message with the daemon chat resumed per thread, and records each reply in a delivery ledger: sent with one retry, redelivered on the next tick if that fails. 5 failed ticks in a row pause the channel for 900 s. Slack alone continues a thread from later replies (`poll_thread_messages`, at most 20 threads per tick). The config keys `channel_model_name` and `channel_max_budget` apply when `-m` / `-b` are omitted, and `[SILENT]` / `NO_REPLY` suppress delivery. An always-on gateway is that tick scheduled as a cron command job, and the same channels are cron delivery targets such as `telegram:123`, `slack:eng`, `ntfy` (section 20).
- **Sign-in**: the Apps subpanel lists the auth state of the 42 channels as connected, not connected or unknown (`auth_status.py` in a subprocess: 8 workers, 20 s for all probes together, 45 s for the subprocess, result cached 30 s), and a click on an unconnected app starts a "Connect my <app>" task; the same probe feeds `/recommendations` (section 9). Every sign-in page is handed to the user in the Browser tab or the default browser (section 17). Flows in `_device_auth.py`: device code (GitHub, Teams, Twitch, Matrix), PKCE on `http://localhost:53682/callback` (Slack, Discord), Nextcloud Login Flow v2; Signal links by `signal-cli link` QR and WhatsApp pairs a locally built bridge by QR. The four client IDs embedded in `_oauth_apps.py` are empty, so browser sign-in to GitHub, Teams, Slack and Discord needs `KISS_<PROVIDER>_CLIENT_ID`; Twitch takes the user's own client ID and Matrix registers itself with the homeserver. Discord's browser sign-in reaches only `list_guilds` and `post_message` to the authorised webhook channel: its other 9 API tools and poll mode need a bot token, which `authenticate_discord(bot_token=...)` stores without a client ID. Five agents probe a pasted credential with one direct request before storing it (`probe_credential`; Nextcloud keeps an older probe of its own), and `enroll_credential` writes the config file and the vault entry under one lock and restores the previous file when the store fails **NEW**. The six Google agents (gmail, gcal, gdocs, gdrive, gsheets, googlechat) go through Composio Connect Links and `tools.proxy`, so no Google token reaches the process (project keys `ak_…`; consumer `ck_…` keys are rejected); their auth checks and cron's pre-flight (section 20) ask Composio for the live connection state through `connection_status()` **NEW**. Gmail exposes 13 tools with no permanent delete, and `reply_to_message(draft_only=True)` and `create_draft(thread_id=...)` leave reply drafts inside the thread **NEW**.
- **Muse auth**: a credential-isolation daemon (`muse_auth/`, protocol v8) holding real tokens in a vault under `~/.kiss/muse_auth/` and handing surrogate tokens to 18 connectors (17 SEAs plus Govee) under a host allow-list and a read/write policy (reads allowed, writes need a grant of scope `once`, `session`, `perpetual` or `ttl`). It is the default on Linux only, because it identifies its peers with `SO_PEERCRED`; `KISS_MUSE_AUTH=0` opts out and `KISS_MUSE_AUTH=1` forces it. CLI verbs `status, import, grant, revoke, audit, clear, daemon, stop, export`; daemon spawn serialised behind `spawn.lock`; Microsoft Teams requires it. **CHANGED** since 09-30: enrolment checks read the vault directory, so building a tool list or probing auth state never starts the daemon; the daemon exits when its state directory is deleted; a `generation` request tells `enroll_credential` whether an interrupted store reached the vault.
- **MCP connectors**: remote MCP servers sign in through `connect_mcp_server` / `finish_mcp_server_connect` or `python -m kiss.agents.sorcar.mcp_oauth <name> [url] [transport]` (known names notion, linear, asana, zoom; CIMD, dynamic client registration, or a pre-registered app given in `KISS_MCP_<NAME>_CLIENT_ID` with an optional `KISS_MCP_<NAME>_CLIENT_SECRET`, the path the module documents for Zoom; loopback redirect on port 53683; the wait for the redirect expires after 600 s), with tokens in `~/.kiss/mcp_auth/<name>.<digest>.json`, one file per server name and URL since 11-01 **CHANGED**. `connectors/` at the repository root is a catalogue of 16 MCP servers (`catalog.json`: 14 launched locally, 2 remote without sign-in; 7 flagged default, none enabled automatically) with `enable.py list|enable|disable` writing `~/.kiss/mcp.json` or, with `--scope project`, `<project>/.kiss/mcp.json`, `verify.py` connecting to every configured server, and two organisation cost reports over the providers' admin APIs (`bin/anthropic_costs.sh`, `bin/openai_costs.sh`). Section 7 covers the MCP tools themselves.
- Recorded use: `gmail_sea` 852 runs (822 of them inside cron-launched tasks), `slack_sea` 80, `github_sea` 5, `whatsapp_sea` 3, `gcal_sea` 3, `gdrive_sea` 3 and one each of `telegram_sea`, `overleaf_sea`, `ntfy_sea`, `gsheets_sea`, `gdocs_sea` and `dingtalk_sea` in the `sea` column; 1,517 `list_messages`, 789 `check_gmail_auth`, 544 `get_message`, 90 `get_profile`, 85 `post_message`, 84 `check_slack_auth`, 57 `get_thread`, 49 `read_messages`, 47 `reply_to_message` and 23 `send_email` tool calls.

## 20. Scheduled automations

`cron_agent.py` (3,044 lines) is a SEA dispatched with `run_agent(agent="cron", task=...)` or with the built-in `/cron` command **NEW** (10-04; section 9). `CronAgentSea` is a `ChannelSea` whose `settings()` returns `settings | {"work_dir": cron_work_dir()}` (`~/.kiss/cron/work`), whose `system_prompt()` appends the cron dispatch preamble and whose `tools()` adds `cron_job` and `gateway_command`; only that session holds the `cron_job` tool, so the chat agent never edits schedules directly. Bundled SEAs, the Schedule subpanel and the `kiss-cron` console script call the same module.

- **Store**: `~/.kiss/cron/jobs.json`, written atomically with mode `0600` under an exclusive file lock on `~/.kiss/cron/jobs.lock` (`fcntl.flock` on POSIX, `msvcrt.locking` on Windows); edits refuse a store that cannot be parsed instead of overwriting it. Each run gets a scratch dir `~/.kiss/cron/runs/<id>-<rand>`, removed when the run ends unless its task may still be running; delivered results are appended to `~/.kiss/cron/output/<job_id>.md`.
- **Scheduler**: a thread inside the blocking `kiss-web` lifecycle ticks every 60 s, reschedules due jobs before running them and runs them concurrently, one thread each; `kiss-cron --tick` / `--daemon` run it standalone (command jobs then work alone, prompt jobs still need a reachable daemon). In-process `start_async()` embedders, private daemons and a server built with `scheduler=False` **NEW** (the Terminal-Bench daemon, 10-27) run no scheduler. Schedules: `every 30m`, 5-field cron, one-shot durations, ISO timestamps; cron expressions and offset-less ISO timestamps are evaluated in **America/Los_Angeles** (an explicit offset is kept).
- **No catch-up, no overlap**: a repeating job found more than 10 minutes overdue (`MISSED_RUN_GRACE_SECONDS`) is rescheduled from now without running, so a restart after downtime does not fire every missed job at once; a one-shot still runs, however late **NEW** (10-04). A job is not started while its previous run is alive in this process, while another live process runs it (`running_pid`) **NEW** (11-07), or while the daemon task of a run that timed out without a confirmed stop, or lost its connection, may still be running (`running_task_id`) **NEW** (10-25). A job with an unreadable schedule or timestamp is disabled, with the reason in `last_summary`, instead of aborting the tick.
- **Job kinds**: *prompt* jobs **CHANGED** launch the bundled `cron_prompt_sea.py` (81 lines; until 10-04 a SEA file was written per run) through the `run_agent` machinery as a top-level task in a fresh session, with the job's model, budget and timeout as call arguments and `work_dir` as an option only when the job names one. `CronPromptSea` is a `BaseSea` whose `settings()` turns the classifier off and whose `system_prompt()` swaps the full system prompt for `SYSTEM_PROMPT_LITE` plus five scheduled-run rules (10-25). The per-job `use_worktree` / `auto_commit` flags were removed 10-09: a run inside a repository uses its own worktree tools (section 12). A prompt that is a slash command (`/recommendations now`) runs that SEA directly **NEW** (10-12). An `in_chat` job **NEW** (10-12) submits its prompt verbatim as a message typed into a new chat that every surface opens and the user answers in; it works in `~/.kiss/cron/chats/<job_id>` unless it names a `work_dir`. *Command* jobs run a shell command with no LLM in the `Bash` tool's shell, in their own process group with `KISS_HOME` set, and deliver the command's stdout, stripped of surrounding whitespace and otherwise unchanged; a non-zero exit is an error reporting the exit code, stdout and stderr. A timeout kills the command's processes, best effort: on POSIX the process group and the descendants that left it (those a `/proc` snapshot taken before the kill finds), on Windows the Job Object the command was enrolled in at start or, failing that, its `taskkill /F /T` tree. Default timeouts are 3,600 s for a prompt job and 600 s for a command.
- **Gateways**: a channel gateway is a command job whose command `gateway_command` builds (`kiss-telegram --channel=-100123 --pairing --quiet`). `create` refuses a prompt that describes a gateway tick and stores a gateway command whose `deliver` names a channel target with `none` instead (the default `local` stays, and jobs already stored are normalised the same way when the store is loaded), so a tick never posts status into the chat it serves **NEW** (10-02; section 19).
- **Unattended runs**: a prompt job's task text is a preamble (nobody can answer questions, no scheduling during the run, the final summary is delivered verbatim, exactly `[SILENT]` when there is nothing to report), then a run-context block **NEW** (10-25), then the prompt. The block gives the time now in Pacific time and UTC, the schedule, the start of the window the run covers, the previous attempt with its status, and the last 1,500 characters of its stored result (`last_summary`, which keeps only a result's first 4,000 characters). The window starts at the watermark `covered_until`: the job's creation, then the start of the last run that ended `silent`, or `ok` with its result at every target (until 11-01 any `ok` run counted). An `error` run never moves it, even when the error reached every target; a job stored before the watermark existed gets a window start one schedule interval back until a run sets the watermark, or none when its run times give no interval. `ask_user_question` returns an error in such a run and in its sub-tasks instead of blocking. Before dispatch each Google connector the prompt names (Gmail, Calendar, Docs, Drive, Sheets, Chat) has its `connection_status()` checked **NEW** (10-25): a status other than `ACTIVE` or `UNKNOWN` fails the run with a fixed message; `in_chat` jobs and prompts that ask to authenticate or reconnect are exempt.
- **Results and delivery**: a run ends `ok`, `silent` (a successful run whose result is empty or, for prompt jobs only, is `[SILENT]` or `NO_REPLY`; nothing is delivered or logged) or `error` (delivered like a result). `deliver` accepts a comma list of `<channel>[:<chat>]` targets, `local` (the default) or `none`, both meaning the output log only; `until_delivered` disables the job once a successful, non-silent result has reached every target. A failure whose first non-blank line repeats the previous failed result's is not delivered again and pushes the next run at least 1 h out, doubling with each repeat up to 24 h **NEW** (10-25); when the previous failure's delivery to a target failed, the repeat is delivered again and nothing backs off (11-07).
- **Tool actions**: `create` (refuses a duplicate: the same prompt or command, work dir, schedule and delivery targets as a scheduled or paused job), `ensure` (idempotent by name; resumes a paused job), `list`, `remove`, `pause`, `resume`, `run_now` (refused while the job is running). A job takes `name`, `schedule`, exactly one of `prompt` or `command`, and optionally `deliver`, `model_name`, `max_budget`, `until_delivered`, `work_dir`, `timeout` and `in_chat`. `kiss-cron` offers `--list`, `--create`, `--remove`, `--pause`, `--resume` and `--run` from the shell.
- **Default jobs** **NEW** (10-12): on start the daemon seeds `DEFAULT_JOBS` once per home (recorded in `~/.kiss/cron/default-jobs-seeded.json`): `/recommendations now` every six hours (`0 */6 * * *`, $2, 900 s, `deliver` `none`) and the `in_chat` `/good_morning now` at 08:00 Pacific (`0 8 * * *`, $0.50, 300 s); section 9 describes both SEAs. A default the user removed, paused or edited is never re-created or reset.
- **Home hand-over** **NEW** (10-26): each home has its own store and only the running daemon's store ticks. Before seeding, `adopt_previous_home_jobs()` records the daemon's home in `~/.kiss-web-home` and, when the previous daemon ran from another home (a white-label install, section 21), copies the jobs whose names this store lacks: a repeating job gets a fresh next run and a reset failure streak, a one-shot keeps its deadline, a finished job stays finished. Only a home that is a direct child of the user's home directory takes part: a `KISS_HOME` anywhere else records no marker and adopts nothing.
- **Auto-scheduled jobs**: picking `autorouter` or `orautorouter` in the model picker ensures "Weekly rsi7d: autorouter evidence and prompt (Sat 1am PT)" (`0 1 * * 6`, `claude-fable-5-2-max` when runnable, since 11-09 **CHANGED**, $25 + $5 relay, 2 h nested run inside a 2 h 10 min job); `/git_extract_knowledge` registers "git-knowledge daily update: <memory slug>" at 04:00 PT (`claude-fable-5-1`, $25 + $2 relay, 4 h) and replaces an outdated job for the same repository rather than adding a second.
- **Schedule subpanel and history**: `getCronJobs` returns the rows of `cron_jobs_report()` (enabled jobs first, soonest next run first, each with a `running` flag); `cronAction` **NEW** (10-11; `create` 10-12) applies `create`, `update`, `remove`, `pause`, `resume` or `run_now` through `apply_job_action()` and answers with the fresh list. The subpanel shows next-run times in Pacific time, per-job edit, run-now, enable/disable, copy and delete buttons and a `+` form in its header (section 16); with a machine picked in the chat header both commands go to that machine's daemon (section 15). Tasks the scheduler launched and their sub-tasks carry `is_cron` in `task_history` **NEW** (10-17; older rows are backfilled), and the history list hides them unless its Cron chip, off by default, is on.
- On the development machine 13 jobs are configured (10 prompt, 3 command; 12 enabled): daily README update, test fix, cost-calculation audit, model-catalogue update, knowledge refresh and browser bot-protection re-check, two weekly rsi7d jobs, the two default jobs, a 4-hourly sync of the task database and memories to a shared machine, a nightly work index, and a finished one-shot audit. The scheduler launched 1,506 of the 9,685 recorded top-level tasks (`is_cron = 1`; $4,398 of spend), 1,392 of them since 09-30. 1,149 top-level tasks mention cron, 1,020 of them launched by the scheduler, whose prompt preamble names it; 131 `cron_job` calls are recorded.

## 21. Installation, deployment, Docker and release

- **One-line install**: `curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ksenxx/kiss_ai/main/scripts/install.sh | bash` runs the bootstrap `scripts/install.sh` (306 lines). It takes the update lock, installs git when missing, clones `ksenxx/kiss_ai` into `~/.kiss/kiss_ai` (a ZIP of `main` when git is unusable) or fast-forwards the existing clone (when the pull fails, local edits are stashed and the clone is reset to its upstream), then runs that clone's `install.sh` from the directory the command was typed in. `./install.sh` in a checkout installs that checkout as it stands.
- **`install.sh`** (104,023 bytes, 2,281 lines) **CHANGED**: since 10-14 the single installer of the app itself (the `kiss-web` daemon, its web app and the `sorcar` CLI) for macOS and Linux on `x86_64`, `aarch64` and `arm64`. Its former steps (git, Node, VS Code CLI, extension build, extension install) went with the VS Code extension, removed 10-14. A re-run updates the app in place.
  - **Five steps**: `[1/5]` copy the tracked files of `src/kiss` (no tests; of the web app only `media/`) with `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`, `README.md` and `LICENSE` into `~/.kiss/kiss`, write the checkout's short commit into that copy's `core` package as `_commit.txt` (a file written at install time, not tracked in the repository; Settings shows it beside the version, 11-07) and apply the `.brand/` overlay (up to 7 media files); `[2/5]` build the Python environment with `uv sync` (uv is fetched from astral.sh when missing, with no version pin); `[3/5]` byte-compile the library and delete its sources, keeping 8 patterns that are run as scripts, read as text or shipped as source to another machine (the bundled SEAs, `*_sea.py`, `cron_agent.py`, `_version.py`, the database-sync and memory-sync modules); `[4/5]` install the `~/.local/bin/sorcar` wrapper, the `rsorcar` and `sorcar-docker` launchers, Playwright's Chromium and cloudflared; `[5/5]` refresh `~/.kiss/MODEL_INFO.json`, run the executables of `~/.kiss/post-install.d/` in name order with `KISS_HOME` and `KISS_APP_DIR` set (a failing hook is a warning) **NEW**, set or keep the remote-access password, install into other brand homes and start the daemon. The installed `sorcar` sets `KISS_WORKDIR` to the current directory and runs the `sorcar` console script (`kiss.agents.sorcar.sorcar_agent:main`) from the app directory, whereas the checkout's own 6-line `sorcar` script runs `kiss.agents.sorcar.worktree_sorcar_agent` through `uv run` (section 5); the two launchers are wrappers that run the checkout's `rsorcar` and `sorcar-docker`, so they stay bound to the checkout the installer was run from.
  - **Process safety**: started from inside the daemon's own systemd service (its Terminal tab, the Update button, a task's shell), the script first moves itself into a transient scope with `systemd-run --user --scope`, so stopping `kiss-web.service` cannot kill the installer **NEW**. A non-interactive run re-executes in a new session (`setsid`). An `flock` on `~/.kiss/.update.lock` makes a second installer exit at once; the kernel drops the lock when its holder dies, so a dead run never blocks a retry. One stray `SIGINT` or `SIGTERM` is ignored and a second within 3 s aborts. When the terminal closes (`SIGHUP`) the run carries on with its output in the log; the password is then not printed, and Settings shows it.
  - **Daemon service**: `kiss-web` is registered as the user's service (the LaunchAgent `com.kiss.web-server` on macOS, the systemd user unit `kiss-web.service` with lingering on Linux, a detached process on a Linux without a systemd user manager) and serves the directory the installer was run from (the home directory when that is inside `~/.kiss`). The restart is skipped when the running daemon already serves this exact runtime (a fingerprint of the launcher, the work dir and the newest modification time among the library's `.py` and `.pyc` files, the bytecode being written afresh by every full install), which keeps its tunnel URL, and it never happens while the daemon reports tasks in flight.
  - **Deferred restart** **NEW** (10-30): with tasks in flight the installer writes `{installer, home, workdir}` to the file the busy daemon names, `~/.kiss/.kiss-web.restart-pending`. The daemon looks for it every 5 s and, once no task is in flight and no installer it started is still running, raises the run-admission barrier and runs the recorded `install.sh --restart-only --non-interactive`, which installs nothing and only restarts the daemon onto the app already on disk (`deferred_restart.py`). The record is taken away as it is claimed, so a broken one is not retried. A task can therefore run `./install.sh`, and the daemon it runs under moves to the new build once the task is done. From a checkout, `scripts/reinstall-kiss-web-when-idle.sh` re-runs the whole installer as a detached one-shot job once the daemon has been idle for 3 probes in a row (4 h deadline) **NEW**.
  - **Remote-access password** **NEW** (10-15): the first install draws 20 random letters and digits and stores them as `remote_password` in `~/.kiss/config.json` (`python -m kiss.core.remote_password`); every later run keeps the stored one, so an update never invalidates the password other devices know. It is stored as typed, not hashed: the file's forced `0600` mode protects it, and the `remote_password_hash` key of the hashed scheme of 10-15, replaced the same day, is purged on every write. The installer prints the password in a box of `*` at the end of the install, on the terminal only, never in the log. `--rotate` draws a new one and `--show` prints the stored one; Settings shows it in an editable field with a button that fills in a new random one (section 16). On every check the daemon stats the file and reads it again only when its inode, modification time or size has changed, so a change is in force without a restart (section 14).
  - **Brand homes** **NEW**: the state directory is a brand property (`home_dir` in `brand.json`, 10-03; `KISS_HOME` overrides it), and the app directory is named after it (`~/.kiss/kiss` for the stock brand). On a machine that also holds a white-label brand's home (a `~/.<name>` with an executable `post-install.d` hook, or the home the registered service runs from) the installer repeats itself into that home, each home keeping its own app copy, model catalogue, hooks and password, and then starts the brand's daemon rather than the stock one. A hook that is an `exec <script>` stub inside a git checkout has that checkout rebased onto its upstream first; if the rebase cannot complete, the checkout is reset to the upstream and the local commits stay on a `brand-update-backup-<time>` branch. Executables in `~/.kiss/services.d/` run for as long as the daemon does (`services.py`, 10-29): each in its own process group, output in `~/.kiss/logs/services/<name>.log`, restarted after 1 s doubling to 60 s.
  - **Failures and notices** **NEW** (10-16): every warning and failure of a run goes to `~/.kiss/.install-problems`, which the web app shows as a sticky error notification for up to 7 days or until a clean run (`install_problems.py`). On a machine that still has the retired extension the installer uninstalls `ksenxx.kiss-sorcar` through the `code`, `code-insiders`, `cursor` and `code-server` CLIs (`KISS_CODE_CLI` names the one to use), leaves `VSCODE-EXTENSION-REMOVED.txt`, and for 7 days the web app shows a "VS Code extension removed" notification with a "Do not show this again" checkbox (`vscode_retired.py`).
  - **Opening the app**: the installer waits up to 120 s (`KISS_WEBAPP_WAIT_SECS`; 900 s before) for the daemon's `remote-url.json`, then opens `http://127.0.0.1:8787` with `kiss-app` and shows the launcher's folder in the file manager; without a usable browser it falls back to the default browser, and on a remote machine (an SSH session, or Linux without a display) it prints the tunnel URL instead.
  - **Controls**: `--non-interactive` (or `KISS_NONINTERACTIVE`) answers the one question a terminal run asks, `[Y/n]` before Homebrew is installed on macOS (optional, the preferred source of cloudflared; `KISS_NO_BREW` skips it); `--restart-only`; `KISS_SKIP_LAUNCH` (no daemon start, no window); `KISS_HOME`; `KISS_WEB_PORT` (8787); `KISS_CODE_CLI`; the timeouts `KISS_WEB_START_TIMEOUT` (90 s), `KISS_WEB_DRAIN_TIMEOUT` (30 s) and `KISS_HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL` (15 s). The log is `~/.kiss/install.log`.
- **`kiss-app`** **NEW** (10-16; `src/kiss/server/standalone_app.py`, 1,486 lines): opens the web app as a standalone window, Chromium's app mode (`--app=<URL>`) in the browser `$KISS_APP_BROWSER` names (once set it is the only candidate: when it does not resolve, no window opens and the command exits with status 1) or, without it, in the first of the system's Chrome, Chromium, Edge or Brave, and Playwright's Chromium, under its own profile `~/.kiss/webapp-profile`. `--install-launcher` registers a brand-named launcher whose icon the running window carries too: on Linux a `.desktop` entry with `StartupWMClass` set to the window's Wayland app id; on macOS a clone `.app` bundle in `~/Applications` holding APFS-cloned copies of the browser's binary and frameworks, the binary re-signed ad hoc under the brand's identifier, because Chromium's sandboxed helper processes read only the bundle they run from. While the daemon is not answering yet the window opens on a packaged waiting page (`appStart.html`) that polls it every half second, and on macOS a launch while the app is open brings its window to the front. The other flags are `--url`, `--wait` (120 s), `--foreground`, `--print-command`, `--uninstall-launcher` and `--reveal-launcher`.
- **Updates**: the daemon asks PyPI for a newer release at start and every hour, and the toast offers **Update**, **Update when idle** and **Remind me later** (a 24 h snooze that a still newer release breaks). Update runs the bootstrap of `~/.kiss/kiss_ai` (it syncs the clone, then installs) detached and non-interactive, output in `~/.kiss/update.log` (the curl one-liner when the clone is gone; `$KISS_UPDATE_BOOTSTRAP_URL` overrides its URL), one run at a time and with the run-admission barrier up, so no new task starts under the installer. Update when idle re-checks every 5 s and starts the update in the same atomic step that finds no task running. An open page reloads itself once it reconnects to a daemon whose per-process token differs (`GET /api/daemon`, 10-23) **NEW**.
- **`rsorcar user@host`** (60,557 bytes, 1,019 lines): deploys the checkout to a remote Linux machine and publishes its web app in nine numbered steps plus six lettered sub-steps (1a to 1c, 4b to 4d), using 12 helper scripts under `scripts/` and 11 Python modules that it checks are present before it starts. Nothing is installed on the local machine any more (the closing `install.sh` run there was removed 10-20), but the two-way syncs of steps 3 and 4b to 4d change both machines.
  - **Steps 1 and 1a to 1c**: ssh check; the missing ones of `git`, `curl`, `tar`, `python3` and the ssh client installed through the host's package manager; a `sorcar.db` from before 2026.10.2 renamed to `history.db`, the old name left as a symlink **NEW**; room under the remote home for the local task database plus `SORCAR_DISK_HEADROOM_GB` (3) GiB, with `scripts/move-home-to-disk.sh` to move the home onto a larger disk.
  - **Step 2** **CHANGED** (10-12): private ssh keys stay home by default and the ssh agent is forwarded (`ssh -A`) for the sync; `SORCAR_SKIP_SSH_COPY=0` copies `~/.ssh` (never `authorized_keys`) and moves the files it replaces to `~/.kiss/ssh-replaced-<time>/`.
  - **Step 3**: two-way repository sync through `origin`: uncommitted work is committed first, a diverged branch is merged, nothing is force-pushed or deleted, and the branch deployed is the main checkout's (`SORCAR_GIT_BRANCH` overrides it), never a `kiss/wt-*` branch (`main` instead).
  - **Steps 4 and 4b to 4d**: API keys go only to a remote that has none, with a probe that fails closed; two-way `history.db` merge; memory sync with tombstones and clock-skew tolerance; `AUTOROUTER.md` and `MODEL_DECISIONS.md` merged both ways. A failure of 4b, 4c or 4d is a warning, not a failed deploy.
  - **The `history.db` merge** **CHANGED** sends only the rows the other side lacks while both web apps keep running. Since 10-20 a task still in flight is held back until a later sync, and deleted tasks and chats travel as deletions (`deleted_tasks`, `deleted_chats`) instead of coming back from the other machine: the receiving database drops the deleted tasks' events and rows, except those of a task still running there, which wait for a later sync, and of a task row newer than its tombstone (section 15). Since 10-17 the two machines may be on different releases, where the merge used to refuse: a `task_history` or `events` column one database lacks is added with the other's declaration, and `is_cron` is worked out for rows written without it, by the same `history_columns.py` the web app migrates with. `chat_summaries` is recomputed on the receiving side, never copied.
  - **Steps 5 and 6**: the remote runs `install.sh` (`KISS_SKIP_LAUNCH=1`, `--non-interactive`), then `uv sync` in the checkout, and `kiss-web` becomes a lingering systemd user service run from the checkout's `.venv` with `--workdir` set to the checkout. A running task no longer stops the deploy **CHANGED** (10-20): while one runs on the remote (its newest event within `SORCAR_LIVE_TASK_WINDOW`, 300 s) only the restart is skipped, and `SORCAR_FORCE_RESTART=1` restarts anyway.
  - **Steps 7 to 9**: the web app password (this machine's by default, `SORCAR_PASSWORD` overrides it), a Cloudflare tunnel and a machine-unique ntfy topic; GitHub credentials sent over stdin (`gh auth login --with-token`), with the remote checkout's `git@github.com:` URLs rewritten to `https` when the keys stayed home; the public URL verified from this machine and opened in the browser.
  - Fourteen `SORCAR_*` environment overrides (thirteen on 09-30; `SORCAR_SKIP_SSH_COPY` is the new one). The `/install` command runs `./install.sh --non-interactive` over ssh, concurrently, on every machine registered with an ssh login, or on the machines it names, in that machine's clone of the private `kiss.git` (`~/kiss`, else another direct subdirectory of the home, matched by its `origin` URL); a machine registered by URL or without such a clone (a public `kiss_ai` clone does not count) is reported as failed **NEW** (11-04; section 15).
- **Docker** **CHANGED** (10-14): `sorcar-docker [PORT] [--rebuild]` builds a plain `debian:bookworm-slim` image (`Dockerfile`, uv **0.12.19** baked in) in place of the `codercom/code-server` image that served the extension on port 8080. The entrypoint `scripts/docker-startup.sh` clones the repository (the private `kiss.git` with `GH_TOKEN`, else the public `kiss_ai.git`), runs `install.sh`, sets the web app password (`KISS_WEB_PASSWORD`, else the stored one, else a generated one printed in `docker logs`) and runs `kiss-web` in the foreground on port 8787 (host port `PORT`, 8787 by default). State persists in the `kiss-sorcar-web-home` volume and 14 variables are forwarded (`GH_TOKEN`, `KISS_WEB_PASSWORD`, the Anthropic key and workspace id, the OpenAI, Gemini, Together and OpenRouter keys, five AWS variables, `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`). The launcher probes `https://localhost:PORT`, whose self-signed certificate is accepted once or installed from `/ca.crt`, in a loop of up to 900 rounds that sleeps 1 s after each failed probe, then once more after the loop whether it succeeded or ran out (901 requests at most); a probe has no time limit of its own, so 900 s is not a hard deadline. Inside tasks, `docker_image=` runs the shell and file tools in a container with the work dir mounted at its host path (section 7).
- **Release** (`scripts/release.sh`, 1,030 lines, 11 steps; 13 on 09-30) **CHANGED**: stash; purge the 18 paths of `scripts/exclude.json` from every branch and tag of the public history; bump the version (`YEAR.MONTH.N`) in `_version.py` and `README.md` (`SYSTEM.md` is no longer edited: since 10-18 the prompt renders the version through the `VERSION` placeholder); commit and push to `origin`; push one `Release X.Y.Z` commit (origin's tree minus the excluded paths, parented on the previous release commit) and its tag to `ksenxx/kiss_ai`; GitHub release; PyPI publish (`uv build`, a 100 MiB per-file check, `uv publish`); local `install.sh`; restore the stash. Since 10-17 the public `main` holds one release commit per release and no development commits (before, it received the full development history with the excluded paths filtered out, plus a release tip commit); a `main` of any other shape is replaced in one atomic push under a lease per ref, and every earlier release tag is re-pointed to its release commit. The VSIX build, the `package.json` bump and the Marketplace publish were removed 10-14.
- **Package** (`pyproject.toml`): `kiss-agent-framework` on PyPI, `requires-python >=3.13`, version read from `src/kiss/core/_version.py`, 29 dependencies with one exact pin (`vosk==0.3.44`) and `cryptography` held below 49 on Intel macOS, 14 `constraint-dependencies` floors, 53 console scripts (among them `sorcar`, `kiss-web`, `kiss-app` and `check`); the wheel's only packages are `src/kiss` and `projects/swedefend`, and the sdist ships those two trees plus `pyproject.toml`, `README.md`, `LICENSE`, `.gitignore` and a generated `PKG-INFO`.
- **iPhone Home Screen icon** **NEW** (11-03): `python -m kiss.scripts.webclip` (361 lines, 8 options) writes an iOS Web Clip profile (`.mobileconfig`, payload `com.apple.webClip.managed` with `FullScreen`) that opens the web app without browser controls.
