The profile is signed with a certificate issued by the daemon's local CA, so a phone that installed `/ca.crt` shows it as Verified. The daemon serves the same profile for its tunnel URL, else its LAN URL, at `GET /webclip.mobileconfig` (the "..." menu's "iPhone Home Screen" item).
- **Windows**: there has been no Windows installer since the extension, which installed MinGit and uv there, was removed 10-14; `install.sh` stops with `Unsupported OS` on anything but macOS and Linux. Windows code paths remain (`kiss-app` finds Chrome, Edge and Brave there but registers no launcher; the installer's active-tasks probe uses a 5 s timeout there), and fixes from the cross-platform test runs landed through 11-09 (section 22).

## 22. Developer tooling and tests

- **`uv run check`** (`src/kiss/scripts/check.py`; `--read-only` **NEW** (11-11) leaves out the cleaning, `uv sync` and the two generated docs and runs every stage with `uv run --no-sync`, so that it can run beside the tests) runs `uv sync`, `generate-api-docs`, `sea lint` and `sea docs` **NEW** (10-05), `python -m compileall -q src/`, `ruff check src/` and `mypy src/`. `--full` adds `pyright src/` and, when `node_modules` exists under `src/kiss/webapp` and `npm` is on `PATH`, the web app's `npm run lint` (eslint over `media/**/*.js`, stylelint over the CSS, htmlhint over the HTML) **CHANGED** (10-14: the VS Code extension's `npm run typecheck`, its lint and its version sync were removed with the extension). Every stage runs even after an earlier one failed, except that a failed `uv sync` ends the run, and the error lines of each failed stage are repeated at the end of the output, at most 60 per stage.
  - `--fix` **NEW** (10-25) first runs `ruff check --fix src/` (lint fixes only, no formatter), prints one status line per stage and only the residual errors, and stores the verdict in `check-cache.json` under `tmp/`, keyed by `--full` and by a SHA-256 fingerprint of the path, size and modification time of every source file under `src/` and of `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock` and `pyrightconfig.json`. A repeat run on an unchanged tree still does the clean-up (unless `--no-clean`) and the auto-fix, then prints the stored verdict and runs none of the stages.
  - Inside a task's worktree **NEW** (11-02) the stages use the main checkout's `.venv` with the worktree's `src/` and `projects/` first on `PYTHONPATH`: dependencies are synced with `uv sync --inexact --no-install-project`, the other stages run under `uv run --no-sync`, and the project's console scripts run as `python -m <module>`, so the shared editable install is never re-pointed at a worktree.
  - The run starts by deleting `dist`, `build`, `*.egg-info`, `.claude`, `artifacts` and `uv_cache` at the repository root; `--no-clean` skips that step and `--clean-only` stops after it.
  - Rules: ruff selects `E`, `F`, `W`, `I`, `N` and `UP` at 100 columns for Python 3.13; mypy sets `disallow_untyped_defs` everywhere but `kiss.tests` and `kiss.viz_trajectory`; pyright reads `pyrightconfig.json`.
- **`uv run findings-report`** **NEW** (11-11; `src/kiss/scripts/findings_report.py`) builds the findings table of a final report from the files fixers and reviewers append to, one JSON object per finding on one line (`file`, `line`, `kind`, `status`, `summary`, `test`): it prints the counts by status and kind and one row per finding, open ones first, as an HTML fragment (`-o` writes it to a file), and exits 1 naming every malformed line without writing a report.
- **`uv run impacted-tests`** **NEW** (10-25; `src/kiss/scripts/impacted_tests.py`) prints the test files within two import hops (`--depth N`; `0` removes the bound) of the changed Python files under `src/`. It parses the `kiss` imports of every source file with `ast`, walks the reverse import graph from the changed modules (`git diff --name-only HEAD` plus untracked files, or the paths given) and keeps the `test_*.py` files it reaches; a changed test file selects itself, and the importers of a deleted module are still found.
  - `--splits N` prints instead a JSON list of at most N commands of the form `uv run pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider --timeout=600 <files>` for `run_commands_parallel`; `--timeout S` changes the per-test limit and `0` omits it.
  - `--owned <paths>` (11-09) selects only the given test files and the tests named for the given modules (`test_<module>.py`, `test_<module>_*.py`), with no import hops, for a sub-agent whose tree also holds its siblings' edits; a module with no such test is named on stderr.
  - Splits are balanced by measured durations (11-09). The suite's `conftest.py` sums the setup, call and teardown seconds of each test file and merges them at session end, under a file lock, into `tmp/test-durations.json` of the main checkout, which its worktrees share (`$KISS_TEST_DURATIONS` names another file). Files go longest first into the lightest split, and a file with no record weighs its size times the median seconds per byte of the recorded files.
- **Python tests**: 2,073 `test_*.py` files with 14,406 test functions (558,090 lines across the 2,140 Python files under `src/kiss/tests/`): 742 files under `server/`, 711 under `agents/`, 377 under `core/`, 142 under `webapp/`, 74 under `scripts/` and 27 elsewhere; 67 of them import Playwright. `uv run pytest` collects `test_*.py` only, deselects the `slow` and `process_killer` markers (`addopts = "-m 'not slow and not process_killer'"`), applies no per-test timeout (`timeout = 0`; the `impacted-tests` splits pass their own) and measures coverage only when `--cov=src/kiss --cov-branch` is given. `live_api` marks tests that call a provider's API and are skipped without its key, `live_cli` tests that spawn the real Claude Code or Codex CLI. `pytest>=9.0.3,<9.1` stays pinned because 9.1.0 and 9.1.1 lose a package's `conftest.py` fixtures in split runs.
- **JavaScript tests**: 407 `*.test.js` files under `src/kiss/webapp/test/`, plain Node scripts that use `assert`, with 21 helper modules. `npm test` (`node test/run-all.js`) **CHANGED** (10-18) runs every suite in its own `node` process, as many at a time as the machine has CPUs up to 32 (`--serial` runs one at a time), and kills a suite's process group after 2 minutes; before, the suites ran one after another. 23 `*.coverage.js` gates run jsdom suites under `NODE_V8_COVERAGE`: 22 run one suite each and fail unless every code line fenced by `// <mark>-coverage:start` and `// <mark>-coverage:end` comments in the `media/*.js` files was executed, and `remotePasswordBypass.coverage.js` runs five suites and requires every code line of the whole of `transport.js` and `assetLoadGuard.js` and every branch except the empty `catch` after a `location.reload()`; a suite that a gate names runs only through that gate. `npm run check` is the lint plus the tests. `scripts/` also holds 11 shell tests (`test_*.sh`) of the deployment and release scripts (section 21).
- **Suite isolation**: the root `conftest.py` registers the reaper plugin, sets `KISS_DISABLE_TASK_CLASSIFIER=1` and raises the open-file soft limit to 4,096 on POSIX. `src/kiss/tests/conftest.py` (1,401 lines) gives the session its own `KISS_HOME` (a `kiss_test_*` directory removed at exit), sets `BROWSER=true` and `KISS_MUSE_AUTH=0`, turns the tips and the update toast off, and:
  - routes every temporary file of the session and of its child processes into one `kiss-pt-*` root, deleted at exit or by the next session when its owner died **NEW** (10-30, after leaked test directories had used 960,000 of the 1,048,576 inodes of `/tmp` and 2,500 tests failed at setup; POSIX only);
  - sets `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES` to the temp roots and drops the repository-scoping `GIT_*` variables, so no `git` call of a test can reach the developer's checkout **NEW** (10-30, after tests had made seven commits on work in progress);
  - applies autouse fixtures that give each test its own `KISS_WORKDIR`, undo changes to `config.json`, empty `tabs.json`, hide ambient AWS credentials **NEW** (10-18) and point `CODEX_HOME` at a missing directory unless the test is marked `live_cli` **NEW** (10-18);
  - offers ephemeral OAuth redirect ports **NEW** (10-12) and helper modules **NEW** that start a second `kiss-web` daemon in its own process (`remote_daemon.py`, 10-10), a private unprivileged `sshd` on a loopback port (`ssh_server.py`, 10-11), a client of the daemon's local WSS endpoint (`local_ws.py`, 10-01) and a re-branded copy of the package (`branded_package.py`, 10-16); a fifth, `lock_injection.py` (10-13), offers `hammer`, which loops a real code path on a worker thread, injects up to 300 `KeyboardInterrupt`s the way a forced Stop does, optionally while another thread contends for a lock, and asserts that the worker ended and that `STATE_LOCK` and the named locks are free.
- **Subprocess reaper** (`kiss.tests.subprocess_reaper`): wraps `Popen.__init__`, so every child, including those of `subprocess.run` and asyncio, is attributed to the running test, to the class-, module-, package- or session-scoped fixture being set up, or to the pytest process, and is swept when that scope ends: SIGTERM, up to 5 s of grace, then SIGKILL. At a test's teardown only the processes that nothing references any more are swept; one that a shared object still holds waits for the end of the run. A child that leads a process group is signalled as a group; on Windows its Job Object or process tree is terminated. A test or fixture sweep that had to kill something emits a `LeakedSubprocessWarning` naming the test or fixture and the commands; the sweep at the end of the session prints its report on the terminal instead, and a last-resort `atexit` sweep on `stderr`. Processes created without `Popen` (`os.fork`, `os.posix_spawn`, `multiprocessing`) are not tracked. Written after a run left 529 detached `muse_auth.daemon` processes.
- **Testing policy** (`src/kiss/SYSTEM.md`) **CHANGED**. The standing rules are the same: end-to-end tests only, with no unit tests, mocks, patches, fakes or test doubles and no structural tests that assert on source code; 100% branch coverage of new and modified code wherever a branch is reachable without a test double, an unreachable branch being documented in the test file; an issue is reproduced by a test before it is fixed; lint and type checks run once per task, at the end, and only when the task created or modified code files; only the impacted tests run after a change, and the full suite only on request or when changes cross module boundaries; a verification that passed is not repeated unless a later change could invalidate it; a load-dependent flake is named in the summary and not chased with a third full run. The agent's own verifier script and the finish gate that runs it are in section 6. Changed since 09-30:
  - the end-of-task check is `uv run check --full --fix` (10-25);
  - the impacted tests come from `uv run impacted-tests`, never from a directory glob (10-25); a sub-agent in a shared tree runs `--owned` on its own files and the parent runs the impacted closure once per round (11-09);
  - test volume **NEW** (11-02): a regression test goes into the existing test module of the code it exercises, one scenario per behaviour, and the test lines a task adds stay near the number of product lines it changes, with duplicated scenarios merged once they pass twice that;
  - the full suite is budgeted (11-02): one run after the last product change, as a background job, plus at most one re-run of each failing split;
  - a suspected flaky test is repeated at most three times, in one `run_commands_parallel` call (10-25);
  - the split rule (10-12): tests run in parallel through `run_commands_parallel` with as many splits as the cores and their load allow; it was an equal split by test methods over `cores - 2` workers.
- **SEA tooling** **NEW** (for Sorcar Extension Agents; section 9 has the contract, the rule codes and the tables): `uv run sea lint [paths] [--fix] [--registered]` (10-05) checks every bundled SEA script and built-in command without calling a model, prints one line per finding and exits 1 when there is any; `--fix` rewrites the four mechanical kinds of finding. `uv run sea docs [--check]` (10-05) regenerates the six vocabulary tables in `src/kiss/server/README.md` and two website pages from the code; `--check` only reports stale tables and exits 1. Both are stages of `check`. `uv run python scripts/run_sea.py <sea> "<task>"` (10-09) runs one SEA in the calling process, with no daemon and no UI, and exits 0 on success and 1 otherwise; `--json` prints the whole outcome with the trajectory file paths.
- **Generated reference and viewers**: `generate-api-docs`, a stage of `check`, rewrites `API.md` (669 lines) from the syntax trees of six modules, listing their public classes and functions (`core/kiss_agent.py`, `agents/sorcar/relentless_agent.py`, `sorcar_agent.py`, `chat_sorcar_agent.py`, `daemon_client.py`, `server/sorcar.py`). The trajectory visualiser also runs standalone, `python -m kiss.viz_trajectory.server <artifact_dir> [--host 127.0.0.1] [--port 5050]`: a Flask page over the `trajectory_*.yaml` files of the `job_*` directories that merges the jobs of every `.kiss.artifacts/jobs` under the project root, worktrees included, and opens the browser (the daemon serves the same page at `/trajectories`, section 14). `scripts/ui_theme_screenshots.py` captures six scenes of the web app's chat page at desktop and phone width and the shared-chat page, each in the dark and light themes, and one screenshot of the trajectory visualiser; `scripts/ui_style_probe.py` writes one line of computed style per distinct control of the same chat scenes and of the shared-chat page, each in its default theme only, so a control can be compared across surfaces and across a CSS change.
- **Benchmark harnesses**:
  - **Terminal-Bench under Harbor** **NEW** (10-25; `src/kiss/benchmarks/terminal_bench/`, 7 Python files, 1,182 lines; `harbor>=0.24.0`). `python -m kiss.benchmarks.terminal_bench.daemon` starts a private `kiss-web` daemon with its own home (`~/.kiss-tb`, port 8797) in which the web tools, memory, task classification and the cron scheduler are switched off (the `is_worktree` and `auto_commit_mode` keys of its config template are read by nothing, section 24) and a trial cannot switch models (section 10). `python -m kiss.benchmarks.terminal_bench.run --model M --job-name N` builds the `harbor run` command for the dataset `terminal-bench/terminal-bench`, named without a version so that Harbor takes its latest release (`--dataset org/name@version` pins one): 5 attempts per task, 40 concurrent trials and a 100 USD cap per trial by default; `-x` and `-i` exclude and include tasks, `--env modal` runs the trials on Modal instead of local Docker, `--effort`, `--reviewer`, `--reading-check` and `--blind-verifier` set the reasoning effort, the reviewer of the finish gate, the reader of the agent's interpretation ledger and the writer of the gate's independent check (section 6), and `--agent oracle` runs the reference solutions instead, the sandbox check Terminal-Bench asks for before an agent run. The Harbor agent `SeaHarborAgent` writes a coding SEA per trial and drives it on the trial's container through the daemon, lists the task's Agent Skills and MCP servers in the prompt, reaches a sandbox that is not a local Docker container through a loopback exec bridge (10-26), and ends a trial whose sandbox is gone with `EnvironmentLostError` (10-30). `python -m kiss.benchmarks.terminal_bench.summarize <job_dir> --attempts K` writes `summary.json` and `summary.md` with the mean reward, the passed attempts divided by the number of tasks times `K`. `K` must be the attempts per task of the launch: `--attempts` defaults to 1, not to the launcher's 5, and an attempt that never ran counts as failed. With `--dataset-dir`, dataset tasks that have no trial count as failed; a further job directory replaces the trials of the tasks it re-ran. Section 3 reports the runs, which the ICML paper and the reports call Terminal-Bench 4.0.
  - `scripts/transport_bench/` **NEW** (09-30) drives one real daemon with a stand-in model over the local WebSocket (WSS) transport and records latency percentiles and the daemon's CPU time and memory for five workloads (`connect`, `ping`, `models`, `burst`, `task`); `report.py` renders the results as HTML.
  - `scripts/web_stealth_bench.py` **NEW** (10-26) loads one page per bot-protected host through the real browser tool, prints `OK`, `FALLBACK`, `BLOCKED` or `ERROR` per URL and exits 1 when a page was blocked or failed (section 17).
  - `kiss.scripts.memory_search_eval` scores memory search against a question set (section 13); `kiss.scripts.cost_levers_experiment` runs A/B cost experiments with real model calls; `kiss.scripts.redundancy_analyzer` lists the test methods whose coverage (coverage.py branch arcs per test, LCOV records) other tests already produce.
  - The `benchmarkings` tree of the previous inventory (the HarnessTax, memoryfield and task-classifier drivers and their job data) has since been moved out of the repository.
- **Maintenance scripts** (`src/kiss/scripts/`, 24 Python files, 12,178 lines; run as `uv run python -m kiss.scripts.<name>`): `update_models` and `update_responses_api_support` maintain the model catalogue and `cost_report` prints the token-cost KPIs of a `history.db` window (section 8); `backfill_subtask_costs` re-adds sub-task spend that was never folded into its parent (it only reports unless `--apply` is given) and `backfill_task_metadata` fills missing tags, SEA names and chat summaries (section 11); `sync_db`, `carry_over_tables`, `db_fingerprint`, `relocate_work_dir`, `running_tasks`, `remote_config` and `legacy_task_db` serve the installer and `rsorcar` (section 21); `sync_kiss_notes` and `merge_memory_pages` serve the memory sync (section 13); `webclip` builds the iOS Web Clip profile (section 21) and `make_victory_sound` renders `media/victory.mp3` (section 18).
- **Continuous integration**: one GitHub workflow remains, `.github/workflows/check.yml` ("Code Quality", on pushes and pull requests to `main`, Python 3.13 with uv). Its only command is `uv run check --clean`, which `argparse` resolves to `--clean-only`, so the job deletes build artefacts and runs no stage (section 24). The three Windows workflows were removed 10-14 with the VS Code extension; no workflow runs a test.
- **Repository hygiene**: a worktree holds tracked files only, so the main checkout's ignored `node_modules` directories are symlinked into each new one (section 12). Inside a worktree the Bash tool rewrites an unambiguous `uv run <program>` to the main checkout's `.venv/bin/<program>` with the worktree's sources on `PYTHONPATH`, refuses `uv sync`, `uv add`, `uv remove` and any `uv run` it cannot rewrite, naming the command to run instead **NEW** (11-02; the rewrite 11-03), and refuses a command that names the main checkout's path outside its `.venv` (section 7). Scratch files go to the task's own `./tmp/<task-id>/` directory **CHANGED** (10-25; it was the shared `./tmp/`), the progress log to `./tmp/PROGRESS.md`, and reports longer than about 800 words are written as HTML under `./reports`. `AGENTS.md` at the repository root (32 lines; `SORCAR.md` until 10-03) and `CLAUDE.md` (30 lines) **NEW** (10-15) carry notes for coding agents: one home-automation pointer in `AGENTS.md` and the same block of AWS rules in both. The build and test rules for agents are in the "Sorcar repo specific" block of `src/kiss/SYSTEM.md`.

## 23. What the trajectories show

Figures from the development machine's task database (`history.db`), read on 2026-11-10 and covering the rows and events dated before 2026-11-10 00:00 UTC (first row 2026-04-22; two more rows carry the timestamp `1234567890`, 2009-02-13, and a NULL id). The file holds more than one machine's history: the history sync (sections 15 and 21) merges task databases, and since 11-08 a copied row names the machine it ran on in `machine`.

| Measure | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Task rows | 41,127 (9,685 top-level, 31,442 sub-agents, 4,477 side channels) |
| Rows naming another machine | 13,165 from 8 hosts (9,377, 3,046 and 726 from the three largest), 3,019 of them top-level with $25,070 of spend; of the 27,962 unstamped rows, 692 have macOS working directories although the development machine runs Linux |
| Chats | 8,614 chat ids (4,927 with a top-level task, 3,687 used by sub-agents only; the two rows dated 2009 have none); 4,927 stored summaries, 4,764 of them non-empty |
| Persisted events | 23,152,517 (16,389,163 `thinking_delta`; 385,054 `llm_call` since 09-27) |
| Tool calls | 968,429 under 177 names |
| Spend on top-level tasks | $77,565 · 69.8 billion tokens · 674,671 steps |
| Sum over every row (double counts folded sub-agent spend) | $137,184 · 119.1 billion tokens · 1,081,998 steps |
| Per-call check | the `llm_call` events sum to $38,819; the top-level tasks launched since the first of them (09-27) record $38,769 |
| Tasks over one hour / over 1,000 steps / over $100 | 564 / 89 / 117 top-level (1,027 / 99 / 146 across all rows); duration runs from launch to finish, with `timestamp` standing in where a legacy row has `start_ts` 0 (the 09-30 figure of 2,300 subtracted that 0) |
| Task with the most steps | 22,104 steps, $1,479, 12.4 hours ("Can you precisely and thoroughly find and fix all race conditions, hangs, deadlocks, dead code, and redundancies …"); the costliest took $1,853 over 14,514 steps, and 5 tasks cost more than $1,000 |
| Distinct top-level working directories / named models | 1,332, of which 1,205 are per-run cron directories (2,534 across all rows) / 87 (64 rows record neither a directory nor a model) |
| Cron-launched tasks | 1,506 top-level tasks with `is_cron = 1` ($4,398) and 1,640 sub-task rows |
| Finish verdicts | 1,742 top-level tasks with `success = 1`, 399 with `success = 0` and 150 with none since the first recorded verdict (10-10) |
| Worktree rows / auto-commit top-level tasks / parallel top-level tasks | 3,532 / 4,585 / 7,644; the first two columns are legacy, last written on 10-09 and 10-10 (section 12) |
| Worktree tool calls | 580 `create_worktree` in 525 tasks, 675 `commit_and_merge` in 502 |
| Voice tasks / slash-command tasks | 119 top-level tasks whose text starts with `Speaker #` (section 18; dictation inserted as bare words carries no prefix and is not counted) / 591 (`/sync` 254, `/good_morning` 96, `/sync_history_and_memory` 53, `/review_paper` 33, `/revise_and_review_paper` 28, `/sh` 27, `/write_paper` 26, `/install` 20, `/machine` 16, `/remember` 9) |

By single tag: `coding` 13,952, `testing` 12,428, `chore` 11,851, `failed` 8,884, `question` 5,969, `review` 5,073, `subagent` 4,983 of the 31,442 sub-agent rows, `debugging` 4,349 and `secret` 1,012.

## 24. Caveats, corrections and stale documentation

### Gated, off by default or platform-bound

- **CI checks nothing.** The only workflow, `.github/workflows/check.yml`, runs `uv run check --clean`; `argparse` reads the flag as `--clean-only`, so the job only deletes build artefacts. No workflow runs `pytest` or the JavaScript tests (section 22).
- **Review by another model family.** Unless `coding_gate_reviewer` names a model, which is used whatever its family, the finish review of section 6 is skipped when every configured model belongs to the run's own family; a sub-agent gets no reviewer either way. Section 10's same-family rule refuses a plain `run_agent` on the caller's own family unless the call is a review or the caller's own task asks for sub-agents.
- **Off by default.** Starting the Chrome bridge at daemon boot (`chrome_bridge`; while it is off, the first `use_my_chrome` call starts the bridge, and unticking it takes effect at the next daemon start), `KISS_REASONING_KEEP_TURNS` (0), `KISS_ROUTINE_TURN_EFFORT` (empty) and the Terminal-Bench launcher's `--blind-verifier` (`none`) ship disabled.
- **Settings without a control.** `classify_tasks`, `classify_with_decisions` and `memory_dir` have no control in the app and are set in `~/.kiss/config.json`. The web client always sends `isParallel: true` and never sends `useMemory`: only the Python API starts a sequential run, and memory is switched per run through it, a SEA setting or `run_agent` options.
- **Needs something first.** Remote access needs a stored remote password (section 14). Browser sign-in to GitHub, Microsoft Teams, Slack and Discord needs `KISS_<PROVIDER>_CLIENT_ID`, the four embedded OAuth client IDs being empty, and Microsoft Teams needs Muse auth, on by default on Linux only (section 19). A Google sign-in needs the Browser tab or the user's Chrome: the agent's own Chromium aborts requests to `accounts.google.com` (section 17).
- **SSH machines only.** `Bash(machine=)`, `/sync_history_and_memory` and `/install` work only for machines registered by SSH login, and `/install` needs a clone of the private development repository there (section 15).
- **Terminal tab.** It has no Windows implementation and is not mirrored to other clients.

### Behaviour a name or a message hides

- **SEAs that name `bestrouter`.** `/discover`, `/write`, `/review_paper`, `/review_patent` and `/revise_and_review_paper` set `model` to `bestrouter`, which resolves to the router's simple model, `claude-opus-5-5-medium`; the routing protocol is layered only when `bestrouter` is the tab's pick (section 9). `discover_sea.py:75-77` still names the primary model.
- **Update Models.** The completion notice says new tasks pick up the refreshed catalogue; `MODEL_INFO` is loaded once at import, so the running daemon keeps the old one until it restarts (section 8).
- **Worktree guard.** It covers `Write`, `Edit` and `rollback`, including a shell rewrite that `Bash` applies as `Write` or `Edit` (section 7); any other `Bash` write run before `create_worktree`, such as a here-document that creates a file, goes straight into the checkout. After a daemon restart an unmerged worktree is not offered for merging again, although its branch and directory remain (section 12).
- **Cron.** A repeating job's missed occurrences are skipped, a repeated identical failure is not delivered again, and scheduled runs stay out of the history list until its Cron chip is on (section 20).
- **Literal `~/.kiss` paths.** The update lock, the install log and the update clone ignore `$KISS_HOME` and a brand's `home_dir`; `scripts/sync-memory.sh` (the memory step of `rsorcar`) and the Terminal-Bench daemon know no brand home either.

### Limits of the figures

- **The task database is a merge.** The file behind section 23 holds several machines' history: 13,165 of its 41,127 rows name one of 8 other machines (3,019 top-level tasks with $25,070 of the $77,565), and 692 unstamped rows have macOS working directories although the development machine runs Linux. A later sync can still add rows dated before the cutoff.
- **Spend.** The top-level sum, $77,565, is the spend; the sum over every row ($137,184) counts folded sub-agent spend twice. Since 09-27 the `llm_call` events give an independent total: $38,819, against $38,769 on the top-level tasks launched since then.
- **Unbilled turns.** Subscription billing, the default for `cc/*` and `codex/*` models, records a turn at $0 (89 of the 130 rows on those models carry no cost), and nine other catalogue entries have a $0 input price. Recorded spend understates what such runs consumed.
- **Cron runs dominate several counts.** The scheduler launched 1,392 of the 3,243 top-level tasks since 09-30, each in its own run directory; they account for 1,205 of the 1,332 working directories and for most keyword hits on report, cron, slack and gmail.
- **Tags and legacy columns.** Rows were re-tagged under the three-tag cap of 10-12, so tag counts are not comparable with 09-30: `subagent` survives on 4,983 of 31,442 sub-agent rows. `is_worktree` and `auto_commit_mode` were last written on 10-09 and 10-10 and exist only in databases created before then.
- **Section 1 counts.** The capability counts repeat the catalogue's flags, which mark `gemini-embedding-2` for generation and function calling instead of embedding and give 7 OpenRouter entries no capability at all. The 14,406 test functions are `test_*` functions at module level and `test_*` methods of module-level classes in `test_*.py` files.
- **Dates and hashes.** Section 2's range and peak are in UTC (90 commits on 10-08; 88 by author-local date), while month-day dates are author-local, so the removal of `benchmarkings/` dated 09-29 (`b9ed868023`) is inside the window. Hashes are development-repository commits: since 10-17 the public repository holds one commit per release, and none of the 31 release tags since 09-30 is an ancestor of the development HEAD.

### Documents and docstrings behind the code

- **Website.** Under `website/kisssorcar.github.io/`, `docs/cli.md`, `docs/overview.md`, `docs/tips.md`, `docs/api.md` and `docs/sea-commands.md` still describe pre-warmed worktrees, a `use_worktree` option, a classifier that decides whether a task needs a worktree and a `classify_tasks` toggle in Settings; the agent now calls `create_worktree` and `commit_and_merge` itself (sections 11 and 12). `index.html` says 695 models and `docs/models.md` 761, against 1,077 entries. `docs/cli.md` still turns memory off for `cc/*` and `codex/*` models, a gate removed 10-18.
- **`README.md`.** Line 173 calls the unmerged-worktree bar "Merge / Discard" (it has three buttons: "Auto-commit and merge", "Do nothing", "Discard"); line 178 says a remote task row has no delete button (since 11-08 delete, favourite and title edits go to the row's machine); line 316 says `--splits` balances by file size (by recorded duration, with file size as the fallback).
- **`API.md` and `src/kiss/server/README.md`.** `API.md:644` still describes the "Use Jev" and "Classify tasks before running" checkboxes, and `src/kiss/server/README.md:1820-1823` the second; both left Settings on 10-15. The `tool_profile` list at `API.md:191` omits `none`.
- **`src/kiss/SYSTEM.md`.** Line 204 says `Write` and `Edit` of a checkout path are refused once a worktree exists (the work dir's own paths are remapped); line 33 caps `run_parallel` nesting at two, which no code enforces.
- **`summary` cadence.** The prompts at `bestrouter_sea.py:75`, `merge_sea.py:78`, `rsi7d_sea.py:331` and `git_extract_knowledge_sea.py:222` say the 10th, 20th, ... call "is refused"; the root SEA layer lets the call run and appends a reminder (section 9).
- **Tool docstrings the model reads.** `talk` says each client speaks the text in the language's voice (clients play only the synthesised MP3), and `run_agent` says a cron sub-task runs in `channel_work` (`/cron` runs in the home's `cron/work`).
- **Transports.** `_kiss_web_launcher.py:293` still submits "over its Unix-domain socket", removed 10-01. `talk_player.py:11` and `web_server.py:3551` list a `sorcar` CLI among the daemon's local clients, and `_channel_cli.py:9` says the `sorcar` command no longer exists; the console script exists and runs its agent in its own process.
- **History sync.** `peer_sync.py:46-47` and `rsorcar:106` say no row is ever deleted; `_apply_deletions` in `sync_db.py` deletes the events and the row of a task recorded as deleted on either side, except a task still running there (it waits for a later sync) and a row younger than its deletion (section 21).
- **Strings in the product.** The Recommendations empty state promises "five tasks from your last 24 hours" (the SEA stores 10 and reaches back until 20 completed tasks show). The Save chat tooltip names `reports/chat-<id>.html` (a titled chat is saved as `chat-<title-slug>-<chat-id>.html`).
- **Install and recipes.** `pyproject.toml:383-384` calls uv 0.12.19 "the version the installers pin" (only the `Dockerfile` pins it), and `RECIPES.md:14` points at `templates/write_paper_prompt.md`, which is not in the tree.

### Corrections to the 2026-09-30 inventory

- **Tasks over one hour.** The 2,300 of 09-30 subtracted a `start_ts` of 0 on 1,984 legacy rows; measured from the launch instant the count was 352 then and is 564 now.
- **Rows dated 2009** are not a clock artefact: both carry the timestamp `1234567890` and a NULL id.
- **Working directories.** The 09-30 counts of 125 and 430 included the empty `work_dir`; without it they were 124 and 429.
- **`summary` and Docker.** The `summary` cadence is no longer left to the prompt alone, and `bash_job` works in a container through `ContainerJobs`; a Docker run still lacks the checkpoint, worktree and memory tools (section 7).
- **Retired caveats.** The `knowledge/` residue was deleted on 09-30 (`f4c197420e`), `website/kisssorcar.github.io/docs/sea-commands.md` now carries a generated table of all 72 commands, and `benchmarkings/` left the tree on 09-29.

### Leftovers

- **Dead launcher.** The root `sorcar` script runs `kiss.agents.sorcar.worktree_sorcar_agent`, a module deleted on 10-10, and fails; the `sorcar` console script works (section 5).
- **Unused files.** `quoted_evidence.py` (194 lines) in `src/kiss/agents/seas/coding/` is imported by nothing, `ROUTING.md` at the root is an empty tracked file, and `.pylintrc` is referenced by no tool.
- **Unused settings.** `auto_commit_mode` and `is_worktree` survive only as two unread keys of the Terminal-Bench daemon's config template; the `redundancy_check` pytest marker is used by no test.
- **Names that outlived a removal.** `evidence_gate.py` no longer gates on evidence: it only reads which files a shell command changes. `git_worktree.py` keeps `load_spare_marker` and `spare_has_content` only to discard spares of the worktree pool removed 10-09.
- **Stale model names.** `DEFAULT_MODEL_NAME` in `relentless_agent.py` is still `claude-opus-4-6` (it reaches only a bare `RelentlessAgent`; `API.md:115` repeats it), and `DAILY_UPDATE_MODEL` of `/git_extract_knowledge` still pins `claude-fable-5-1`.
